Every other fixture type the compiler can emit (tall storage, chair, bed, sofa, TV, toilet, bathtub and the rest) is one solid box from `furniture_xml`, so the arm, the base and the walkers cannot pass through it; before, they were not built at all. A sink with no counter or cabinet under it gets `sink_unit_xml`, a carcass with the same basin and faucet lever a counter gets. And `drawer_x` works in the counter's frame, so the west run of the rotated kitchen has its drawer in the left quarter like a north-wall run instead of the middle. Drawer and door tasks are also no longer offered on a cabinet whose stand spot lies within 0.15 m of another fixture (the pre-flight in the next paragraph, using the `front_stand` spot `SimEnv` parks at): the north cabinet in the corner of the U had its stand spot inside the east run, 0.0 m clear, and its three tasks missed. Results with `--families all --seed 5`: the U kitchen goes from 0 tasks to 16/16 over seven families with 0 and with 2 people, 0 human contacts, and the dataset validates (16 episodes, 2662 frames); the mug in `counter_to_sink` rests on the basin bottom at 0.75 to 0.76 m. The hero kitchen's MJCF, twin and proposed tasks are byte-identical and its 14-episode runs give the same 14 lines with 0 and 2 people. The rotated kitchen stays 13/14 (the same bowl miss). Its west-run door and drawer episodes change because the drawer moved: two `close_door` episodes now end with a 1.9 cm IK miss instead of 0.02 cm (both still pass), and with two people the closest walker pass in two west-run door episodes drops from about 25 cm to 6 to 7 cm, with no contact. #49's wall cabinets over a storage run are kept: in the U kitchen none of them overlaps a wall or another fixture, and every task passes under them. Still open: tall storage is a box with no door, `counter_to_counter` finds no pair in the U kitchen for a fixed base, and the corner cabinet gets no drawer or door task until MAN-3's base can stand somewhere else.

**Fixtures on any wall, rebased, with a pre-flight and skip-not-crash (L1, L2).** #49, #75 and #77 are merged into one branch on top of the new walkers (PPL-1 gait and PPL-2 fridge visit), which conflicted in `people.py`, `collect.py`, `policy.py`, `scene.py` and `tasks.py`. Both behaviours are kept: `Walkers` now plans everything (floor strip, lane, counter stop, the fridge trip's stand points, the routing round fixtures and the door sector) in the frame where the work fixture faces +y, with the fixtures moved into that frame, and rotates paths, hinge, centre and stand points back to the world before the first step, so the gait, the door drive and the safety monitor are unchanged world-frame code. Rotating a whole room by 90, -90 or 180 degrees rotates the paths and the fridge trip with it to 1e-9 (a test, over eight seeds). In the U kitchen a fridge trip is planned from a work wall that is not yaw 0 and the hinge matches the world-frame door. The stand rule moved out of `SimEnv` into `arrival/sim/preflight.py` (`base_pose`, `place_point`, pure functions of the twin), so `make_tasks` can ask where the base would stand without building a MuJoCo model. `check_task` rejects a candidate variation whose stand spot is outside the room, within 0.15 m of a wall face or a fixture, or whose object, place point, drawer tray or wipe strokes are farther than `REACH` = 0.745 m from the base, at every start offset `make_tasks` can draw. That constant is measured, not the 0.78 the base placement used to assume: with the mug on the rotated kitchen's east run, `put_in_drawer` succeeded at 0.742 m and missed at 0.75, 0.76 and 0.77 m. It is a horizontal-envelope test, not an IK solve; the reach planner (L17) would replace it. Rejections: the rotated kitchen's fridge (its free edge is at the room corner, so the base would stand at y = -0.02, outside the room) and `mug_2` for `put_in_drawer` on the east run; the U kitchen's corner cabinet for drawer and door tasks (as #77 had it, now from one check); the hero kitchen loses nothing. Past the pre-flight, `collect` wraps each task's env build and rollout: an exception is logged as `SKIP at build|rollout`, counted as `skipped_error`, closes the env and writes no episode, and the run goes on; a `VLANotConfigured`, or an `OSError` while the policy is `vla`, still propagates, because that is the server, not the task. `attempts` now counts tasks that ran to the physics check, so `dropped` is physics misses only; `skipped`, `skipped_unreachable`, `skipped_error` and `skip_reasons` are in the report and in `info.json` (`arrival_*`). The worker fails a job on which nothing can run as `unsupported layout: ...` with `error_kind: unsupported_layout` and the reasons, no traceback, and records `skipped` on every job. Checked: hero kitchen, 14 episodes, seed 5, two people, `--families all`: the 14 result lines (outcome, IK error, minimum human distance) are identical to origin/main to the digit, 13/14 with one human contact on both. Rotated kitchen, same settings: 12/14, the two misses being the bowl `counter_to_sink` picks, which is a grasp limit and not the wall (the hero kitchen's bowl, moved from 1.15 m to 0.9 m or farther along the counter, misses the same way); no human contact. U kitchen, six episodes over three families with two people: 6/6, one fridge trip, one corner cabinet rejected. Not done: the base is still fixed per episode, so a corner fixture whose only stand spot is outside the room (the rotated kitchen's fridge) stays unreachable until MAN-3 gives it somewhere else to go, and the pre-flight does not check the arm against wall cabinets or the people.

**W1-jobs: worker limits, concurrent claiming, retention and metering (L3, L18, L19, L20).** `arrival/jobs/limits.py` names every ceiling (`Limits`, overridable from `ARRIVAL_*` variables; a bad value is a `LimitError` naming the variable). `arrival work` now runs each job in a child process (`arrival/jobs/child.py`) and kills it at `--max-job-seconds` (default 1800), recording `TimeoutError ... during stage 'sim'`; a child that dies on its own is recorded as `WorkerProcessError` instead of leaving the job `running`. `--max-episodes` and `--max-people` refuse the worker's own options before any job is touched, an oversized `upload.bin` fails at `normalize`, and the web server returns 413 from the configured upload limit and 429 when `ARRIVAL_MAX_QUEUE` jobs are already queued or running (the count is checked before the body is read and again at creation, and is not atomic, so simultaneous uploads can overshoot a little). No memory cap exists: that was part of L3 and is not done, because `RLIMIT_AS` is unreliable on macOS and nothing was measured on Linux. Claiming is now `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` on `claim.lock`; a heartbeat thread touches it while the job runs, and `reap_stale` (called at start and periodically by every worker, serialised per job by `reap.lock`) requeues a `running` job whose heartbeat is 120 s old or whose lock belongs to a dead process on the same host, failing it with `WorkerLost` after 2 requeues. This replaces the old rule that no second worker may be started; the tests race 4 threads and 3 real processes over one queue and assert every job is claimed exactly once, and that 4 simultaneous reapers requeue a dead job once. `cancel_job` exists (queued: immediate; running: the child is killed) but no HTTP endpoint or page button calls it, and there is no per-episode progress. `arrival gc --max-age-hours N [--dry-run]` and the worker's optional sweep (`ARRIVAL_RETENTION_HOURS`, off by default) delete finished jobs with their twin, dataset and report, only for rooms named after the job's own id; there is no `DELETE /jobs/<id>` and no retention note on the page yet. Each finished job gets a `usage` block in `status.json` (wall seconds, per-stage seconds, episodes, frames, upload, zip and dataset bytes; unmeasured values are `null`) and a line in `out/usage/jobs.jsonl`, which survives `gc`; `arrival.eval.cost` reads both into job rows and a `metering` total and prices nothing from them, since the run is local CPU. Per-shard GPU seconds, token counts and the `scripts/teleop_baseline.py` timer from L20 are not done. 31 new tests in `tests/test_jobs_ops.py` use corrupt uploads and fake child commands (no sim, under 4 s); the one real-sim job test in `tests/test_worker.py` now runs through the child process and checks the metered frames against the dataset's `info.json`.

**CAP-1: stop polling cancelled capture jobs.** The worker and retention contract treat `cancelled` as terminal, but the iOS saved-scan model only recognized `done` and `failed`. It now also recognizes `cancelled`, so the existing poll loop exits after cancellation instead of requesting status indefinitely. Saved-scan metadata moved unchanged into Foundation-only `Scan.swift` to exercise the actual model without RoomPlan. Seven offline compiled-Swift cases round-trip metadata through JSON and check terminal, active, missing and unknown status values. The cancelled case failed before the predicate fix. No changes to server behavior, metadata schema or website.

**W2-web: web hardening, `arrival doctor`, RGB-D upload (L4, L22, L14).** `arrival/web/app.py` now refuses what a public URL would attract: job ids are 128 random bits (`secrets.token_hex(16)`, was `uuid4().hex[:8]`) and every id route checks them against a strict pattern, uploads must be a `.json` scan starting with `{` (and an optional `.zip` of frames), the client filename is reduced to a safe label and never becomes a path, and a per-client sliding-window rate limit (429), a queue cap (503), a concurrent-upload cap (503), a free-disk check (507), `--max-upload-mb` (413) and a 10 minute body deadline bound what one client can cost. Responses carry `nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, a hash-based CSP (the inline style attribute was replaced by a class so no `unsafe-inline` is needed), HSTS and a `Secure` cookie when `--behind-proxy` sees `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`; errors are generic pages or JSON and crashes log a redacted line to stderr. `--public` lists only the visitor's own jobs (cookie token, hash stored as `owner`) and strips server paths from the status API. `arrival doctor` (`arrival/doctor.py`) checks Python, the core packages, MuJoCo (warning only), a running ffmpeg, free disk, and whether the four optional keys and URLs are set in the environment or a `.env`, never reading a value into its output. `POST /jobs` takes a `frames` zip in the CAP-2a layout (`arrival/web/frames.py`): zip-slip, symlink, type, count and streamed-size guards, then `load_manifest` plus a per-frame image and depth check, stored under `out/jobs/<id>/frames/` with `"consumed": false` because nothing reads frames yet. The queue gained `extra_status` and `adopt` arguments so frames land before the job is visible to the worker. Not done: authentication and TLS (put a proxy in front), per-user quotas on total disk, retention of old jobs (L18), the secrets loader `arrival/config.py` (each model module still reads `.env` itself) and any use of the frames. The scan upload is sniffed, not parsed. 73 new test cases in `tests/test_web.py`; no real frame or network was used, frames come from `write_synthetic_bundle`.

**W3: CI matrix, repo checks and an offline `make demo`.** CI now runs the test, demo and validate steps on ubuntu, macOS and Windows with Python 3.11 and 3.12. Only ubuntu can fail the run (`continue-on-error` on the other two): hosted runners have no GPU, and MuJoCo offscreen rendering on macOS and Windows runners has not been seen to work, so those rows are evidence to read, not a gate. None of the workflow has run on a real runner yet; it was checked by parsing it and by running each command locally on macOS. On Linux the job still tries EGL and falls back to OSMesa. New jobs: `lint` (ruff with only the syntax-error and undefined-name rules, because the code still has 12 unused imports and variables in files other branches are editing; `check_docs.py`; the new `check_links.py`), `package` (`scripts/smoke_package.py` builds the sdist and wheel, installs the wheel in a fresh venv and runs it from another directory) and `docker` (builds the CPU image and runs `validate --help`). Each test cell uploads `demo_summary.md`, `demo_summary.json` and `index.html` as an artifact, pip is cached, and `.github/dependabot.yml` covers Actions, pip and Docker. Not done: secret scanning with gitleaks (its action wants a token secret and the tests assert CI uses none) and pinned dependency constraints (not verifiable on all six OS and Python cells from one laptop). The smoke test shows that the wheel does not ship `scans/` or `configs/`: an installed copy fails without `--scans`, in one clear line, which the smoke test asserts. So "scans found outside the repo root" is false today and is documented rather than hidden. The Windows black-video-after-episode-0 bug from L6 was not reproduced or fixed; there is no Windows machine here. `check_docs.py` was failing on `main` because the README to-do section names files that do not exist yet; it now skips code-path existence checks under a `## ... to-do` heading (links are still checked). `check_links.py` found no broken links in the 28 tracked Markdown files. Also added: `CONTRIBUTING.md` and more `.gitignore` entries (`build/`, `dist/`, `.ruff_cache/`, editor and OS files). `make demo` is now `scripts/demo_all.py --quick --out out` with an environment preflight (exit 2 and one line if mujoco is missing or no GL backend renders a 16 px frame; backends are probed in child processes because a bad one can abort the interpreter), an offline guard (API keys hidden, non-loopback connections refused), a `--timeout` watchdog (default 900 s, exit 1) and a self-contained `index.html`. A `--quick` run took about 20 s and 0.5 GB on an Apple Silicon laptop; a from-scratch venv install was not timed because it needs the package index. Whether `plan.pdf` should be public, regenerating `demo.mp4` and `demo.gif`, the README status table and closing issues #18 to #34 need the owner and were left alone.

**W4: twin compile fixes, scan fuzz, provenance, `arrival realism` (L10, L11, L15, L16).** Twin: PR #49's commits (credited to aarochu) are cherry-picked, so a counter-height RoomPlan `storage` cabinet under a sink counts as the counter and no `counter_synth_<sink>` is stacked over it. A test rebuilds the U-shaped scan geometry of #42 (positions and yaws from its table, sizes assumed) and asserts no overlapping fixtures and a scene that builds. A synthesized counter now stops at a fridge, dishwasher, tall cabinet or real counter standing in its row (it still holds the sink, to within 2 cm), and neighbouring sinks share one counter instead of stacking a counter per sink with a sink on every seam. The old default, 1.8 m centred on the sink, is unchanged when nothing is beside it. Islands were already measured against oblique walls and open sides; tests now pin that. Scan robustness: `roomplan.to_bundle` and `bundle.validate` refuse an export the twin cannot be built from with a `ScanBundleError` that names the item (missing or non-finite transform or dimensions, a floor polygon that is not 3-D points, a non-integer story, a zero-length wall, a dimension above 100 m, a coordinate above 1000 m, walls spanning under 0.5 m or over 100 m, duplicate ids), RoomPlan identifiers are made safe for XML and unique, and `build_mjcf` says so when a twin has no walls. A zero-size object still falls back to the catalog default size, as before. `tests/test_scan_fuzz.py` runs 300 seeded random kitchens (rotated, mirrored, open walls, zero to three corruptions each) plus named variants (open wall, rotated 30/90/137 degrees, mirrored, no sink, five sinks, island, 1.2 m and 14 m rooms, the #42 U) through normalize, compile, default objects, layout, tasks and a MuJoCo scene build, and fails on anything but a sane twin or a `ScanBundleError`; before the fixes about half of 150 seeds escaped as KeyError, TypeError, ValueError and MuJoCo size errors, and `ARRIVAL_FUZZ_N=6000` ran clean. A twin with no fixtures or no sink compiles and proposes no tasks: that is a valid twin, not a refusal, and the worker still reports it as zero successful episodes. Tasks on scanned cabinets (#77) and the base pose for rotated kitchens (#75) are not part of this change. Provenance: `twin.json` gets `provenance` (input file sha256 and normalized-bundle sha256, git sha and dirty flag, generators, seeds), every dataset's `meta/info.json` gets `arrival_provenance`, and each twin object is labelled `authored` or `default_synthetic`; `detected` is reserved because nothing detects objects. The web UI and job result do not show the labels yet. `arrival realism <scan>` drives `eval/realism.py` over a scan's `frames_manifest.json`: held-out frames only, ARKit axes converted to the renderer's, invalid-confidence depth masked, a misaligned-pose control that must raise depth error or the run is marked invalid with exit 1, JSON plus side-by-side PNG, and a refusal for a scan with no frames. It has only been run on frames rendered from the twin itself (error about zero, control error higher, wrong axes caught); no real scan has been scored, so `realism` in `docs/07-eval.md` stays null. Tests: 7 new in `tests/test_compile.py`, 322 in `tests/test_scan_fuzz.py` (300 of them seeded sweeps), 14 in `tests/test_provenance.py`, 16 in `tests/test_realism_cli.py`.

**L12 + L28 + L23: per-episode validator, multi-room summary, static page export.** `arrival/export/quality.py` adds a per-episode pass next to the structural `validate`: `arrival validate DIR --episodes` flags NaN/inf, zero motion, a stuck arm (state frozen over 40 steps while the command is over 0.15 rad away), a joint within 3% of its limit for over 40 steps (`arrival_sim_panda` only), length outliers (2.5x off the family median, families of 4+) and, with `--frames`, blank cameras; `--drop CLEAN_DIR` writes a re-indexed copy with stats recomputed, a `meta/dropped_episodes.json` and a structural re-check. The thresholds were set against a real 2-episode `hero_kitchen_sim` run (largest tracking gap 0.32 rad, no joint within 3% of a stop) so good sim data passes, and the tests inject each defect into a synthetic dataset. Carried-object tilt and "useful wrist pixels" from the L12 text are not done: the dataset stores no object pose, and only frame uniformity can be measured. `arrival summary` (`arrival/eval/summary.py`) runs normalize, compile, tasks, sim and validate on hero_kitchen plus the two real RoomPlan fixtures with 2 episodes at 64 px and writes `out/summary/summary.md` and `.json`; on this machine hero_kitchen and amal passed every stage and shopyseek compiled but 0 of 2 episodes passed the physics check (the miss reason is in the table), which is the honest state of RoomPlan rooms without kitchen fixtures. Tape error appears only for hero_kitchen and is labelled authored, realism is "not measured" for every room (no held-out frames exist), and invented objects are labelled `default_synthetic`. `arrival export-site` (`arrival/web/static_export.py`) crawls the real page on an ephemeral port and writes `out/static_site/` with relative links, `data/*.json`, previews, no form and no script, optional zips, and a link check that discards the export if any link is broken. It does not touch `site/`, and a Pages workflow and CORS for a hosted upload are not part of it. 28 tests.

**Room summaries stop after structural validation fails.** Returned validation errors and validator exceptions now mark later per-episode checks not_run rather than checking malformed data and potentially presenting an OK cell. The dataset path and validation reason stay in the report. Two regressions assert the quality checker is never called; the existing successful simulated room still exercises the complete pipeline.

**QUALITY: detect featureless colored video frames.** The blank-frame check measured variation across RGB channels as well as spatial pixels. A uniformly red, green or blue image therefore appeared variable even though it had no spatial content. It now measures spatial deviation per channel and uses the largest deviation, retaining any channel's real image structure. Three encoded solid-color clips reproduce the missed blank finding; an encoded checkerboard control stays accepted. Existing blank-fraction thresholds and website UI are unchanged.

**Mobile base on top of any-wall, 11-float schema (L13, #33).** #33 (Aadi Tiwari's MAN-3, one commit on the old main) is taken over #36 because it matches the plan in #18: base pose at `state[8:11]` and `action[8:11]`, 0.3 m/s. It conflicted with everything the any-wall branch changed (`collect.py`, `policy.py`, `people.py`, `tasks.py`, `scene.py`, `export.py`, `eval/harness.py`, `cli.py`), so it was re-applied by hand rather than merged. What changed from the original: the base stand rule lives in `preflight.base_pose(..., mobile=)` (a mobile `counter_to_counter` parks 0.3 m toward the target along the source run, in the run's own frame) with `drive_spot` giving the place-side spot, so a run on any wall drives along that wall; `check_task(..., mobile=)` checks both stand spots, nine points along the straight drive for walls and fixtures (`base drive path`) and the place point against the drive spot's reach, and `make_tasks(mobile=True)` drops the "target within 1 m of the object" rule for counters of the same run. The expert plans base moves as extra waypoint fields in `ScriptedExpert._plan`, which now returns 11-wide targets in both modes (the base part is the parked pose unless a waypoint drives), sizes a drive so the min-jerk peak stays under 0.3 m/s and 0.5 rad/s, and uses the any-wall `env.grasp` and `run_yaw`. The walkers' loop keeps clear of the whole drive (`base_to`, turned into the work frame like `base_xy`) and the base waits while a person is within 0.7 m ahead. The fixed base is the default and builds the byte-identical MJCF. Not taken from #33: its `VLAClient` body (main now has the shared `policy_server/protocol.py`, which is 8-float arm only), so `--base mobile --policy vla` is refused with exit 2 instead of silently holding the base. The 8 vs 11 decision, stated in `docs/REFERENCE.md`: every `sim` dataset is 11 floats with a `base` slice at [8, 11); `configs/gr00t/arrival_panda_config.py` reads only the first 8 (the default, for the fixed base whose three columns are constant) and the new `arrival_panda_mobile_config.py` adds `base` as an absolute commanded pose; old 8-float `sim` datasets need regenerating to be mixed with new ones. `arrival/export/loader_check.py` now requires the base slice at [8, 11) with 11-float rows and a boolean `base_actuated`, errors on a base that moves while declared fixed (`base_fixed_moves`) or moves faster than the writer allows (`base_speed`), and reports a parked base's constant columns as one `fixed_base_constant` warning instead of a generic constant-dim one. Measured on the merged branch (macOS, mujoco 3.16, res 32): hero kitchen `--base mobile --families counter_to_counter --episodes 6 --people 2 --seed 1` 6/6, 0 human contacts, closest 0.11 m, the dataset validates and passes the loader check (2404 frames, 11-float rows); `--families all --episodes 8 --people 2 --seed 1 --base mobile` 8/8 over eight families; the fixed base at `--seed 5 --episodes 14` is 13/14 with one human contact, the same line as before this change; on `u_kitchen_42`, whose storage run faces -y (yaw pi), mobile `counter_to_counter` is 3/3 with and without people while a fixed base finds no pair there. In the hero kitchen the drive is along x, so the base y and yaw columns are constant even in a mobile dataset (the loader check says so with `constant_dim`). Not done: the base still parks once for every other family, so `open_door` still stops at 63-66 degrees and `put_in_drawer` keeps its 0.6 m rule; a target on a different wall needs a turn and a route and is not offered; and none of this has been through Isaac-GR00T's own loader (G1), so whether it accepts an arm-only config over 11-float rows is untested.

**EXPORT: validate modality bounds before sorting slices.** Sorting slice tuples could compare integers with null/string bounds before the later type checks ran, and a null slice object crashed on get. Each slice is now checked for an object with exact integer start/end bounds before sorting; booleans are rejected as bounds. Ten state/action regressions use valid two-slice datasets before changing one slice: eight reproduced crashes or incorrect acceptance, and two preserve rejection of fractional starts. No website changes.

L5 (shardable, resumable sim collection): `arrival sim --shard i/n` runs a contiguous block of the `--episodes` tasks that `make_tasks` draws (every shard builds the full list from the same seed, so each task keeps its seed and `sim_<family>_<global index>` id, and the families stay balanced inside a shard), into `out/shards/<i>of<n>/`; `arrival merge-shards` joins the finished shards into one dataset with episode, task and frame indices and stats rebuilt, a summed report and a validator pass, and refuses a missing, duplicate, unfinished or mismatched shard. Every run now journals each finished task (`out/progress/<room>_sim.jsonl`, written after the episode files) and `--resume` continues from it, removing the files of the task a kill interrupted and refusing a journal from different settings. New code is `arrival/jobs/shards.py` plus `SimDatasetWriter.restore/add_copied` and shard/resume arguments on `collect`. Measured on the merged branch (macOS, mujoco 3.16, res 32, hero kitchen, `--families counter_to_sink,open_drawer --episodes 4 --seed 3 --people 0`): two shards of two tasks merge to the same episodes.jsonl rows, tasks.jsonl, per-frame parquet contents, stats and `info` counts as one run of four, and the merged dataset validates; a shard killed during its second task and resumed equals the uninterrupted shard. The same change fixes a latent limit in the sim writer, which put every episode in `chunk-000` while the validator expects `chunk-{i // 1000}`, so a merged run of more than 1,000 episodes could not validate (tested with a shrunk chunk size, not with 1,000 real episodes). Not done: nothing submits or monitors shards on Nebius (C1), merge assumes the shard folders are on one disk, and no run was done across machines or with `--policy vla`.

**SIM-5: a scanned office becomes a room the arm can work in.** On the real `tests/fixtures/roomplan/amal.json` export (a 2.96 m table and two chairs) the twin compiled the table to a `counter`, so `counter_xml` built it as a kitchen cabinet with a drawer stack and a hinged door, and `make_tasks` gave 20 drawer and door tasks on a dining table and nothing else; the chairs (type `box`) were not built at all, so arm, base and objects could pass through them. Now a dining-config counter (`is_table`) is built by `table_xml` as a top on four legs with no moving parts and is left out of every drawer and door family (and of `part_names`), and every fixture type without its own model (chairs, beds, sofas, TVs, storage) is one solid box (`furniture_xml`). The new `move_on_table` family picks a pickable object (or the bowl by its rim) off a table and sets it down 0.35 m along the edge the robot stands at: the edge nearer the object, only when the object is within 0.44 m of it (the pre-flight reach, see L9 below), and only when the base would be at least 0.25 m from furniture and walls. Success is the object resting on the table top within 6 cm of the target and nearly still. It reuses the counter-to-counter pick and place waypoints with `place_point` returning the target spot. Walkers needed the floor strip in front of a kitchen-style fixture run, and `arrival sim` on the office scan stopped with a numpy error at its default of two people; the first version of this change ran such episodes without people and recorded `people_skipped`, a workaround that L9 below dropped once the any-wall change (L1) made the walkers use the whole floor. `move_on_table` is in the default families of `sim`, `eval`, `work` and the upload worker; a kitchen has no table, so it is skipped there and kitchen datasets do not change (checked: `sim --episodes 4 --people 2` still gives counter_to_sink 2/2, open_drawer 2/2). Measured on the office scan: the three reachable objects (cup, apple, mug) each succeed, landing 1.6 to 3.5 mm from the target, with no arm overlap with the table, chairs or walls; `demo --room office --from-roomplan` then `sim --families all --episodes 8 --people 2` gives move_on_table 8/8 and a dataset that validates. The second real scan, `shopyseek` (a bedroom), gets no task: its desk's only free long side is where its chair stands, so a fixed base has nowhere to go. Not done: tasks for beds or sofas, reaching objects in the middle of a large table (needs the mobile base, MAN-3). `arrival demo`'s placeholder generator still proposes no tasks for the office.

**Non-kitchen rooms, rebased (L9, #57).** #57 (SIM-5, one commit on the old main) is merged onto the any-wall, pre-flight and shard chain and its credit is kept in the PR body. Where it overlapped, the chain won: its `people_skipped` workaround is dropped (the walkers use the whole floor when a room has no strip, and a test now runs an office episode with two people), its solid-furniture `furniture_xml` is the chain's, and `move_on_table` is placed in `arrival/sim/preflight.py` like the other families (`base_pose` stands at the table edge on the object's side facing in, `place_point` returns the target spot, `reach_points` checks the object and the target against the 0.745 m reach) so a variation the base cannot stand at or reach is rejected and counted, not tried. The default objects now depend on the room: `room_kind` in `arrival/catalog/objects.py` reads the scanned categories (any kitchen fixture, then bed, then sofa, television or fireplace, then table or chair; nothing scanned is a kitchen, as before) and `place_default_objects` draws from `ROOM_OBJECTS`: the kitchen keeps its mug, bowl, plate, cup and apple (the hero kitchen run is unchanged: `sim --episodes 4 --people 2` still gives counter_to_sink 2/2, open_drawer 2/2), an office gets a laptop, a book and a cup, a living room a remote, a book and a cup, a bedroom a book and a cup. Three catalog entries are new: a book (17 x 7.5 x 2.5 cm, 0.30 kg), a remote (17 x 4.5 x 2 cm, 0.10 kg) and a closed laptop (30 x 21 x 2 cm, 1.2 kg). The book and remote lie long side along the run so the pads close across the short side, and both are in `PICKABLE` because the scripted expert moved each on the office table in physics (target error 3.7 and 5.1 mm); the laptop is wider than the 10 cm opening in both directions, so it stays scenery and is never picked. On a table deeper than 0.8 m, outside a kitchen, the objects go within 0.40 m of a long edge instead of anywhere on a 1.6 m top, because a fixed arm reaches 0.44 m past the edge it stands at and most random spots were out of reach (the first draft left the default office with no task at all for seeds 0 and 1). A first sim on the office found 2 of 6 episodes missing: the book had been set down on the cup already at its target, so `table_move` now skips a target that overlaps another object (4 cm gap) and tries the other way along the edge before giving up. Office sim after the change, `--episodes 8 --people 2 --res 64 --seed 3`: move_on_table 8/8, validator passes. Not done: a living-room scan is not in the repo, so that path is covered by a test that adds a sofa to the office scan, not by a real export; a room with no table or counter (a bedroom with only a bed) still gets no objects and no task; the bedroom scan `shopyseek` still gets no task because its chair blocks the desk.

**MAN-5, second half: open oven, open dishwasher, turn on faucet, load and unload the dishwasher (L24, from #27).** Five families on the parts SIM-3 added. The two bottom-hinged doors carry their bar on the outside of the panel, so a top-down grasp puts the hand body into the panel as soon as the door tilts; the hand instead points into the panel with the fingers closing across the bar vertically, and that frame turns with the door. The handle swings toward the base as the door opens, and past about 0.5 rad the wrist would have to fold back under the arm (IK residual 19 cm at 0.75 rad), so the expert pulls to 0.5 rad and lets go; gravity takes the door to its flat stop, and a test checks the door is already past 0.4 rad while the gripper is still closed. The base stands straight in front, 6 cm beyond where the open door's top edge lands; parking beside the swing was tried and puts the wrist under the arm (IK residual 10 to 20 cm). The dishwasher bar is 4 cm lower and 3 cm further out than the oven's and did not reach with a horizontal hand (13 cm residual), so the hand tilt below the door's horizontal is chosen per task from three candidates by the IK residual of the whole chain: 0 for the oven, 0.7 rad for the dishwasher. The faucet lever is pushed up from below with the closed fingers, hand horizontal, from 34 cm in front of the counter (closer is worse for this reach above shoulder height: 22 cm gave a 5 cm residual and a half-lifted lever, 40 cm is out of reach). Two things about the lever came out of an independent check: SIM-3's lever had its centre of mass out on the handle with almost no damping, so it crept back to off within 17 s of being let go, and any touch flung it past its 1.2 rad stop; it is now balanced on its cartridge (centre of mass on the hinge) with ten times the damping, so it stays where it is put, and the push is sized to the lever's travel (fingers start 4.5 cm under the tip, rise 5 cm) so it ends at 1.12 rad without being driven into the stop, and a test holds the arm still for 20 s afterwards and checks the lever is still up. For loading and unloading, the SIM-3 dishwasher became a hollow carcass with an upper rack on a slide joint that rolls out over the flat door; the lower rack would sit 15 cm off the floor, below what the arm reaches from its pedestal, so it was not built. The base parks beside the dishwasher in front of the counter; the first version of unloading dipped the hand onto the dishwasher's top edge on one long joint-space move from HOME and then dropped the cup on the way to the counter when lifted 54 cm in one second, so the approach and the lift are chains of 10 cm via-points like put_in_drawer's descent. Measured on the rebased code (L24, #27 on the any-wall chain), 2 seeds each, with 0 and with 2 people, res 32, scripted expert, physics-checked: open_oven and turn_on_faucet 4/4 each in the hero kitchen (door to its 1.50 rad stop, lever 1.12 rad and holding); open_dishwasher, load_dishwasher, unload_dishwasher and turn_on_faucet 4/4 each on the test twin with a dishwasher at the counter's end (the hero scan has none, so those three families are skipped there with a log line); and all five 4/4 each on the same layout turned to an east-wall run (yaw 90 degrees, a stove added), 44/44 in all with no human contact. Max IK residual 1.3 cm (oven), 2.7 cm (faucet), 5.8 cm (dishwasher door, during the pull), 6.4 cm (oven on the east wall), 0 for load and unload. Rebasing onto the chain changed three things. The stand rules moved out of `SimEnv` into `preflight.base_pose`, so the five stand spots are now written in the fixture's own frame (`park`), and `check_task` also rejects a dishwasher task whose object or rack spot is out of reach and a stand spot outside the room or too near a wall; the pool rules use the fixture's frame too (`_holder`, `to_local`) instead of an x-only test on counter centres. `finish_task` already lived in `arrival.sim.tasks` and is shared by `collect()`, the eval harness and the retarget path, so the half of #27 that moved it is dropped and only its five family entries are added; a test checks the harness and tasks module hold the same function and that every joint and handle name it writes exists in the model the task runs in. And `turn_on_faucet` now pushes along the front of the counter run the sink is set into, not the sink's own yaw. The one sensitivity found on the east-wall layout: with a 0.92 m stove (the hero's is 0.90 m) the oven door only reached 0.01 rad, so `open_oven` is measured on the hero's stove size only; the cause was not investigated, and it is probably the bar-height edge of the arm's reach noted above. The lower dishwasher rack is still not built.

**L25: the real Franka behind `--arm menagerie` (#25 rebased onto the any-wall, mobile-base and appliance chain).** PR #25 (Aadi Tiwari, MAN-6) is cherry-picked in spirit and re-applied by hand: its `menagerie.py`, fetch script and tests are kept, and the wiring was redone on the current `SimEnv`, `collect`, eval harness, shard merge and CLI, which had all moved. `python scripts/fetch_menagerie.py` sparse-clones only `franka_emika_panda` at a pinned commit into `arrival/assets/menagerie/` (34 MB, gitignored, never committed, never downloaded by a test or by CI); with the folder absent `sim --arm menagerie` and `eval --arm menagerie` exit 2 with the fetch command, and the Menagerie tests skip (a test also checks the folder is gitignored and nothing under `arrival/assets` is tracked). `menagerie.arm_fragments` rewrites `panda.xml` at load time: `link0` becomes `robot_base` on the same pedestal and ego camera as the procedural arm, the fingers become `finger_l`/`finger_r` with the procedural pads' friction, the hand gets the current `wrist` camera and an `eef` site at the pad centres, the collision meshes take the robot's contact classes, and `robot.actuators_xml` supplies the position actuators. Rebasing changed three things: the pedestal pieces now live in `robot.py` (`pedestal_xml`, `base_joints_xml`) so the Menagerie mount also works with `--base mobile` (planar joints first in `robot_base`, three base actuators after the fingers); the writer, reports, journal fingerprint and shard-merge check carry `arm` (`embodiment.json` has `arm` and `gripper_open`, and the expert opens to 0.04 m per finger on the real hand, 0.05 on the procedural one); and the eval loop's local `arm` string (the arm-contact note) shadowed the new `arm` argument after the first episode, so it is renamed `arm_note`, with a test that every episode gets the requested arm. The procedural arm is unchanged: the MJCF is byte-identical to the previous branch's over 48 combinations of base mode, yaw, lighting, people and base position (SHA-256 of the concatenated strings compared). Measured on the hero kitchen with 2 episodes per family, res 32, scripted expert, `eval` seeds 0 and 1, fixed base: before the fixes below the real arm was 14/20 on seed 0 (counter_to_sink, open_drawer, open_door, close_drawer, put_in_drawer, wipe_counter and open_fridge all 2/2; close_door, open_oven and turn_on_faucet 0/2); after them it is 18/20 on seed 0 and 20/20 on seed 1, both with 0 and with 2 people (80 episodes in all, 76 successes), every family 2/2 except close_door on seed 0; the procedural arm is 2/2 on the families re-checked (open_door, close_door, open_oven, turn_on_faucet). `open_door` (the door family PR #25 reported failing) already passes with the real hand on the current code, 2/2 on both seeds. Two fixes were geometry, found by tracing the eef and contacts. The real hand's pads are 17 mm tall against the procedural pads' 60 mm and sit at the eef, not 6.6 mm below it, so the flap-door plan's 15 mm eef-to-bar offset left the bar at the pad's edge and it slipped out; `HAND["flap_bar"]` is 8.4 mm for the real hand, which keeps the same bar-to-pad distance. After letting the oven door go the wider hand sat under the falling panel at the procedural 15 cm back-off and held it at 0.61 rad, so `HAND["flap_up"]` is 0.30 m (0.12 m out and 0.30 up, 0.20 out and 0.30 up, and 0.0 out and 0.25 up all let it fall to its 1.50 rad stop; 0.20 out and 0.15 up did not; the procedural 0.15 m is untouched). The faucet is pushed with the closed fingertips, which reach 8 mm past the eef on the real hand and 23 mm on the procedural fingers, so the base stands 2 cm closer (`preflight.ARM_STANDOFF_TRIM`; a standoff of 0.34 never reached the lever, 0.32 turns it to 1.10 rad against 1.13 procedural, 0.30 and below only 0.6). Not fixed: `close_door` on seed 0 (counter_b, the east counter 5 cm from the wall) fails 0/2 with the real hand with or without people, because the hand body touches `wall_east` on the way to the bar and the door stops at 0.33 rad; the same task passes with the procedural hand (hand box 18 cm) and the real hand closes the other door on seed 1, 2/2. Turning the hand a quarter turn about its approach axis was tried and cleared the IK error but pushed the door further open, and was dropped. The real hand also has no mesh for the camera housing the procedural wrist has, and the real pads are the Menagerie collision boxes with the procedural pad friction (2.0), not a measured rubber coefficient. Pad friction above 3 and finger force of 70 to 100 N were tried on the oven and did not replace the geometry fix. No render, GPU or paid call is involved; the Franka meshes are used only through `--arm menagerie`, the default is unchanged.


**DEM-3: Windows release checks.** Docs checks now read UTF-8 explicitly and compare slash-separated repository paths. Portable tests are required on Linux, macOS and Windows; tests that create a renderer are reported as skipped there, while a separate required Linux job probes its graphics backend and runs the complete suite and offline demo. The Docker smoke requires a responsive Linux-container daemon. The status-file concurrency test keeps its read-count check without requiring a fixed disk throughput. On Windows with Python 3.13.3 and MuJoCo 3.16.0, the full suite passed with 373 passed and 3 skipped. The full offline demo exported 8 episodes and 1,408 frames, validated, and decoded all frames from both cameras across every episode without blank frames. A regression test checks both cameras across successive episodes. Hosted Actions and other operating systems have not been run for this patch.

**DEM-3: hosted portable fixture ordering.** The first hosted run exposed a gap in `--no-render`: module-scoped dataset fixtures opened the renderer before the function-scoped guard ran. Windows and macOS reported graphics-context errors, and Linux aborted. The guard now patches the public renderer before test collection and marks rendering tests to skip before their fixtures start. Subprocess regressions record renderer calls from session, module and function fixtures, an imported constructor, a plugin's session fixture and a marked test's child-process fixture. Portable mode must make no renderer calls; the normal suite must execute them.

**DEM-3: software-rendering CI budget.** Hosted Linux runs of the companion branches hit the 20-minute job limit before their full suites finished. The rendering gate now allows 75 minutes for the complete suite and offline demo. The portable gate keeps its 20-minute limit. Both gates remain required.


**W3: documentation section boundaries.** Planned code paths remain exempt only within actual level-two to-do sections. A level-one or level-two heading ends that scope; heading examples inside fenced code do not change it. Regression cases cover space and tab separators and both Markdown fence styles, while broken links still fail inside planned sections.


**DAT-3: replayable evaluation records.** Each evaluation now writes a separate run directory with a hashed manifest, per-attempt JSONL and a completed summary. The manifest embeds the twin and ordered tasks, so policy comparisons replay the same inputs without recompiling or resampling. VLA runs require a caller-declared checkpoint label; the server does not attest that identity. Invalid hashes, twin hashes, duplicate tasks, missing trials and unsupported rollout settings are rejected before simulation. Errors count as failed attempts while unobserved safety and simulation time remain null; summaries name their observed-episode denominator. Invalid rollout results are saved as error rows and later trials continue. The CLI accepts a saved manifest, and the demo and cost reader follow the new output layout while the cost reader retains legacy reports. Interrupted runs keep their trial records without a completed summary. Tests check exact replay inputs, non-overwrite, aggregate reconciliation and failure records; local HTTP mock policies exercise the same evaluator. This is fixed-base evaluation bookkeeping, not evidence that a learned policy improves. Source and simulator versions still need to match for controlled comparisons.


**DAT-3: standalone CI prerequisites.** The hosted suite found the inherited documentation checker rejecting planned file names in the README to-do section. The branch now carries the existing W3 checker fix and the Windows UTF-8/path fixes; links and implemented paths remain checked. The Linux software-rendering suite reached the job time limit before finishing, so the full required test job now has a 60-minute limit and reports slow tests. No tests or render checks are removed.


**EVAL-1a / L14: uploaded frames reach the comparison page.** The worker now compares uploaded held-out RGB-D views against the compiled twin before it places synthetic objects. It uses the realism driver and provenance helpers from #100 so the web path and command share the same masks, frame sampling and camera-offset control. The driver accepts an explicit camera transform and uses numeric preview filenames. RoomPlan normalization records its ARKit-world transform; the worker maps frame poses through it and checks source room IDs when the scan provides one. The job page links the comparison image and safe metrics, with owner checks in public mode. Unavailable or invalid comparisons have null metrics, a comparison error leaves dataset generation running, and retries clear the previous result. Synthetic HTTP tests exercise the whole upload, worker, validation, ZIP and preview flow, including room-frame changes, low-confidence depth, train exclusion, failed controls and retries. A browser check displayed the image, metrics and JSON link without console errors. Windows upload checks also close depth mappings before rejecting an array and validate ZIP names before platform normalization. No real captured frames were used; this establishes wiring only.


**EVAL-1a: standalone CI prerequisites.** The hosted suite found the inherited documentation checker rejecting planned file names in the README to-do section. The branch now carries the existing W3 checker fix and the Windows UTF-8/path fixes; links and implemented paths remain checked. The Linux software-rendering suite reached the job time limit before finishing, so the full required test job now has a 60-minute limit and reports slow tests. No tests or render checks are removed.

**L20: teleop baseline timer.** `scripts/teleop_baseline.py` (logic in `arrival/sim/teleop.py`) is the capture path OPS-3 (#67) was waiting on: a person teleoperates `counter_to_sink` in the twin and the run writes the `teleop_baseline.json` that `arrival.eval.cost.load_baseline` already validates. Keys are read by its own GLFW window (`TeleopWindow`), not the MuJoCo viewer, because that viewer binds most letters to its own render toggles: the first person to try it typed `w` into the viewer and got wireframe instead of motion. The window is just an iterator of key lists, so tests replace it with a scripted one. Keys move an end-effector target in the base frame (forward is the base's +x, toward the counter) and the target goes through `ik.solve` with `ik.down(yaw)` every control step, warm-started from the last solution, so teleop shares the expert's IK, `SimEnv` and oracle. The dollar figure is only what the operator's hourly rate and setup cost make it; there is no default rate. `tests/test_teleop.py` drives a full successful mug-to-sink demo through the key path (key presses computed from the scene: over the mug, down, close, up, over the sink, down, open, Enter), checks the baseline loads in the cost module and that `--record` writes a valid dataset; the same scripted demo also succeeds through the window's key callbacks with the window rendering every step, next to `SimEnv`'s offscreen renderers. First human run, hero kitchen, bowl to sink: 2 attempts (first judged with the bowl still on the counter), 1 success, 117 operator seconds in all; the recorded demo validates and the cost report reads the baseline. Against it, 20 scripted `counter_to_sink` demos took 127 s of local wall time (about 6 s each). One operator and one success is a smoke test, not the ~20-success baseline OPS-3 asks for. The run also found that MuJoCo 3.16 dropped `mjv_moveCamera`'s scene argument, which crashed the window on the first drag; fixed with a fallback for older versions.

**L22: one settings loader.** `arrival/config.py` holds the table of every environment setting (`SETTINGS`: who reads it, whether it is a secret, its default) and the only `.env` reader; `token_factory.py` and `tavily.py` had each carried a copy of it (`load_dotenv` stays importable from both). The GR00T policy URL and `COSMOS_URL` used to ignore `.env`; they now go through `config.get` like the rest. `status()` reports set or unset without values, for `arrival doctor` (#97) to use. `redact()` scrubs the values of set secrets plus Bearer tokens, `nvapi-`/`tvly-`/`sk-`/`hf-` keys, `api_key=`-style pairs, `user:pass@` in URLs and `?token=`-style parameters; the `--policy vla` connection error goes through it, and `install_log_redaction` attaches it as a logging filter. `.env.example` lists every setting with empty values. `tests/conftest.py` sets `ARRIVAL_DOTENV=0`: before, a developer with `TOKEN_FACTORY_API_KEY` in a real `.env` would have had `tests/test_vlm_planner.py` load it and call the network, despite the tests deleting the variable first. `tests/test_config.py` also fails if any module under `arrival/` reads one of these variables from `os.environ` directly.

**CONFIG: preserve literal quotes inside dotenv values.** The lightweight reader used `strip` with both quote characters, removing every quote at either end rather than one matching outer pair. Values containing a literal opposite quote could change before use. It now removes exactly one matching quote pair after whitespace trimming. Four synthetic-value regressions cover opposite quote characters at one or both ends and reproduce the previous loss; existing environment precedence is unchanged. No website changes.

**C2 (part): smoke test for a running server.** `scripts/smoke_hosted.py` is the "good and bad upload" check from C2, written now so the hosted demo has an acceptance test before it exists. It talks HTTP only (stdlib `urllib`), so it runs the same against `python -m arrival serve` and a deployed URL. `tests/test_smoke_hosted.py` runs it against the real server (pass, and exactly one job created), against a stub server that answers 202 to every upload and 200 to every path (fail, naming the bad-upload and crafted-path checks), against a closed port (fails after the first check) and with `--wait` and no worker (fails on the unfinished job). Not done from C2: docker-compose, TLS, `/healthz`, `docs/DEPLOY.md`, the pre-seeded room and the Playwright browser smoke.

**CAP-1: reject unsupported capture-server URLs.** The iOS API client previously accepted any URL with a host, including FTP, file and WebSocket URLs. It now requires a nonempty host and an HTTP/HTTPS scheme and reports the supported schemes in the validation error. Bare LAN addresses still default to HTTP; whitespace trimming, trailing slash removal, HTTPS, path prefixes and IPv6 addresses are preserved. The Mac-side Swift test driver now exposes the real parser to twelve offline regression cases: five normalized addresses and seven invalid addresses. The unsupported-scheme cases failed against the original parser before the fix. Checked: all 17 iOS bundle/parser tests, simulator build, lint, documentation checks and diff whitespace checks passed. A read-only code review found no actionable issues. This change does not modify website files or require a network service.

**Reachability planner (L17).** The pre-flight's reach test was a horizontal envelope, 0.745 m from the base to every pick, place and stroke point, fitted to one sweep of one family. `arrival/sim/reach.py` replaces that envelope with an IK solve of the expert's own waypoints (over the object, the grasp, the lift, over the target, the target; the wipe strokes, the drawer tray, the dishwasher rack) on an arm-only model of the chosen arm, built once, with each target turned into the base's frame, both half-turned grasps tried as the expert does, and a rim or handle grasp taken where the expert takes it. The stand rules in `preflight.base_pose` stay the first choice for where the base parks; the planner checks that spot and only moves the base when it fails. To set the tolerances, every pool variation of the seven families with twin-derived targets on the hero, rotated and U kitchens was run in physics with its start pushed back 0, 8 and 16 cm on the counter and moved 16 cm either way along it (117 episodes, scripted expert, no people). Successes never had more than 2.2 cm of IK error where the hand touches, and pick and place still succeeded with up to 9.0 cm in free air, where the hand only hovers or carries lower; a task is refused above 2.5 cm at a contact waypoint or 5 cm at a hover one. 5 cm is the cautious side: 10 cm would admit three more `counter_to_sink` successes from this sweep and no extra misses, but three episodes is thin evidence for letting the carry run 10 cm low in a real scan. `put_in_drawer` is the exception: its misses (1.3 and 4.0 cm over the mug) overlap its successes (3.1 to 4.0 cm), so its hover tolerance stays at 1 cm. The tolerances were fitted on these same 117 episodes, so the comparison that follows is in-sample for the IK rule and not for the envelope, which was fitted on an earlier sweep. The envelope accepted 54 successes and 9 misses and rejected 25 successes; the IK rule accepts 73 successes and 13 misses and rejects 6 successes. Every miss it accepts has 0.0 cm of IK error at every waypoint, so none is a reach failure: `counter_to_sink` with the bowl (once on the hero kitchen, twice on the rotated one), the U kitchen's `put_in_drawer` with the mug 16 cm along the counter (twice), and the U kitchen's `load_dishwasher` and `unload_dishwasher` with the mug 16 cm along, 8 cm back, or pushed back 16 cm (four each). The four pushed back 16 cm are the extra accepted misses: the envelope rejected them for being far, and they still miss with the arm reaching every waypoint. When the stand rule's spot fails, `find_shift` tries a fallback grid of base positions up to 0.2 m along the fixture's run and from 0.06 m closer to 0.12 m further out from its face, nearest first, and the variation keeps the first that passes the pre-flight at all four start offsets as `base_shift`, which `base_pose` applies in the fixture's frame (on the far side of a table, out is away from that edge). The stand check used to measure only closed fixture footprints, so a shifted spot could put the base column on a dishwasher door lying open (U kitchen `load_dishwasher` moved 0.2 m toward the dishwasher passed the pre-flight and missed in physics); it now also keeps the base 0.13 m clear of the part the task opens or starts open, a bottom-hinged door lying flat or a pulled-out drawer and its handle, which changes none of the rule spots in the three kitchens and none of the 117 verdicts. Hinged cabinet and fridge doors sweep an arc and are not in that check. From the planner's spot, the 13 sweep variations the rule's spot cannot reach and the grid can were run in physics: 12 succeed, against 3 of the same 13 from the rule's spot (the miss is rotated mug_2 into the east drawer 16 cm along, shifted 6 cm closer). On the rotated kitchen the planner keeps mug_2 into the east drawer, which the envelope dropped: from the rule's spot it is 4/8 in physics (misses at the 0 and +0.08 m offsets, IK 1.3 and 2.6 cm), from 0.1 m along the run 8/8 over the four offsets and two seeds. `collect` on all families, 30 tasks, seed 3, res 32, no people, before and after: hero 30/30 both, 0 skipped; rotated 28/30 both, `skipped_unreachable` 2 to 1; U kitchen 28/30 both, 4 skipped both (storage_b's stand spot is on the sink, a stand check). Pre-flight time for `make_tasks` over every family went from under 10 ms to 0.6 s (hero), 1.2 s (rotated) and 1.1 s (U), about 1% of the 95 to 105 s those 30 episodes take. `--base mobile` checks the place waypoints from the drive spot, `--arm menagerie` plans with the real arm's kinematics, and the arm reaches `make_tasks` through `collect` and the eval harness. Not changed: the stand rules and their hand-tuned offsets; door, drawer, fridge, flap-door and faucet families get only the stand checks, because their hand targets come from the built model; `tasks.REACH` (0.78) and `TABLE_REACH` still pre-filter candidate sinks and table objects before the pre-flight; collisions between the arm and the room are not part of the solve.

**L26: the spatula is pinched, picked into the sink and used to wipe the counter.** The spatula (3.5 x 22 x 1 cm) was scenery because the expert held everything by the body, with the eef 1 mm above the object's centre; the procedural pads reach 23 mm past the eef, so on a 1 cm object their tips went into the counter and stopped there. Reproduced with the old rule on the hero kitchen: `counter_to_sink` 0/4 (seeds 3 and 4), the spatula never rose, and the pads were 1.3 to 1.4 mm into the top. `THIN_HELD` (spatula) is a new grasp class: the pads close across the short side with their tips `PAD_CLEAR` = 3 mm over the top it lies on. How far the pads reach is read from the loaded model (`SimEnv.pad_reach`, the finger collision geoms' bounding boxes along the approach axis): 23.4 mm on the procedural fingers, matching `PAD_BELOW_EEF`, and 10.2 mm on the Menagerie hand, so one rule serves both arms. The spatula is in `GRASPABLE`, so `counter_to_sink` and `counter_to_counter` draw it, and `WIPE_TOOLS` (sponge, spatula) is what `wipe_counter` drags; a spatula scraping the top is held at the pinch height instead of being pressed 4 mm into it. Measured with the scripted expert, two episodes per seed, each with 0 and with 2 people: hero kitchen, procedural arm, seeds 0, 1, 3, 4 and 5 (sizes drawn within 8% by seed): `counter_to_sink` 20/20 and `wipe_counter` 20/20; Menagerie arm, seeds 3 and 4: 8/8 and 8/8; the rotated kitchen (pick on the west run, wipe on the south run), procedural, seeds 3 and 4: 8/8 and 8/8. In the 32 Menagerie and rotated-kitchen episodes, where every step was logged, and in 8 hero episodes measured again separately, both pads touched the spatula on every step the gripper was closed (145 of 145 for the pick, 115 of 115 for the wipe), no finger touched the counter, and while closed the pads stayed 2.5 to 3.0 mm (procedural) and 3.6 to 4.0 mm (Menagerie) above it. The pick lifts the spatula 22 cm. The wipe moves it 1.11 m over the four strokes and it ends at its start x to the millimetre. With the old rule the wipe also passed 2/2, which says the success check cannot tell a good grasp from a bad one: the pads were 3.1 mm into the top and the spatula moved only 0.73 m while the hand, held back by the counter, covered 0.97 m of its 1.2 m stroke, and the spatula ended 2 cm off its line. One test in `tests/test_scene.py`, run for each family and each arm (the Menagerie cases skip without the asset), check the pinch on every closed step, no finger-counter contact, success, a 20 cm lift or 1 m of wiping, and that the procedural pad reach is 23 mm. Not done: no scan has a spatula and `place_default_objects` does not add one, so it appears only when a scan or an authored twin holds one; the spatula is a flat box with no handle or blade and is held at its middle; `counter_to_counter` with a spatula and `--base mobile` were not run; the wipe wordings are the sponge's ("Wipe the counter with the spatula."); the dishwasher families still draw from `PICKABLE`, which does not include it. Seen while measuring and not changed: the existing sponge wipe also puts the pads 2.5 mm into the top, and both pads touch the sponge for 77 of the 115 closed steps.

**G5a: GPU fine-tune files (offline slice).** `Dockerfile.gpu`, `scripts/run_finetune.sh` and the Makefile targets `train`, `eval-policy` and `serve-policy` are written so someone with a GPU host can run a 100-step GR00T N1.7 fine-tune as a Nebius Job. Nothing here has run on a GPU: no GPU, Docker daemon or shellcheck was available, and the only checks are `bash -n` and `make -n` in `tests/test_gpu_files.py`. The script runs `check_dataset_loader.py` first, fails on a missing `DATASET_DIR`, `HF_TOKEN` or GPU (it never prints the token), runs `launch_finetune.py` with the flags from the Isaac-GR00T README example, and writes `finetune_run.json` (command, duration, checkpoint path) next to the checkpoint. Unverified, each marked `UNVERIFIED` in the files: the base image tag `nvidia/cuda:12.8.0-cudnn-devel-ubuntu22.04` and that it suits Isaac-GR00T (its `docker/README.md` was not read); the unpinned Isaac-GR00T clone; installing `arrival` into its uv environment; that `HF_TOKEN` is the variable the Hugging Face loader reads; `--global-batch-size 32` fitting memory with two cameras; checkpoints appearing as `checkpoint-<step>` under `--output-dir` (the `serve-policy` default `checkpoint-100` assumes it). The Nebius Job submission is not written; platform and preset ids must be confirmed in the console. `serve-policy` uses our shim, not the README `run_gr00t_server.py` (port 5555); the shim API calls were already unverified in POL-3.

**G6a: offline layout.yaml check.** `scripts/check_layout.py` (`python scripts/check_layout.py out/twins/<room>` or `--room hero_kitchen`) compares `layout.yaml` with the twin it came from and exits 1 on a defect: frame `z_up` and units meters, full sizes rather than half-extents, every fixture `pos` within 5 cm of the twin and `z_rot` equal to yaw, wall ends and thickness recovered from `pos`/`size`/`z_rot`, the floor covering the walls, and no fixture or wall dropped, duplicated or invented; doors, windows, openings and objects are listed as declared omissions. `tests/test_layout_check.py` runs it on the hero kitchen and both RoomPlan fixtures and checks that each seeded defect is caught. It found no emitter bug; it found two doc errors, now fixed in `docs/04-contracts.md` (the synthesized counter size is `[max(x + 1.2, 1.8), 0.65, 0.92]`, and walls are centred on the scanned line, not shifted inward), and that RoomPlan walls run clockwise while the contract says counterclockwise (reported as a warning; the YAML does not depend on winding). This only checks the YAML against the twin; it does not show that RoboCasa loads the file, and RoboCasa was not run. The RoboCasa schema could not be confirmed offline, so these assumptions come from the repo docs and are untested: absolute `pos`/`size` rows are accepted next to `main_group`, `size` is the full extent, `z_rot` is radians about +Z, `bottom_row` accepts any `type` including `box`, and wall rows with `z_rot` are accepted. Not done: loading in real RoboCasa, `align_to` chains, doors and windows in the YAML.

**Job delete, cancel and progress (L30).** `DELETE /jobs/<id>` works only for the browser whose `arrival_client` cookie hash is the job's `owner`; everyone else, and every unknown id, gets the same 404. A finished job goes through `retention.delete_job`, a queued one through `cancel_job` (200 `cancelled`), a running one gets the cancel marker (202 `cancelling`). The jobs table has Cancel and Delete buttons on the caller's own rows and a note saying whether finished jobs are deleted after `ARRIVAL_RETENTION_HOURS` or kept. There is no separate job page, so these sit in the table. While `sim` runs, the worker wraps the log hook it hands to `collect` and counts the `[sim] <task>: OK|MISS|DROP|SKIP` lines into `status.progress = {done, total}`; the table shows `sim: 3/8 episodes`. Total is the requested episode count, so a run with skipped tasks still ends at total. `ARRIVAL_MAX_JOB_MB` (off by default) sets `RLIMIT_AS` in the child process on Linux only. Tests cover the endpoint, the buttons, the counter and the cap with a fake `resource`; the real-allocation test skips off Linux. The cap was not measured on Linux: RLIMIT_AS counts address space, which for numpy and MuJoCo runs well above resident memory, so a useful value has to be found by trying it.

**C1a: shard fan-out client, offline only.** `arrival/jobs/fanout.py` (and a small `arrival fanout` subcommand) submits the L5 shards through a `Backend` protocol (submit, poll, fetch result, put/get/exists/list object), skips shards that already have a done-marker in storage (written last, after the shard files), retries a failed shard up to three submissions, then downloads the shards and joins them with `merge_shards`. Only `LocalBackend` exists: shards are local subprocesses and a directory stands in for object storage. `NebiusBackendStub` is an untested interface stub that raises. A real Nebius run was not done: there was no Nebius account, so no Serverless Job and no Object Storage call has been made, and the job spec, auth and storage client are unwritten. `tests/test_fanout.py` covers an injected failed shard that is retried and a killed run that resumes, each giving a dataset equal to an uninterrupted fan-out.

**C1: Nebius backend for the fan-out, tested against fakes only.** `arrival/jobs/nebius.py` adds `NebiusBackend` behind the existing `Backend` protocol, and `arrival fanout --backend nebius` uses it. Shards run as Serverless AI Jobs created through the REST API (`POST /ai/v1/jobs`, polled with `GET /ai/v1/jobs/{id}`, stopped with `POST /ai/v1/jobs/cancel`), and the run manifest, shard outputs and done-markers live in an Object Storage bucket reached over the S3 API. Both clients are stdlib (urllib and a short Signature Version 4 signer), so the package gains no dependency; boto3 and the nebius SDK are not installed here. Inside the job, `python -m arrival.jobs.nebius run-shard` runs the shard and uploads it to a staging prefix unique to that attempt; the orchestrator copies it to the shard prefix and only then writes the marker, as `LocalBackend` does. A job state outside the documented enum stops the run with an error naming it. The bucket key reaches jobs only as a MysteryBox reference, and a missing setting is reported by name before any request. `tests/test_nebius.py` runs it against `tests/fake_nebius.py`, an in-process fake of the job API (checks the token and the documented field names, runs each job's command as a local subprocess) and of S3 (verifies every signature, pages listings 3 keys at a time). The signer matches two cases of the AWS signing test suite. With shard 1's first job failed, the run resubmits only shard 1 and merges to the same episode rows and frames as an uninterrupted local fan-out (4 episodes, no duplicate ids); a run killed after shard 0 reruns only shard 1. `arrival fanout --backend nebius` against the fakes took 8.7 s for 2 shards of 4 episodes at 32 px with one injected failure. This has never run against Nebius: there is no account here, so no job was created and no bucket was touched, and the small real run that issue #66 asks for is still to do. The doc pages it follows are listed in docs/REFERENCE.md under Fan-out to Nebius.

**C1 follow-up: shard status records and job time limits.** Issue #66 asks for job ids, resource use and failure stage in the status; the first C1 change kept them in memory for the run only. `arrival/jobs/fanout_status.py` adds `StatusRecorder`, a `Backend` that wraps another and writes `<run>/status/<i>of<n>.json` into that backend's storage next to `run.json`, so the record lives in the bucket for Nebius and in the store directory for local runs; `arrival fanout` uses it for both. Per attempt it keeps the job id, each state change with its time, submit and end times, wall seconds on the orchestrator's clock, the platform, preset, disk and job timeout (none for a local subprocess), and for a failure the stage (submit, run, fetch or marker) and the reason. `run_fanout` is unchanged, and `LocalBackend` only gains a `describe` method the recorder reads. `NebiusBackend.poll` now cancels a job that has not finished 7200 s after it was created (`NEBIUS_MAX_JOB_SECONDS`) or has reported no state for 1800 s in a row (`NEBIUS_MAX_NO_STATE_SECONDS`) and reports it failed, so run_fanout retries it up to the attempt limit; before, such a job was polled until the token expired. The job spec already stops the container at 1 h, so the first limit only catches a job the service failed to end, and a value not above the job timeout is refused. 9 new test cases in `tests/test_nebius.py`: a stuck `RUNNING` job and a job that never reports a state are each cancelled before the next attempt and the shard fails after 2 attempts, on a fake clock so the defaults are what is tested; each failure stage lands in the record, for Nebius and for `LocalBackend`, with the reason redacted; a stop after a job succeeded is recorded at fetch, not as a cancel; a run killed with shard 1 open leaves that attempt `running`, and the rerun marks it `abandoned` and appends the attempt that succeeds; and the staging prefix differs across attempts and across processes, a test that fails when the tag is fixed or is the attempt number. `arrival fanout --backend nebius` against the fakes with shard 0's first job failed: exit 0 in 8.0 s, and shard 0's record shows attempt 1 failed at run with `FAILED: container exited with code 3`, then attempt 2 succeeded in 6.85 s. Neither limit has been checked against Nebius's real timing.

**L7 (rest): demo video regenerated, status table re-checked.** `docs/media/demo.mp4` (2.1 MB), `demo.gif` (5.8 MB) and `poster.png` were re-rendered with `scripts/make_demo_video.py` on top of the merged `feat/demo-site` code; a frame shows the current kitchen (sink, faucet, gas range, slim handles). `plan.pdf` is linked from the README and the docs as the product spec and holds no private data (title, date, a placeholder author, the method). `check_docs.py` and `check_links.py` pass. Spot checks: `validate` on a fresh sim dataset passes; `eval` for pick mug, open drawer is 100%, but open door lost 1 of 4 and 1 of 2 episodes to a walker contact, so the README open-door row now says so instead of claiming 100%.

**G1 offline slice (224 px sim dataset through the loader check, Oct 20).** `python -m arrival sim --room hero_kitchen --families all --episodes 28 --people 1 --res 224 --seed 5 --out out/g1` on a laptop CPU: 28 episodes recorded from 28 attempts (0 dropped, 0 human contacts) over ten families (counter_to_counter is skipped in this room), 5126 frames, 98.7 s in the run (1:39 wall), 5.8 MB on disk (56 mp4s). `arrival validate` printed OK (28 episodes, 5126 frames, 26 tasks, 56 videos, physically_valid=True). `scripts/check_dataset_loader.py` exited 0 with PASS (state and action 11d, cameras ego_view and wrist, 6 mp4s decoded, 420/5126 action windows padded at the tail, relative action range [-1.922, 2.893] rad) and four warnings: `fixed_base_constant` for state dim 10 and for action dim 10 (the parked base pose is constant, so use the arm-and-gripper Panda config) and `stats_no_quantiles` for state and action (the writer stores min/max/mean/std, no q01/q99). `arrival validate --episodes --frames` exited 1 with 5 of 28 episodes flagged, all `joint_at_limit` (close_door episodes 4, 14, 24: joint 2 for 41 to 45 steps, episode 14 also joint 7 for 43; open_oven episodes 8 and 18: joint 5 for 71 steps; threshold 40 steps); no unreadable, non-finite, zero-motion, stuck-arm, length-outlier or blank-video episodes. Frame 0 to end of the first ego_view and wrist mp4 decode with OpenCV as 236 frames of 224x224x3, matching episodes.jsonl. No bug found in the loader check or exporter. This is not a GR00T load: the real loader needs a GPU host.

**OPS-1: bounded automatic retry.** A failed job attempt is queued again up to `ARRIVAL_JOB_RETRIES` times (default 2, `--retries`; `0` = never), except a bad upload, a request over a limit, a room the sim cannot use, a dataset with nothing to ship, or the wall-clock timeout. `claim_next` counts starts in `job_attempts`; `retry_errors` keeps each earlier error and `error` stays null while the job will run again, so a client polling `status.json` never sees `failed` for a job that is not finished. The requeue is written under `claim.lock` and sets `not_before` (`ARRIVAL_JOB_RETRY_BACKOFF` seconds, default 5, doubling per attempt, capped at 300); `claim_next` skips a queued job until then. The usage ledger gets one line per attempt. Tests in `tests/test_jobs_ops.py` cover a transient failure then success, exhaustion, invalid input, a cancel that stops a retry, racing workers, and retry backoff. Left: Nebius is OPS-2.

**L27: walkers yield to the hand, hold the counter pause, and heads are checked against cabinets.** `Walkers.step` takes an optional `clearance(xy, radius)` (collect passes `SafetyMonitor.yield_clearance`, the horizontal clearance from the walker's torso cylinder to the robot hand, fingers and base). Walking speed scales from 0 at 0.25 m to full at 0.50 m (`safety.YIELD`), so a walker slows, stands with the swing fading, and resumes when the hand moves off; at a pause tagged as the counter stop the timer is held while the clearance is under 0.25 m. Without the callback, as with `--people 0`, nothing changes. Measured on the merged feat/demo-site code, `sim --families all --episodes 14 --seed 5 --people 2 --res 64`: 14/14 with 0 human contacts before and after, the same per-family counts. The smallest clearance per episode moved in 5 of 13 listed episodes: put_in_drawer fell from 0.224 to 0.134 m (a walker now stands where it did not before), another rose from 0.171 to 0.300 m; this is not a general improvement. Height: `safety.overhead_clearance` is the 3D distance from head, hair and arm capsules to wall cabinets and the fridge door; a test walks two people with a fridge visit for 15 s and asserts it stays above 0, but the planner still uses footprints (the wall cabinets sit inside the counter footprints, so no path reaches them). Test: `open_fridge` with two walkers at seeds 3 and 5 succeeds with no human or door contact; whether those seeds plan a fridge visit was not asserted. Not done: the mirrored right-hinged fridge plan does not exist in this tree (the scene builds the hinge on the left only), so there is no mirrored test; Yield uses the closest of the base and the arm-link bodies on fixed-base setups, while mobile bases preserve pedestrian drive clearance without mutual deadlock.

**Right-hinged fridge.** A fridge fixture may carry `hinge: "right"` (default `"left"`; RoomPlan data has no such field, so scans are unchanged). `scene.fridge_mirror` gives the sign and `fridge_door_frame` carries it, so the MJCF (hinge body on the right edge, joint axis -z, panel and handle on the other side), the expert's `open_fridge` plan (panel heading `pi + m*q`, mirrored hook and push), the stand spot in `preflight` (beside the free edge on the left), and the walker's visit (stand points, door sector test, `door_point` rotation) all use it; `part_names` is unchanged because q > 0 still opens. Measured: the hero kitchen's MJCF hash (first 16 hex of sha256, base (2.6, 1.0, 0.75), 2 people, seed 3) is c21c9615b58d63ec before and after, and `sim --families all --episodes 14 --seed 5 --people 2 --res 64` gives the same 14 lines before and after (14/14, 0 human contacts). The hero fridge sits on the west wall, so right-hinged there its free edge is in the corner and the pre-flight rejects it (base outside the room); the tests use the hero twin reflected in x (`tests/test_fridge_hinge.py`). There `open_fridge` with the expert succeeds 2/2 with 0 people and with 2 people at seeds 3 and 5, no human or door contact. Those open_fridge runs plan no fridge visit (logged `fridge_visits` 0, on the left hinge too): the robot parks beside the free edge, within CLEAR of the walker's stand point, so the plan declines. The visit is exercised with `open_drawer`, `open_door` and `wipe_counter` on the mirrored twin, 6 episodes at seeds 3 and 5, 2 people: 6/6 each, 3 fridge visits each, 0 human and 0 door contacts. Not done: the fixture yaw is still ignored by the expert's push heading (as before, for left), and no right-hinged fridge in a rotated room was tried.

**L28: walkers keep out of the door the robot opens.** With the `eval` defaults (hero_kitchen, `--people 2`) `open_door` lost episodes to human contact: on `--seed 2 --episodes 8`, `sim_open_door_0003` (`counter_a_door`) ended with the walker standing still at the counter stop (2.32, 1.37) while the hand, pulling the free edge of the door, closed from 0.24 m to 0 m on it at t=7.15 s (min distance -0.054). The L27 yield only stops the walker; it stood on the door's swing, so the hand walked into it. The stop, free points and via points of every walker except the fridge visitor now avoid a disc around the hinge of the task's `<fixture>_door` (panel width + 0.3 m, `Walkers(keep=...)`, built in `SimEnv._door_keepout`; a stop inside it is resampled further along the counter). Measured, `eval --families open_door --episodes 8 --people 2`: seeds 0, 1, 2, 3 were 8/8, 8/8, 7/8, 8/8 with minimum human distance 0.061, 0.107, -0.054, 0.039 m, and are 8/8 on all four at 0.273, 0.277, 0.316, 0.281 m. `sim --families open_door --episodes 8 --people 2 --seed 5 --res 64`: 8/8, 0 contacts both times, minimum distance 0.037 m before, 0.295 m after. Regression `eval --families all --episodes 14 --seed 5 --people 2`: 139/140 before (open_door 13/14, one contact), 140/140 after, 0 contacts. The README's 3/4 (seed 0, 4 episodes) and 1/2 (seed 5, 2 episodes) re-checks did not reproduce on this branch: seed 0 and seed 1 at 4 episodes were 4/4 before the change. Not done: the hand can still reach a walker who stops at the edge of the disc; fridge-visit walkers are not kept out (they stand at the fridge, not the counter); only hinged `*_door` fixtures get a keep-out, drawers do not.


Joint-at-limit episodes in the 28-episode hero_kitchen run (`sim --room hero_kitchen --families all --episodes 28 --people 1 --seed 5`, res 64 and 224): `validate --episodes` flagged 5, close_door 4, 14, 24 (joint 2 on its -1.76 stop for 45, 41, 45 steps; episode 14 also joint 7 on +2.90) and open_oven 8, 18 (joint 5 on its +-2.90 stop for 71 steps). Cause: the oven grasp points the hand straight into the panel, and from HOME the IK settles on the wrist branch with joint 5 at the stop; the target is at the edge of what the arm reaches from the stand spot (position error 0.013 m at best, 0.06 to 0.1 m for every other tilt, seed or base shift tried), and the plan interpolates in joint space so the joint stays on the stop through the whole pull. A 4% margin in the IK clip instead broke open_fridge and close_drawer (the fridge grasp needs the stops), so it was dropped.
