Fix (arrival/sim/policy.py): `ScriptedExpert.reset` measures how long the plan sits within the quality check's 3% band (`_limit_run`) and, when that is over `LIMIT_PLAN_STEPS` (38, the check flags at 40), re-plans with a grasp yaw about the vertical (up to 0.5 rad), a wrist roll about the tool axis (up to 0.4 rad) and either jaw heading, smallest change first, taking the first plan that reaches as well and clears 30 steps. Plans already under 38 are untouched, so the other families run as before. Oven: yaw 0.5 rad gives position error 0.0 and no step on a stop. Measured same seed and flags: before, 28/28 successes (counter_to_sink 3/3, open_drawer 3/3, open_door 3/3, close_drawer 3/3, close_door 3/3, put_in_drawer 3/3, wipe_counter 3/3, open_fridge 3/3, open_oven 2/2, turn_on_faucet 2/2) and 5 flagged; after, the same 28/28 per family and 3 flagged (close_door 4, 14, 24). Test: tests/test_joint_limits.py. Not done: close_door is excluded from the re-plan and stays flagged. Its first plan parks joint 2 (and joint 7 on counter_a) on a stop for 41 to 45 steps; the other jaw heading clears it in the sim run but puts a finger 2.5 cm into the door front (tests/test_sim.py::test_arm_stays_out_of_drawn_fronts), and every yaw, roll or tilt change that clears the stops misses the handle on counter_b (the door stays at 1.67 rad). Base shifts of 0.03 to 0.15 m along or out from the counter left the stretch at 40 to 50 steps. A fix needs the push contact checked in physics while choosing the pose, or a different close_door approach, neither of which is in this change.

**L25 follow-up: Menagerie close_door retuned (Oct 20).** With the real Franka (`--arm menagerie --families all --episodes 14 --seed 5 --people 0 --res 64`) the run before this change recorded 13/14 (per family counter_to_sink 2/2, open_drawer 2/2, open_door 2/2, close_drawer 2/2, close_door 0/1, put_in_drawer 1/1, wipe_counter 1/1, open_fridge 1/1, open_oven 1/1, turn_on_faucet 1/1): open_door already passed, close_door left the counter_b door at 0.45 rad. The push stood 3 cm from the bar as for the procedural fingers, but the closed real fingertips reach 15 mm less, so `HAND` (robot.py and menagerie.py) gains `door_push` (0.03 procedural, 0.015 Menagerie) and `door_z` (0.04 both) which the close_door plan reads; after it the same run is 14/14 with every family complete, close_door also 4/4 and open_door 4/4 on seeds 5, 6 and 7 (8 episodes each). The procedural 14-episode seed-5 result lines are identical before and after. Only one 14-episode seed was measured, so wider Menagerie coverage (people, other rooms, other seeds for the other families) is still unmeasured.

**Wider stand-spot search for corner fixtures (fixed base).** Before, u_kitchen_42 rejected four tasks (open and close of the drawer and the door on storage_b, whose rule spot sits on the sink run) and rotated_kitchen rejected open_fridge (the free-edge spot is 0.02 m outside the north wall); every other family and fixture on both was accepted. `reach.find_shift` now has a second grid, `WIDE_SHIFTS` (0.05 m steps up to 1 m along the fixture run, out from -0.2 to +0.3 m), tried only after the old SHIFTS fail and only for the IK families and open_door/close_door; room-wall and fixture clearances are the same `check_task`. Result: storage_b open_door and close_door now stand 0.25 m along the run and run end to end, 10 of 10 in two sim runs (u_kitchen_42, seeds 5 and 7, doors only; the other doors and drawers on that fixture unchanged, 33/40 over the four door and drawer families). storage_b open_drawer and close_drawer stay rejected: the drawer is pulled from in front of the sink, and over 30 stand spots the expert opened it 0 times. rotated_kitchen open_fridge stays rejected: the hook-and-push plan needs the free-edge side, and from the hinge side only one of eight neighbouring spots (-0.30 m, +0.20 m) succeeded, so it is not a usable spot and the family is not widened. Hero kitchen `sim --episodes 14 --seed 5 --people 2` log lines are byte-identical before and after. Tests: the corner-cabinet test in test_scan_fixtures.py, and two skip counts in test_any_wall.py that dropped from 2 to 1. Not done: a reach check for drawers and the fridge in the planner, a mirrored fridge plan.

**P3: twenty timed human demonstrations.** One operator recorded 20 successful `counter_to_sink` demos in the hero kitchen at L20 revision `db26a01`, seed 0, no people, 128 px ego and wrist cameras. All 26 attempts count toward 1230.359 operator seconds. At the declared USD 2/hour and zero setup cost, the cost basis is USD 0.6835; these are assumptions, not invoices. The portable baseline, unchanged attempt log, capture arguments, archive hashes and results are in [the P3 report](results/p3-teleop-2026-10-21/README.md). The separate ZIP contains 20 episodes, 22669 frames and 40 videos; every extracted file matches its checksum and basic validation passes. The optional episode/frame check flags episode 0 for length (13406 steps, 674.028 operator seconds); it remains in the dataset and totals. The [dataset archive](https://github.com/Aadi-Tiwari/ExeaLabsNvidiaHacks/releases/download/p3-teleop-2026-10-21/p3-teleop-2026-10-21.zip) is published on the contributor fork. **Hand checks: browser upload and job delete.** The laptop hand checks in `LOCAL-README.md` were run on `main` (b4f3e61). Fresh clone with Python 3.11: `make install` 17 s; `make test` 1096 passed, 13 skipped in 23 min (other sims shared the CPU, so the time is an upper bound); `make demo` exit 0 in 40 s. Two `arrival work` processes on one queue with three uploads of the hero scan: every job claimed exactly once, split across both workers, three distinct rooms and valid zips. `sim --shard 0/2` killed (`kill -9`) after its first journalled task, `--resume` kept that episode and ran the other three, `merge-shards` with shard 1 validated, passed the loader check, and its state and action arrays are identical to one unsharded run of the same 8 tasks. The job page's buttons were clicked in headless Chromium against a real `arrival serve` and `arrival work`. That found two bugs. **Upload from the page was broken:** a browser always sends the optional `frames` file input, left empty, as a part with `filename=""` and no bytes, and the server answered `400 frames file is empty`, so no scan could be queued from the form. The tests post with `http.client` and never send that part. The server now treats a nameless, empty `frames` part as absent; a named empty file is still a 400. **Delete left the sim progress journal behind:** `retention.job_paths` now includes `out/progress/<room>_sim.jsonl`, so `DELETE /jobs/<id>` and `arrival gc` remove it with the twin, dataset and report. After both fixes, in the browser: Cancel on a queued job (cancelled at once, the row switches to Delete), Cancel on a running job (the worker kills the child, the row shows cancelled), Delete on cancelled and finished jobs (the confirm dialog appears, the row leaves the table, nothing of the job is left on disk except the usage ledger, which is kept on purpose), and the page logs no console or CSP errors. Docker is not installed on this machine, so the compose and smoke-test check was not run. Tests: `test_frames_input_left_empty_by_a_browser_is_ignored` and the progress journal in the `gc` and `delete_job` tests; both fail on the old code.

**q01/q99 in stats.json (G1 prep).** Every `stats.json` this repo writes had only min, max, mean and std, and the loader check warned `stats_no_quantiles`. GR00T's state/action processor reads `stats["q01"]` and `stats["q99"]` when it normalises with percentiles (`gr00t/data/state_action/state_action_processor.py`), and its loader only asserts that `meta/stats.json` exists, so training straight on our folder would fail on those keys unless someone first ran `gr00t/data/stats.py`. `arrival/export/lerobot.py` now has `dataset_stats`: the per-episode stats merged as before, plus q01/q99 per dimension for every column, computed over all frames read back from the data parquet files (quantiles cannot be merged from per-episode rows). The sim writer (and so `merge-shards`), the placeholder writer and `validate --drop` all use it. We write no `__fingerprints__` key, so GR00T's `generate_stats` still treats the file as stale and recomputes it if it is run; that is harmless. The loader check still warns on an old dataset without quantiles and now compares q01/q99 with its own recompute when present. Tests: a fresh sim dataset has all six keys on every column with min ≤ q01 ≤ q99 ≤ max, no `stats_no_quantiles` warning and q99 equal to the loader check's recompute; a shifted q01 is `stats_mismatch`; a `--drop` copy's state quantiles equal `np.quantile` over its parquet. Not checked: a real GR00T load (needs the GPU host).

**CAP-2: RGB-D recorder in the iOS app (#58).** The capture app now records a CAP-2a frame bundle during a RoomPlan scan. `FrameRecorder.swift` reads `RoomCaptureSession.arSession.currentFrame` every 0.5 s (it does not take the session's delegate, so RoomPlan's own session is untouched) and keeps a frame when tracking is normal, `sceneDepth` (or `smoothedSceneDepth`) is present and the camera has moved 0.15 m or turned 15 degrees since the last kept frame, up to 300. It copies depth, confidence, intrinsics and the pose out of the ARFrame at once and encodes the JPEG on a background queue, skipping a sample while the last is still being written. `FrameBundle.swift` (Foundation, CoreGraphics and ImageIO only) writes the files and `frames_manifest.json`: 1920x1440 JPEG, float32 `.npy` depth in metres, 8-bit confidence PNG, `camera_to_room` = the Y-up to Z-up swap times ARKit's camera-to-world, `arkit_world_to_room` = that swap, every fifth frame held out. The worker's realism stage then maps this frame onto the twin with the scan's own `arkit_world_to_room`, so the phone does not need to know how `roomplan.py` squares the walls. A store-only `ZipWriter` puts the manifest at the zip root, the layout `POST /jobs` wants. The scan keeps `frames/`, the share sheet gets `frames.zip`, and the upload sends it as the `frames` part, streaming the multipart body from a temporary file. A toggle turns recording off, and the new-scan footer states the consent and sanitising rule. Checked: `xcodebuild` for generic iOS builds with no errors or warnings; `tests/test_ios_bundle.py` (macOS with `swiftc` only, skipped elsewhere) compiles the writer and `ArrivalAPI.swift` with `ios/BundleCheck/main.swift`. With six synthetic frames plus one repeated timestamp and one NaN pose, it checks that the two bad frames are skipped; the zip passes `frames.extract` and `frames.validate` with one held-out frame; depth and confidence come back exact and `camera_to_room` matches the pose; every unprojected point lands on the synthetic floor or wall within 0.1 mm; and the app's two-part upload body with a real RoomPlan export queues a job with the frames stored. Not checked: nothing has run on a phone. Whether RoomPlan's session delivers scene depth, whether RoomPlan's CapturedRoom and the ARSession share a world origin (the alignment assumes it), the JPEG colour and orientation from `capturedImage`, and the memory and heat of recording during a scan are all unmeasured. Field test is P1.

**TWN-3 leftover: doors, windows and openings in the sim.** They reached `twin.json` (with `wall_id` and `is_open`) but not the scene. `arrival.sim.scene.portals_xml` now draws each one on its wall as visual-only geoms on the world body (`contype` and `conaffinity` 0, group 2), named `door<k>_<part>`, `window<k>_<part>` and `opening<k>_<part>`: a closed door is a frame, a wood leaf and a knob; an open door or an opening is a frame around a dark recess; a window is a frame, a sill, a pane and a mullion. The wall is the one `wall_id` names, else the nearest within 0.25 m, because exports from before iOS 17 have no `parentIdentifier` (amal with its parent ids deleted draws the same geoms). Every box runs through the wall and stands 2 mm to 3 cm proud of both faces, so nothing has to work out which side is the room. An open door's leaf is not drawn: RoomPlan gives no hinge side, and a leaf swung into the room would be a visual obstacle the arm and the walkers pass through. Checked: rooms without portals build byte-identical MJCF (hero_kitchen from `scan.json` and `roomplan.json`, rotated_kitchen and u_kitchen_42, with and without people, fixed and mobile base; amal and shopyseek with their portals removed). On amal, qpos and qvel stay bit-identical over 400 steps of random arm targets with a cup dropped through the door knob, and they diverge when only the portal geoms are made to collide (checked by hand). At 96 x 128, 7.4% of the amal overview's pixels change. Not done: the wall stays solid behind a doorway, so walkers and the mobile base cannot pass; doors and windows are still not in `layout.yaml`; no kitchen scan with a window or a door next to a counter has been seen.

**Stand spots off joint 5's stop and off the east wall: open_oven to the side of the bar, open_door further out by a wall.** PR #151 found that the G1 run's open_oven flags and `counter_b`'s wall graze come from where the base stands, not from the hand plan. Measured here with the G1 command at 32 px (`sim --families all --episodes 28 --people 1 --seed 5`, then `validate --episodes`) and with every family on the three kitchens (hero, rotated, u_kitchen_42; 30 tasks each, seed 7, two people). open_oven: straight in front of the bar, joint 5 sits on its stop for 71 steps in all three kitchens. A sweep of the stand spot along the run gave the same result in each: 0.15 m to either side still holds joint 5 there for 57 to 73 steps, and 0.20 to 0.30 m keeps every joint out of the validator's 3% band with 0 IK error. `preflight.OVEN_ALONG` (0.25) stands the base there, still in front of the door: on the run's +x side, else the -x side, else straight in front as before, the first spot that passes the stand check. With the hero stove and `counter_b` swapped so the stove's +x end is 1 cm from the east wall, the base stands on the -x side and the episode clears with no joint at a stop. Over 1,500 fuzzed rooms plus the named and adversarial layouts, fixed and mobile base, no room loses open_oven against main and 26 gain it (main's straight-in-front spot failed the stand check there; those were not run in physics). open_dishwasher keeps its spot. open_door on `counter_b`: the door is hinged 1 cm from the east wall and link7 grazed it for 22 steps (0.9 mm) every episode. In a 20-spot grid (along the run -0.1 to 0.1 m, out -0.06 to 0.12 m) the wall is clear only from 0.12 m further out, or 0.06 m out and 0.05 to 0.1 m nearer the hinge; 0.06 m straight out still grazes for 15 steps. 0.12 m straight out opens the door to 65.6° (65.9° before). A door hinged within `WALL_CLEAR` of a wall now stands `OPEN_DOOR_WALL_OUT` (0.12) further out; in the three kitchens that is only `counter_b`. The 0.15 m trigger is wider than the one wall it was measured on: in the fuzzed rooms it also moves 10 open_door spots, where main's spot may not graze; review runs found the door still opens there. Halving the hand's 15° tilt also cleared the wall, but left `counter_a`'s door at 60.3°, so it was dropped. The stand spots of 405 tasks per tree (seeds 0, 5 and 7, every family, three kitchens) are bit-identical to main except open_oven and `counter_b` open_door. G1 run: main 5 of 28 flagged, with this change 3 (the close_door joint 2 flags #151 fixes); main plus #151 3, with this change 1 (`counter_a` close_door, joint 7 for 43 steps). 28/28 succeed in all four. Three kitchens: successes unchanged (30/30, 27/30, 28/30), human contacts unchanged (one walker contact on a hero open_door, the same episode before and after), flagged episodes 6, 6, 10 to 3, 3, 8 on main and 5, 4, 9 to 2, 1, 7 with #151; only the eight open_oven episodes and the `counter_b` open_door episode differ. Not fixed, because it breaks something without #151: close_door on `counter_b` still grazes the east wall (29 steps, 2.0 mm on main, 1.5 mm with #151). With #151's push, every spot in a 16-spot grid either grazes or misses; the 10° hand tilt leans the wrist toward the wall, and 5° clears it only with #151's 10 cm push (on main the 4 cm push at 5° leaves the door at 1.70 rad, a miss). `counter_a` close_door keeps joint 7 at its stop for 43 steps: standing at most 0.45 m along the run from the open handle brings it to 35, but with main's push the hand then runs 2.5 cm into the drawn front at the spots that get joint 7 under 40 (`test_arm_stays_out_of_drawn_fronts` allows 1 cm). On top of #151 the two changes give 0 of 28 flagged in the G1 run, 0 flagged in the hero and rotated kitchens and 0 wall contacts for `counter_b` close_door, and the door and oven tests pass, so they wait for it. Tests: `test_open_oven_stand_keeps_every_joint_off_its_stop` runs the episode through the validator's `check_trace`, `test_open_oven_stands_on_the_other_side_when_one_side_is_against_a_wall` covers the swapped layout, and `test_door_pull_keeps_the_wrist_off_the_wall_beside_the_hinge` counts link-to-wall contacts on `counter_b`; both fail with the old spots. On Avneh's feat/gap-fixes, whose re-plan already keeps open_oven off joint 5, the G1 run is 0 of 28 flagged with and without this change, so the oven rule adds nothing there; `counter_b` open_door still grazes the wall for 22 steps on that branch and stands clear of it with this change. Not measured: the menagerie arm (its asset is not fetched on this machine), whose open_oven also stands at the new spot.

**C4: objects from posed RGB-D frames, offline slice (TWN-5, #60).** `arrival/twin/detect.py` reads the train frames of an uploaded CAP-2a bundle (at most 12; held-out frames stay with the realism comparison) and asks Nemotron Omni through the existing Token Factory client (role `omni`, temperature 0) for catalog objects, each as a label, a pixel box and a confidence. Each box is back-projected with `rgbd.unproject` from the frame's depth, intrinsics and pose, mapped into the twin the way the realism stage maps it. For each counter or table, the box's points over its footprint at object height are taken, less those within 3 cm of a wall's face in the twin and those on the back 2.5 cm of a counter or RoomPlan storage worktop, where its backsplash stands, and split into groups connected on a 2 cm grid. A group counts only if it could be the object standing on that top: no wider than the catalog object plus 4 cm, its lowest point within half the object's height (at least 3 cm) of the top, at least 40% of the object's height tall, and with depth in x and y (a wall seen face on is a line there). The group whose centre projects nearest the box centre gives x and y, moved away from the camera by pi/4 of the radius because only the near side of a solid object is seen (a bowl or plate shows its far inner wall too, so it is not moved); z sits the catalog-size object on the top. The object's footprint, a circle of its smaller half-width, must then lie over the top to within 1 cm, clear of walls, and clear of any other fixture it would stand in, a sink with the 10 cm behind it where the faucet stands included. Two boxes of one label in one frame that land within the object's footprint of each other are one object. A hit joins the nearest object of its label within 0.10 m seen in other frames, never one from its own frame, and a merged object with no counter or table under it, or whose averaged position fails the footprint check, is not placed. Every box that is not placed is listed in `twin.json` `object_detection.rejected` with its reason (`unsupported_label`, `low_confidence`, `bad_box`, `no_depth`, `no_support`, `nothing_above_top`, `too_wide`, `not_standing`, `flat_surface`, `off_top`, `against_wall`, `on_fixture`, `same_object`). `compile.fill_objects` uses the detections when there are any and otherwise calls `place_default_objects` as before (`default_synthetic`). The worker's `objects` stage calls it when the scan lists no objects, and the job status gains `objects` (counts per provenance label, detection state and reason), which the jobs table shows as a chip. Without credentials, or with an unreachable endpoint or an unusable reply, a frame is not read and the state is `not_run`; nothing is guessed. Fixtures: `scripts/make_detection_fixture.py` renders hero_kitchen's twin (objects removed) from three poses at 128 x 96, once with one mug at (3.2, 0.45, 0.97) and once without (93 KB in all). `tests/fixtures/detection/replies.json` holds hand-written replies keyed by image hash: the mug's rendered pixel extent in each frame, and nothing for the empty room. They are not model output; the model has never been called for this feature. On the mug fixture the merged mug lands 1.31 cm from the true centre in x and y and 0 cm off in z, the same with the boxes padded by 0, 2, 3, 4, 8, 20 or 40 px (1.67 cm without the near-side correction). The empty fixture places nothing, and the worker falls back to default objects. Four review rounds with re-rendered scenes found faults that were reproduced and then fixed: a box with no counter under it placed at the nearest surface; one frame's two hits merged, so mugs 11 or 12 cm apart along the view became one object 5.5 cm off; every point at mug height kept, so a 3 px pad took in the backsplash and moved mugs 6 to 7 cm and an 8 px pad invented a second mug; support checked at the median of all the box's points and the largest narrow group kept, so an empty room got a mug over the stove, objects behind the backsplash, two books on the wall, and two loosely boxed mugs became four; and only the centre checked against the top, so the L where two faces meet (a table in the room's corner, the east wall and the backsplash, the fridge side and the backsplash) placed a mug in 6 to 11 of 21 cases each, and the faucet was placed as a mug, cup, bottle, book, apple or remote in 25 of 84 cases. Rendered probes on the current code, scored one-to-one: 609 empty-room runs over 29 regions (faucet, sink basin and rims, cooktop and stove edges, cabinet fronts, wall, backsplash and fridge corners, a corner table, a table edge) place nothing. Of 112 counter runs (8 spots, 7 labels, 0 or 5 px of padding) 96 are within 3 cm, the worst a book at 2.8 cm, and a lone bowl 0.6 to 0.7 cm in the open and 2.6 cm 7 cm from the east wall (it was 3.8 to 4.7 cm with the near-side shift); all 7 labels by the backsplash and by the east wall, which round 3 missed at 5 px, are placed; of the other 16, 14 are objects over the basin opening, rejected as `on_fixture`, and 2 a bowl cut into the backsplash, placed 4.1 cm forward. Of 40 table runs 30 are within 1.8 cm; the other 10 are at a spot whose probe cameras stand behind the room's south wall and never see the object. Mugs touching the backsplash are placed 1.5 to 1.6 cm off with 0 to 8 px of padding, two mugs 16 cm apart boxed with 20 px each give two objects 1.3 and 1.4 cm off, two mugs 11 or 12 cm apart along the view two objects within 1.7 cm, a mug and a bowl touching 1.7 to 2.3 and 0.7 to 0.9 cm, 30 or 70% of depth missing in the box 1.2 to 1.3 cm, a mug cut by the image edge 1.2 to 1.7 cm. Limits, measured: two mugs touching along the view (8 cm apart) give one object 3.6 cm from one mug and nothing for the other, while touching across the view they give two, 1.5 and 1.7 cm off. Two same-label objects 9 cm apart that are each seen only in different frames merge into one. An object is not placed where its footprint would reach the sink, or within 10 cm behind it where the faucet stands; in the probes every object at (1.75, 0.2), over the basin opening, is rejected (14 of 14); the 10 cm strip behind a sink costs little usable counter here, since only 6.3 cm of top lies between the backsplash face and the basin opening, but a dish inside a basin is never placed. A bowl whose footprint cuts into the backsplash (back edge at y 0.06 against a face at 0.087) is placed 4.1 cm forward. Frames whose poses do not line up with the twin place nothing (`no_support`). Geometry cannot catch a wrong label of similar shape: a real bottle boxed as a mug or cup is placed under 0.8 cm off in x and y but 6.0 to 6.5 cm low in z (9.8 cm as a book), a mug boxed as a bottle 6.0 cm high, a sponge boxed as a book 1.7 cm off; a bowl or plate boxed as a mug, cup or book, and a sponge boxed as a mug or cup, are rejected. The boxes are hand-placed or taken from the renders and the depth is clean render depth, so this measures back-projection and merging, not detection; real ARKit depth noise could defeat the depth-in-x-and-y test, which separates a mug's 4 cm arc from a face-on wall's 0 cm. Yaw is always 0, the size is the catalog's nominal size, and the worker keeps no reply cache. A fifth review found three gaps, now closed. The backsplash strip skipped RoomPlan storage worktops, which the scene builds with a backsplash like a counter: a mug 1 cm in front of u_kitchen_42's storage backsplash, boxed with 6 px of padding, was not placed, and now is. The merged position was not checked again, so two clear views of a cup beside and in front of the sink averaged onto the sink's corner; the merged position now goes through the same footprint check, and a failure rejects every hit with its reason. No test covered an object next to a wall or backsplash: a mug, a bottle and a bowl 1 and 3 cm in front of the backsplash, the east wall, their corner and the storage backsplash are now placed within 3 cm with 0 or 6 px of padding. The wall strip and `against_wall` stay measured from the twin's 2 cm wall, not the 5 cm wall the scene draws on the same centre line, so against a rendered wall the strip reaches 1.5 cm in front of the face. Measuring from the drawn face was tried: it placed nothing more in the empty sweeps and moved a bowl 0.5 cm from the rotated kitchen's east wall from within 3 cm to 3.08 cm, and in a real scan, where the twin's wall is the scanned surface, it would reject a mug touching the wall. The scene also draws a backsplash on an island; the strip skips islands because a real one has none, and applying it moved a probe bowl on the island to 3.08 cm. The sweep over all three kitchens gives the same placements before and after: 1239 empty-room runs place nothing, 291 of 294 counter runs and 30 of 40 table runs land within 3 cm. Tests: 37 in `tests/test_detect.py`.

**DAT-2 and G1: GR00T fine-tunes, a diagnosis, and a DART data-quality problem.** GR00T N1.7-3B (Isaac-GR00T `dac54b3`) was fine-tuned on `hero_kitchen_sim` at 224 px on one 96 GB GPU with the DAT-1 config (`configs/gr00t/arrival_panda_config.py`): stats finite, a 100-step smoke run in 58 s, then 3,200 to 6,000 steps at batch 32 (1.4 steps/s, 66 GB, `--tune-visual --state-dropout-prob 0.1`). Scores are in `docs/07-eval.md`. The diagnosis: open-loop error on expert frames is 0.3 cm at the next step, but closed loop closes the gripper 2 to 8 cm from the mug, so the loss is compounding drift, not perception. DART (`sim --dart`, #162) trains on perturbed states with the clean target as the label; it lifted counter_to_sink (6/24 to 13/24) and cut open_drawer (20/24 to 9/24, p = 0.001). Cause, measured on the scripted expert: the clean expert ends every drawer pull at 0.2997 m, and with 0.01 rad of noise 30% of the kept demos end under 0.28 m (the bar is 0.15 m), so the physics check keeps marginal pulls and the policy learns to under-pull. `arrival subset --min-drawer-opening 0.28` drops them (646 to 544 episodes). Retraining on the clean data plus the filtered DART data (run 4, same recipe) scored 39/48 (counter_to_sink 18/24, open_drawer 21/24) against 26/48 for the clean-data run (paired p = 0.007) and 22/48 for the unfiltered DART run (p < 0.001); drawers recovered and the grasp gain held. Scoring practice that came out of this: use paired trials (`eval --manifest`), one policy server per client, and never score on the training GPU. Not done: the base-GR00T and generic-RoboCasa cells (DAT-3), held-out wordings, MimicGen.


**close_door next to a wall, the U kitchen's sink cabinet, and the elbow on narrow doors (on feat/gap-fixes).** After #152 (the push from the side to 40°, then from in front), `counter_b` close_door in the hero kitchen still put link6 on `wall_east` for 27 steps, 2.0 mm deep, on the approach to the side push: the door is hinged at that wall, and at its 70° start the outer face is turned toward it, so the hand starts the push between the panel and the wall. The 5° hand tilt that cleared it on main with #151's push does not carry over: with the side push tilted 7.5°, 5°, 2.5° or 0° the hand misses the bar and the door ends at 1.76 to 1.79 rad. Moving the stand spot does. Over a grid of -0.1 to +0.1 m along the run and -0.04 to +0.12 m out, the side push is narrow (0.12 m out, the open_door offset of #159, leaves the door at 1.74 rad), but 0.06 m toward the hinge closes the door with no wall or panel contact and no joint near a stop from 4 cm nearer the counter to 2 cm further out, and so does 0.07 m from 2 cm nearer to 2 cm further out; 4 cm further out misses the bar. `preflight._stand_rule` now stands close_door `CLOSE_DOOR_WALL_ALONG` (0.06 m) nearer the hinge only where the hand is squeezed: the run ends flush against a wall square to it (hinge edge within 5 mm of the wall face, `CLOSE_DOOR_FLUSH`) and the handle, at the 70° start, is within 0.13 m of that wall (`CLOSE_DOOR_ROOM`), and only if the nearer spot passes the stand check. `counter_b` has its hinge edge 1 cm inside the wall face and the handle 0.117 m from it. Slid along its wall, the usual spot still grazes with the hinge at 0 (handle 0.127 m) and neither spot does at 1 cm (0.137 m). A first version moved close_door whenever the hinge was within `WALL_CLEAR` (0.15 m) of a wall, and in fuzzed rooms that lost doors the usual spot closes: 0.46 m doors hinged 0.10 to 0.16 m from a wall (counter_b cut to 1.0 m, hinged 0.10 m from the wall, is left at 0.09 rad), 0.16 m doors hinged 0.11 m from it, a 0.85 m door flush with it, and a counter skewed 14° to the wall. With the narrow rule, over fuzzed and synthetic rooms built with the helpers in tests/test_scan_fuzz.py (10,875 cabinets), 232 close_door tasks change stand spot and none fails the stand check; in physics 61 close on both spots, 8 close only from the nearer spot and 0 only from the usual one, and none of the 232 touches a wall from either spot: the graze has only been seen on the hero kitchen's `counter_b`. In the three test kitchens the rule fires only on `counter_b`. Measured over all families, 30 tasks each in the hero, rotated and u_kitchen_42 kitchens (seed 7, 2 people): 30/30, 27/30 and 29/30 before and after, the same 0, 0 and 2 flagged; 89 of the 90 episodes have bit-identical states and actions, and the one that changed is `counter_b` close_door, wall contact 27 steps to 0 and panel contact 9 steps to 0. `sim --families all --episodes 28 --people 1 --res 32 --seed 5` then `validate --episodes`: 28/28 and 0 flagged before and after; 26 episode parquet files are identical, the two that differ are the `counter_b` close_door episodes. The u_kitchen_42 `storage_sink` close_door misses seen on main need no change here: on main the hand meets the open panel on its way in (step 13) and throws the door to 1.887 rad, by its 1.9 rad stop, on all three seeds tried, and on this branch #152's side push closes it to 0.00 rad on the same seeds and in 22 of 22 `storage_sink` tasks over seeds 0, 1, 2, 3, 5 and 7 with 0 and 2 people. Not changed: on the 0.46 to 0.56 m doors (rotated `counter_s` and `counter_e`, u_kitchen_42 `storage_sink` and `storage_a`) open_door rests link4 on the top edge of the panel near its free edge from about 69° to the lift, 17 to 24 steps, 0.4 mm at most. A 70° pull instead of 80° removes it on those four, but ends the doors at 63 to 67° instead of 63 to 75°, adds 4 steps on `counter_a`, which has none now, and changes every open_door episode, so it is left as it is. With the Menagerie arm, `counter_b` close_door succeeds but grazes the wall for 59 steps (7.2 mm) before this change and 47 (7.3 mm) after; that is not fixed. Tests: `test_close_door_keeps_the_wrist_off_the_wall_beside_the_hinge` and `test_close_door_keeps_its_spot_when_the_handle_has_room` (tests/test_sim.py), `test_u_shape_sink_cabinet_door_closes` (tests/test_any_wall.py).


**Ego/exocentric capture.** `sim --exocentric` exports the existing room-wide `overview` camera alongside ego and wrist at each pre-action observation, using one rollout and the same timestamps. The default remains two views. Camera roles are recorded in dataset metadata; camera selection is included in provenance and the resume fingerprint (old journals default to ego/wrist), and shard merge retains the declared cameras. The writer checks every required camera's frame count, resolution and dtype before writing episode files. `--save-preview` adds a labeled ego/exo MP4 and PNG built from the dataset's own observation arrays. An optional fixed-base GR00T modality config reads all three views with the existing arm/gripper semantics. Tests cover equal observation state during rendering, equal per-camera frame counts, bit-identical original videos and parquet with the extra camera enabled, rejection before writes, resume protection, legacy journals, shard merge and offline config/schema agreement. This adds no learned policy, three-view policy-server support, GPU/cloud execution or measured training benefit.

**open_door: stand further out where the run is drawn into a wall.** On `counter_b` the hinge end of the run sits 1 cm inside the east wall's face, and the pull ends with the outer face of the door turned to that wall and the hand between them: from the usual spot link7 grazes `wall_east` for 22 steps (0.9 mm deep) on main, as it did on feat/gap-fixes. `base_pose` now moves open_door 0.12 m further out when three things hold, all read from the twin: the run's hinge end is at least 5 mm inside the face of a wall square to the run (within 5 deg), the handle at the 80 deg the pull ends at is within 0.06 m of that wall, and the further spot passes the stand check; otherwise the base keeps main's spot. On `counter_b` the wall contact goes to 0 steps (0 people, and 2 people at seed 5) and the door ends at 65.6 deg (65.9 on main). An earlier rule, hinge within 0.15 m of a wall, was too broad: over the verifier's fuzzed and synthetic rooms the further spot left one door that stops flush with its wall at 1.024 rad, under the 60 deg bar (1.067 from main's spot), and main's spot had not grazed there; no door that opens from a run flush with a wall grazed it in the measured rooms. With the narrow rule, 10,875 fuzzed cabinets give the same stand check result as main for every one (286 moved), and 44 of their 46 deduplicated layouts run in physics with no task lost or gained (the other 2 fail to build on main too). The rule does not help most of those rooms: link-to-wall steps fall from 1,541 to 899 overall, but of the 15 doors that open, only one loses its graze and 8 graze 1 to 8 steps longer; a run drawn 5 cm into a wall grazes about 50 steps from either spot. In the hero, rotated and u_kitchen_42 kitchens, 270 tasks over every family (seeds 0, 5 and 7) keep main's stand spot except the 2 `counter_b` open_door tasks, and 24 door, drawer and sink episodes at seed 7 with 2 people are bit-identical to main. The fuzz wall count first read 0 everywhere because its filter looked for `wall_` names, which the RoomPlan rooms do not use (`wall0`); it now matches the twin's wall ids. Tests: `counter_b` opens with no link-to-wall contact from the further spot and `counter_a` keeps its spot; `counter_b` slid 1 cm off the wall keeps the usual spot and opens.

**site/data re-exported; the exporter fixed (Oct 21).** `scripts/export_demo_data.py` had not been run since Oct 19 and no longer finished: since the sim state became 11 floats (arm, gripper, base pose) its own `verify` failed (`joint_states` rows are 11 wide), and `site/js/data.js` packs 8 values per frame, so the new rows would have overrun its buffer. `pack_episode` now writes the first 8 values (arm and gripper), as `site/data/README.md` documents. Two more defects surfaced on the way. The eval block in `stats.json` gained `manifest_path` and `episodes_path` (absolute paths on the exporting machine) after frozen trials landed; every `*_path` key is now dropped, and `verify` fails if any JSON under the output holds the home directory or the output's parent path. And the script clears its output folder, which deleted `site/data/todo.json`, the hand-edited to-do list the README and the schedule are rendered from; files named in `KEEP` are now carried across the re-run. The re-export changes the published numbers: state and action are 11 wide with a fixed-base warning in place of `stats_no_quantiles`, the six episodes and previews come from the current expert and walkers, and the eval and cost blocks carry run ids. `scripts/check_site.py --offline` resolves all 62 referenced paths, and the page loads in headless Chromium with no console errors and no failed requests. Tests: `tests/test_export_demo_data.py` (rows are 8 wide and match the first 8 state values; a re-run keeps `todo.json` and removes stale files), both failing on the old script. `LOCAL-README.md`'s known gaps are rewritten against `main` at c333ee1.

**Job memory limit, measured on Linux and replaced.** `ARRIVAL_MAX_JOB_MB` set `RLIMIT_AS` in the job's child process, and it had only been tried on a bare `bytearray`. With a real hero_kitchen job on Ubuntu 24.04 in Docker (2 episodes, 1 person, res 64), the child peaks at 5.7 GB of address space for 0.36 GB resident on 18 cores, 2.4 GB on 2 cores, and 2.6 GB with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`. Under caps from 5120 down to 512 MB the child aborted, segfaulted, hit an Arrow thread error, or had MuJoCo's allocation failure reported as "unsupported layout". It never raised `MemoryError`. The cap now limits resident memory instead: the worker reads the child's process tree every 0.5 s (`tree_rss`, `/proc` or `ps`), kills it past the limit, and records `MemoryLimitError ... during stage '...'`, which is not retried. The same job finishes at 600 MB and up and fails cleanly at 300 MB, on 18 cores and on 2. Tests: a fake child that allocates 300 MB against a 100 MB limit, one at 20 MB under a 1 GB limit, and a grandchild's memory counted toward its parent. Numbers are in docs/ref/MEMCAP.md.

**open_fridge: the push is re-planned from the door's measured angle.** The expert planned the whole fridge motion at reset, for a door that stays at the 40 deg hook angle while the hand slides off the bar and rounds the free edge to a point `FRIDGE_BACK` (0.08 m) behind the panel. A right-hinged fridge (`hinge: "right"`, the mirrored hero kitchen in `tests/test_fridge_hinge.py`) ended every run at 66.8 deg, 13 short of the planned 80, because the hand reached the panel's free edge instead of its inside face. Phase 1 now ends with the slide-off and `FRIDGE_SETTLE_STEPS` (6) held. `act()` then reads the door joint and replaces the rest of the plan with one built for that angle, starting from the arm's current command (`_plan(..., start=)`, both jaw headings as at reset). The re-plan is skipped when the planned hook's IK error is over `FRIDGE_REPLAN_IK` (2 cm). The U kitchen's side-wall fridge plans a 1.70 m error, and re-planning there flung the door to 117 deg and counted a success, so that room fails as it did before, and its pre-flight should reject it. Measured with 0, 1 and 2 people at seeds 0 to 7: the original fridge 24/24 at 79.8 deg (unchanged), the mirrored one 24/24 at 79.8 deg (was 66.8), the door at 41.6 deg when re-planned, 0 contacts. The hero kitchen with every family and 2 people stays 30/30. Two alternatives were rejected. Opening the fingers before the slide-off gave the mirrored door 80 deg but flung the original to 111.5 deg. `FRIDGE_BACK` 0.05 fixed both hero doors, but in the U kitchen it flung the door to 113.6 deg. `test_both_hinges_open_the_fridge_as_far` asserts both doors pass 75 deg within 1 deg of each other.

**TWN-3 leftover: curved walls.** RoomPlan puts `curve` (`startAngle`, `endAngle`, `radius`, and on iOS 17 `center` in local XZ) on a wall that is not flat. The normalizer used to project that wall as one straight box, the same as a wall with `curve: null`. `arrival.scan.roomplan._walls` now walks the arc in 15° steps (at least two chords) and emits one `start`/`end` segment per chord, still in the wall's transform, then the usual room alignment. A missing, null or unreadable `curve` stays one straight wall. Object yaw is unchanged. Checked with a synthetic quarter-circle and a 10° arc in `tests/test_roomplan_coverage.py`: segment count, equal chord length, a shared polyline, and vertices on a circle of the given radius. Per-wall `polygonCorners` are still unused.

**Fine-tune guide command.** `docs/GR00T-FINETUNE.md` told readers to run `python -m arrival sim ... --videos all`, a flag `sim` does not have (argparse exits 2; only `demo` has `--videos`). `sim` always writes every episode video, so the flag is dropped. `scripts/check_docs.py` only parsed commands in README, REFERENCE and ios/README, which is why this survived. It now also covers DEPLOY, GR00T-FINETUNE, RELEASE-QA, SCANNING, 07-eval and 08-access, which were already clean. `tests/test_docs_claims.py` pins the fine-tune guide to the checker.
**Fanout exit code.** `fanout` validated the merged dataset but dropped the problems and returned 2 (usage error), so a bad merge looked like a bad command line and said nothing. It now prints each problem as `INVALID: ...` on stderr and returns 1, the code `validate`, `subset` and `merge-shards` already use. `tests/test_cli.py` covers it with a stubbed fanout and validator.
**Job limits in .env.example.** `.env.example` is meant to list every setting, but eight of the nine `ARRIVAL_*` job limits that `arrival/jobs/limits.py` reads (upload size, episodes, people, job seconds, queue, job memory, retries, retry backoff) were missing; only the retention window was there. They are now listed with their defaults, with a note that `Limits.from_env` reads the process environment and not this file. `tests/test_config.py` fails if a variable in `limits.ENV` is absent from the example.
**Stale cached twin.** `sim`, `eval` and `research` loaded `out/twins/<room>/twin.json` whenever it existed and never looked at `scans/<room>/scan.json`, so after editing a scan the next run silently used the old room. They now share `_load_twin`, which recompiles from the scan when `scan.json` was saved after the cached twin was written. A twin with no scan beside it (uploaded jobs) is still used as is. Tests in `tests/test_cli.py` set file times to cover both orders.

**Test suite runtime.** The README said the suite runs in about 15 s; the weights CI shards by sum to 1,228 s (about 20 minutes on one core), which matches a sequential run here (90 files, each run alone, Windows laptop). The row now says so. 31 test files had no entry in `tests/shard_weights.json` and counted as 1 s each; they now carry the times from that run (all under 4 s), and `tests/test_ci_hygiene.py` fails when a test file has no weight or a weight names a file that is gone.

**Paired comparison of eval runs.** The README and `docs/07-eval.md` quote paired exact sign tests between fine-tune runs (for example 17 won and 4 lost, p = 0.007), but no code in the repo computed them. `python -m arrival eval-compare RUN_A RUN_B` (`arrival/eval/compare.py`) reads two runs' `episodes.jsonl`, refuses different manifest IDs or trial sets, pairs trials by `trial_index` and prints successes, trials won by each side and the sign-test p per family and in total. Wilson intervals are not repeated here; `report.json` has them per run. Tests in `tests/test_eval_compare.py` build a 48-trial pair with 17 wins and 4 losses and get p = 0.0072, and check the refusals and exit codes. It was not run against the real run 2 and run 4 records, which are not in the repo.

**EVAL: paired trials must agree on task family.** Comparison checked manifest, trial index and task ID but grouped results using only run A's family labels. A differing family in run B could therefore be scored under the wrong family without an error. Paired records now must have matching families before aggregation. Two regressions alter the family on either side while keeping all pairing IDs unchanged; both were accepted before the fix. Valid comparison results and website UI are unchanged.

**EVAL: require unique integer trial identities before paired comparison.** Repeated trial records in both runs were counted as independent evidence, changing reported trial counts and sign-test results. The run reader now requires nonnegative integer trial indices and rejects duplicates before sorting or pairing. Regressions duplicate either side or both and cover six invalid identity shapes; the baseline accepted duplicates on both sides and either accepted or mishandled malformed indices. Valid run ordering and comparison calculations are unchanged. No website changes.

**EVAL: require boolean success outcomes.** Paired comparison summed success values without checking their JSON type. Numeric values could produce success rates above one or fractional success counts, and strings/null caused type errors. The reader now requires a boolean outcome before aggregation. Six regressions cover integer, fractional, string and null outcomes; each exposed acceptance or a traceback before the fix. Existing true/false comparisons and website UI are unchanged.

**Fanout sim options.** `fanout` could only pass room, families, episodes, people, res, seed and scans to its shards, so a DART or mobile-base or three-camera dataset could not be built with it (run 4 used `--dart 0.025`). `FanoutConfig` and `arrival fanout` now take `--dart`, `--base` and `--exocentric` and add them to each shard's `sim` command only when they differ from the defaults, so existing commands and Nebius job arguments are unchanged. The values are checked in `FanoutConfig`. A real two-shard local run with `--dart 0.02` and `--exocentric` merges into a dataset that validates and has an overview camera. A run stored by an earlier version has no such keys in its `run.json`, so resuming it reports different settings; start it under a new run id. `--planner`, `--instructions`, `--arm` and the shard-level `parallel` and `max_attempts` are still not exposed.

**eval --manifest now refuses --base and --arm.** `eval --manifest` already refused `--seed`, `--room`, `--holdout`, `--res` and the rest, but `--base` and `--arm` carried real defaults (`fixed`, `procedural`), so the conflict check never saw them as given. `eval --manifest run.json --base mobile` silently replayed the frozen base and arm. Both options now default to unset and take their defaults only when no manifest is given, so the same message ("--manifest cannot be combined with --base") fires. `test_cli_manifest_refuses_base_and_arm` covers `--base mobile`, `--base fixed` and `--arm menagerie`, and checks that no rollout starts.

**Policy wire: an integer too large for a float is a 400.** `protocol._numeric_array` turned bad numbers into `ProtocolError` but caught only `TypeError` and `ValueError`. A JSON integer of 400 digits makes `np.asarray(..., dtype=float)` raise `OverflowError`, which the mock server and the GR00T shim do not catch, so the handler thread died and the client saw `RemoteDisconnected` instead of an HTTP status. The conversion in `_numeric_array` and in `encode_response` now catches `OverflowError` too. Checked against the mock server with a `state.gripper` of one followed by 400 zeros: before, `RemoteDisconnected: Remote end closed connection without response`; after, HTTP 400 with `state.gripper is not numeric`. `tests/test_policy_wire_golden.py` and `tests/test_policy_server.py` each gained one test that fails on the old code.

**CAP-2: handle out-of-range numeric manifest values as validation errors.** JSON integers can be larger than floating-point conversions support. Timestamp checks, intrinsic conversion and transform conversion previously leaked OverflowError, bypassing callers' ManifestError handling. Each conversion boundary now reports its existing field-specific error code. Six tiny synthetic-bundle regressions cover timestamp, three intrinsics, the world transform and a frame pose using an oversized integer; all raised OverflowError before the fix. Valid numeric manifests remain unchanged. No website changes.

**subset: a folder with no dataset exits 2 with a message.** `arrival subset` caught `SubsetError` only, but `_find_dataset` raises `ShardError` for a folder that holds no `meta/info.json` and no `datasets/<room>_sim`, so a mistyped source printed a Python traceback. `cmd_subset` now catches both and prints `subset: <reason>` with exit code 2, the same as `merge-shards` does for the same error. Checked on an empty folder: before, `arrival.jobs.shards.ShardError` traceback; after, `subset: ...: no dataset here (no meta/info.json, none under datasets/); is the shard finished?`. `tests/test_subset.py` gained a CLI test that fails on the old code. The 9 tests in that file pass.

**demo_all: a loader-check stage.** `scripts/demo_all.py` stopped at `validate`, which checks the dataset's own files, and never ran `arrival.export.loader_check.check_loader`, the check that reads the files the way a LeRobot v2 loader does and decodes the mp4s. The demo now has a "loader check" stage right after `validate`; a failing check fails the demo with the first three error codes in the summary. On `--quick` hero_kitchen the stage passed in 0.3 s, so the run still takes about 24 s. `tests/test_demo_all.py` now expects seven passing stages, and a second test patches `check_loader` to fail and expects exit 1. A mock policy-server round trip was not added; it starts a server thread and is a separate change.

**Export stats and quality checks read parquet columns without Python lists.** `dataset_stats` (`stats.json` q01/q99) and `export.quality` turned every list column into Python floats with `to_pylist` before building a NumPy array. `lerobot.list_column` now reads the Arrow buffers directly and raises `ValueError` for null rows or rows of unequal width, as the old path did. Measured on a synthetic dataset of 200 episodes of 400 steps (88,000 frames, 11-wide state and action), median of five runs, same input: `dataset_stats` 0.353 s before, 0.194 s after; reading and checking every episode in `quality` 0.309 s before, 0.200 s after; Python heap peak inside `dataset_stats` 84.9 MB before, 24.4 MB after. The `stats.json` content is byte-identical on that input. The saving is small at this size because reading the parquet files is most of what is left; it matters mainly for the heap on a merged dataset of thousands of episodes, which was not run here.

**Loader checker rejects episode statistic shape mismatches.** Per-episode comparisons require identical dimensions before numerical tolerance checks. A truncated action mean now produces stats_mismatch instead of a NumPy broadcasting exception, and a nested mean with identical values cannot pass by broadcasting. Both shapes have regression coverage alongside the valid dataset checks.

**Mobile base with a served policy.** `_sim_args` still refused `--base mobile --policy vla` because "the policy server wire format carries no base pose yet". That stopped being true when the wire got its 11-float mobile schema (`state.base` in the request, `action.base` in the reply), and `VLAClient` already picks that schema when the env has a mobile base, so the guard is gone. Checked against the mock server with a reply that adds 0.02 m to base x every chunk: `sim --base mobile --policy vla --families counter_to_counter --episodes 1 --res 32` used to exit 2 without sending a request; it now exits 0 after 100 requests, every state 11 floats, base x going from 1.53 to 3.44 m. `test_cli_mobile_base_is_driven_by_a_served_policy` checks the same thing for `sim` and `eval`. The GR00T shim has still only been run with the 8-float schema.

**sim exit code when every task errors.** `sim` returned 0 even when no task got as far as the physics check because every one raised while building the env, for example a renderer that cannot start (`--res 0` on Windows gives `gladLoadGL error` for each task). The run printed "0 episodes / 0 attempts" and left a dataset that `validate` rejects (`missing meta/stats.json`), so a script or job runner saw a success. It now exits 1 with a line saying every task errored. A run that reached the physics check, a partly failed run and an empty shard (`--shard 0/50` over one task) still exit 0. `test_sim_exits_1_when_every_task_errors` replaces `SimEnv` with one that raises.

**L18 follow-up: gc and DELETE no longer report a job deleted when files are left.** `delete_job` removed every path with `shutil.rmtree(..., ignore_errors=True)`, the job folder last. A file that could not be removed (a dataset or zip held open on Windows, a permission error) was skipped in silence: `gc` listed the job as removed with its full byte count, `DELETE /jobs/<id>` answered `deleted`, and the job folder with its `status.json` was gone, so no later sweep could find what was left. Reproduced on main with one dataset file held open: `removed` named the job, `bytes` was 1234, the file stayed after it was closed and a second `gc` removed nothing. Deletion errors now raise, and `status.json` is removed after everything else, so the job stays listed. `gc` logs the error and skips the job (counted neither in `removed` nor in `bytes`), DELETE answers 500, and the next sweep finishes it. `test_gc_keeps_a_job_it_could_not_fully_delete_and_retries_it` blocks a dataset file and the job's zip in turn (held open on Windows, a read-only folder elsewhere); it fails on main and passes now.

**L3 follow-up: `arrival work` checks its time flags like their variables.** `ARRIVAL_MAX_JOB_SECONDS` and `ARRIVAL_RETENTION_HOURS` must be finite and >= 0, or `work` exits 2. The `--max-job-seconds` and `--retention-hours` flags went into `Limits` unchecked. With one job queued, `--max-job-seconds -5` killed it at once and recorded `failed` with `TimeoutError: job exceeded the -5 s wall-clock limit during stage None` (a timeout is not retried), and the worker exited 0. `--max-job-seconds nan` ran the job with no limit at all. `--retention-hours` set to `nan`, `inf` or `-1` crashed the worker at its first sweep with a `ValueError` traceback. `cmd_work` now applies the variables' rule to both flags and exits 2 before claiming a job; `0` keeps its meaning. `test_work_cli_refuses_a_bad_time_limit_before_touching_jobs` (both flags with `-5`, `nan`, `inf`) fails 6/6 with cli.py from main and passes now, and the queued job stays `queued`.

**L3 follow-up: `arrival work` refuses a `--res` that fails every job.** The worker checked `--episodes`, `--people` and the families before claiming a job, but not `--res`. With a hero kitchen scan queued, `work --once --res 0` failed the job with `unsupported layout: no task can run in this room: FatalError: gladLoadGL error`, which blames the uploaded room, and `--res 100` failed it with the exporter's "multiples of 16" error. Both exited 0, so a worker started with a bad `--res` fails every job it picks up. `cmd_work` now exits 2 before claiming anything unless `--res` is a positive multiple of 16, the exporter's own rule. Every `--res` in the README, docs and scripts (32, 64, 128, 224) still passes. `test_work_cli_refuses_a_res_every_job_would_fail_on` (0 and 100) fails 2/2 with cli.py from main and passes now, and the queued job stays `queued`.

**WEB: report invalid ZIP entry-name encoding as an archive error.** UTF-8 decoding errors in a ZIP's central directory or local entry header escaped the frames archive error handlers and returned HTTP 500. Both opening and member-reading handlers now map those decoding failures to the existing bad_zip response. Three real multipart regressions cover invalid encoded names in either header or both, expect HTTP 400 identifying frames and verify no job is queued; all returned 500 before the fix. Valid archive handling and website UI are unchanged.

**Upload: a non-list `walls` or `objects` is the upload's fault, not retried.** `load_upload` decided whether an upload is a RoomPlan export by looping over `d.get("walls") or []`, so a number or `true` there raised a `TypeError` before the RoomPlan checks ran. `retryable` treats a `TypeError` as a fault another attempt may not hit, so the job went back to the queue twice for an upload that can never pass. On main, the hero kitchen export with `"walls": 1` ran as `failed at normalize: TypeError: 'int' object is not iterable` then `queued again, retry 1 of 2`. The check now only looks inside a list, so the export reaches `roomplan._clean` and fails once with `ScanBundleError: walls must be a list, got int`. A fuzz of the hero kitchen `roomplan.json` and `scan.json` (every key and first list item, 16 wrong values each, through `load_upload` and `compile_twin`) found no other error that is not a `ValueError`. `test_wrong_type_list_in_roomplan_fails_without_retry` covers `walls` 1 and -2.5 and `objects` true.

**CAP-2: validate ZIP source folders before touching the destination.** ZIP enumeration previously treated a missing source or a regular file as an empty directory and returned success, potentially replacing a good archive with an empty ZIP. The writer now reads the source's directory resource value before creating or removing output; missing paths throw the filesystem error and regular files report a directory-required error. A genuinely empty directory remains valid for this generic ZIP writer. Five offline cases compile the actual Swift writer: four invalid-source/destination combinations reproduce the old behavior and check preservation, and an empty-folder ZIP is read back with Python. No website changes.

**Frames upload: a non-text `depth_unit` is a 422, not a 500.** `rgbd.load_manifest` checked `unit not in DEPTH_UNITS`, and a list or object there raised `TypeError: unhashable type` instead of the loader's `depth_unit` error. The upload route only turns `FramesError` into a 4xx, so a frames zip with `"depth_unit": ["m"]` got a generic 500. The check now refuses anything that is not a string first. A fuzz of the synthetic bundle's manifest (every key and first list item, 25 wrong values each, through `frames.validate`) found no other error outside `FramesError`. `test_bad_frames_bundle_is_rejected_and_nothing_is_queued` has a new case: 500 on main, 422 with `code: depth_unit` now.

**CAP-2: reject NPZ archives masquerading as depth arrays.** NumPy accepts ZIP-based NPZ content regardless of a `.npy` filename. The frames upload validator then accessed `.shape` on an NpzFile outside its error handler, producing HTTP 500. It now checks that the loaded object is an ndarray, closes an unexpected archive and returns the existing `bad_depth` validation error. A real multipart upload regression replaces one metric depth file with NPZ bytes, expects HTTP 422 with field `frames`, and verifies no job was queued; it returned HTTP 500 before the fix. No website changes.

**RoomPlan: `"rooms": null` reads like no rooms.** `roomplan._clean` says a missing or null list is empty, but it looped over `[out] + out.get("rooms", [])`, which is `None` when the key is there and null. An export with top-level walls and `"rooms": null` raised `TypeError: can only concatenate list (not "NoneType") to list`; through the upload worker that `TypeError` counts as retryable, so the job was queued again twice. The loop now uses `out.get("rooms") or []`. On main the hero kitchen export with `"rooms": null` ran as `failed at normalize: TypeError` then `queued again, retry 1 of 2`; now the same upload finishes, 1/1 episodes. `test_null_rooms_reads_like_no_rooms` checks the bundle equals the one without the key.

**export-site: only replace an earlier export.** `export_site` deletes `dest` before it moves the new export in. #250 made it refuse the working directory, `out`, `scans` and their parents, but a folder inside them was still deleted. `arrival export-site --dest out/datasets` exited 0 and the datasets were gone, and a `--dest docs` holding other files came back holding only the export. A non-empty `dest` is now replaced only when it has `data/index.json`, which every export writes. Anything else stops with exit 1 before the crawl and nothing is deleted. A missing folder, an empty one and an earlier export work as before. On main, a scratch `out/` lost `datasets/hero_kitchen_sim` this way; now the command prints `refusing to replace ...: it is not empty and not an earlier export (no data/index.json)` and the dataset is still there. `test_export_refuses_a_folder_that_is_not_an_earlier_export` covers `out/datasets`, a folder of notes through the CLI, and an empty folder. The replace test used to seed its "old export" with a folder that only had `stale.txt`; it now runs a real export first.

**gc: `--max-age-hours nan` or `inf` is a usage error.** `cmd_gc` only refused values `<= 0`, so `nan` and `inf` reached `retention.gc`, which raises on them, and the command ended in a `ValueError: max_age_hours must be finite and > 0` traceback with exit 1. The flag now has to be a finite number above 0, or `gc` prints `--max-age-hours must be a finite number > 0` and exits 2 before looking at any job, the same as `0` already did. `test_gc_cli_refuses_nan_and_inf_as_usage_errors` (`nan`, `inf`, `-1`) fails on main and passes now, and the finished job is still there.

**work: `--poll` has to be a number `time.sleep` accepts.** `--poll` went to the worker loop unchecked, and the loop calls `time.sleep(poll)` whenever the queue is empty. `arrival work --poll -1` therefore crashed with `ValueError: sleep length must be non-negative` the first time it found no job; `nan` crashed with `ValueError` and `inf` with `OverflowError` the same way. `cmd_work` now exits 2 with `--poll must be a finite number >= 0` before the worker starts; `0` still means poll without waiting. `test_work_cli_refuses_a_poll_time_sleep_cannot_take` fails with cli.py from main and passes now.

**serve: `--max-upload-mb nan` or `inf` is a usage error.** `cmd_serve` refused `--max-upload-mb <= 0` with a usage message, but `nan` and `inf` passed and reached `int(max_upload_mb * 1024 * 1024)` in `web/app.py`, so `serve` stopped with `ValueError: cannot convert float NaN to integer` or `OverflowError: cannot convert float infinity to integer` and exit 1. The flag now has to be a finite number above 0; both values get the existing message and exit 2. `test_serve_hardening_flags_parse_and_validate` checks both and fails with cli.py from main.

**sim: a negative `--people` or `--seed` is a usage error, before the old dataset is cleared.** Neither was checked. The writer cleared `out/datasets/<room>_sim` first, then every task failed at build (`ValueError: high <= 0` from the people sampler, `expected non-negative integer` from numpy's seed), and the run ended with "0 episodes / 0 attempts" and exit 0. On a scratch `out/` holding a valid 2-episode exocentric dataset, that left `INVALID: missing meta/stats.json` where the dataset had been. `_sim_args` (shared by `sim` and `eval`) now returns `--people must be >= 0` before anything is written, so `sim` exits 2 and the old dataset still validates. `eval` already refused a negative `--people` later with a general message and now gives the same one. `--seed -1` gets `--seed must be >= 0` from `cmd_sim` only, because `eval --seed -1` runs fine on main. `test_sim_negative_people_or_seed_is_a_usage_error_and_keeps_the_old_dataset` (both flags) fails with cli.py from main and passes now.

**POL-1 follow-up: each episode says what planned it.** With `--planner vlm` and no `TOKEN_FACTORY_API_KEY`, `VLMPlanner` falls back to the rule parser and the log says `planned by rule_based`, but `collect` kept that in `task["planned_by"]` only. The dataset's provenance and `reports/<room>_sim.json` say `planner: vlm`, which is what was asked for, so a dataset with no VLM call in it looked VLM-planned. `episodes.jsonl` rows now carry `arrival_planned_by` (`token_factory`, `cache` or `rule_based`) when a planner ran, next to `arrival_task_id`. Rows are copied whole by `merge-shards`, `subset` and `--resume`, so the field stays with its episode. A no-key run of 2 open_drawer episodes gives 2 rows with `rule_based`; a 2-shard run merged and then subset keeps both; validate, `validate --episodes` and `check_dataset_loader.py` pass on the output. Runs without `--planner vlm` write no new field. `test_each_episode_records_what_actually_planned_it` fails on main with `KeyError: 'arrival_planned_by'`.

**open_fridge on a side wall, and no flagged fridge episodes.** Two problems on `main` after #199. First, the push heading `qa(q)` was `pi-or-0 + m*q`, without the fixture's yaw, while the hook used the live door geometry. So u_kitchen_42's fridge (yaw pi/2) had its edge rounding, push and retreat planned 90 deg off: 1.70 m of IK error, and the door never opened (the README's "fridge push heading ignores the fixture's yaw"). `qa` now adds the yaw. Second, #199's six-step hold after the slide-off sat where joint 4 is near its stop, so every `open_fridge` episode held it inside the quality check's band for 43 steps, past `LIMIT_STEPS` (40), and was flagged (3 of 30 in a hero `--families all` run). The hold now happens `FRIDGE_SETTLE_IN` (6 cm) back toward the hinge, which brings the run to 30 (4 cm gave 38, two steps under the limit, and 8 cm gives 28; the door reads 79.8 deg in each). Dropping the hold altogether also gives 38, but the door is still moving when it is read: hero and U-kitchen doors stayed shut, and the mirrored one was flung to 110.9 deg. Holding 4 cm higher flung two doors to 111.5 deg. At 4 cm, with 0, 1 and 2 people and seeds 0 to 7, all three fridges (hero, mirrored, U kitchen) opened to 79.8 deg with the longest joint-limit run at 38. At 6 cm, seeds 0 to 3 with no people give 79.8 deg and a run of 30 on all three, and 8 episodes with 2 people on the hero and U kitchens are 8/8 each, 0 human contacts, 0 flagged. Tests: `test_both_hinges_open_the_fridge_as_far` now also checks the joint-limit run stays 5 steps under the limit, and `test_a_fridge_on_a_side_wall_opens` is new; both fail on `main`.

**To-do counts checked against the rows.** The merge that marked L32 and L33 done (3dea619) moved both rows from review to done but left `counts` at 55 done and 3 in review. The README to-do and the demo site both print `counts`, so they said 55 shipped and 3 in review while the list held 57 done and 1 in review, and the site's "In review" chip showed 3 over one row. `scripts/check_docs.py` now recounts the statuses in `site/data/todo.json` and reports any count that differs, so CI's docs step catches the next one. The counts are fixed and the README block regenerated with `scripts/render_todo_readme.py`. The `prs` merged and open numbers are not derived from the file and are not checked.

**fanout: a bad setting is a usage error, not a traceback.** `cmd_fanout` built its `FanoutConfig` one line above the `try` that turns a `ShardError` into `fanout: ...` and exit 2, so the checks in `FanoutConfig.__post_init__` never reached it. `arrival fanout --shards 0` and `arrival fanout --run-id ../x` printed a full traceback ending in `arrival.jobs.shards.ShardError: shards must be an integer >= 1` (or the run_id rule) and exited 1. The config is now built inside its own `try`: one line, `fanout: shards must be an integer >= 1`, exit 2, and no fanout store is created. `test_fanout_bad_config_is_usage_error` fails with cli.py from main and passes now.

**serve: `--port` has to be one `bind()` accepts.** `--port` went to the socket unchecked, so `arrival serve --port 70000` or `--port -1` crashed with a traceback ending in `OverflowError: bind(): port must be 0-65535.` and exited 1. `cmd_serve` now exits 2 with `--port must be 0-65535 (0 = any free port)` before anything is imported or bound. `--port 0` still serves on a free port and prints it. `test_serve_refuses_a_port_bind_cannot_take` fails with cli.py from main and passes now.

**sim: `--dart` has to be a finite number >= 0.** `collect` only adds noise when `exec_noise > 0`, so `--dart -1` and `--dart nan` ran a clean dataset, but both values went into the run's provenance in `meta/info.json` as if they had been used. `nan` was written as `"exec_noise": NaN`, which is not JSON, and `validate` still passed the dataset. `cmd_sim` now exits 2 with `--dart must be a finite number >= 0 (0 = off)` before anything is written. `--dart 0.02` is unchanged (`"exec_noise": 0.02`, 1/1 on hero_kitchen). `test_sim_refuses_a_dart_that_is_not_a_finite_number_at_least_0` (-0.02, nan, inf) fails with cli.py from main and passes now.

**validate: `--json` makes its folder.** `arrival validate DIR --episodes --json out/reports/q.json` crashed with `FileNotFoundError` from `write_text` when `out/reports/` did not exist yet, after the per-episode table had printed and, with `--drop`, after the cleaned copy was written but before its structural re-check. The report's parent folder is now created first. `test_cli_json_report_goes_into_a_folder_that_does_not_exist_yet` fails with cli.py from main and passes now.

**check_layout: a missing room or twin folder is a usage error.** `scripts/check_layout.py` exits 1 when the layout fails the check, and it also exited 1, with a `FileNotFoundError` traceback, for `--room no_such_room` or a folder with no `twin.json`, so a caller could not tell a bad layout from a typo. Both now print `check_layout.py: error: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: ...` and exit 2, like the existing "give a twin folder or --room" error. `test_cli_missing_input_is_a_usage_error_not_a_layout_fail` fails with the script from main and passes now.

**Generator scripts answer `--help`.** `scripts/make_demo_video.py`, `make_detection_fixture.py`, `render_contributors.py` and `fetch_menagerie.py` read no arguments, so `--help` or any typo ran them in full. `python scripts/make_demo_video.py --help` rendered 930 frames and rewrote the tracked `docs/media/demo.mp4`, `demo.gif` and `poster.png`; the others rewrite `tests/fixtures/detection/`, `README.md` and `docs/CONTRIBUTORS.md`, or clone 34 MB of Menagerie meshes. Each now parses an empty argparse parser first: `--help` prints the module docstring and exits 0, an unknown argument exits 2, and no arguments run as before. `test_generator_scripts_answer_help_instead_of_running` covers all four.

**An empty `--families` is a usage error.** `sim`, `work` and `summary` split `--families` on commas and dropped blank names, so `--families ,` (or a script that built the list from an empty variable) became no families at all and passed the unknown-family check. `sim --room hero_kitchen --families ,` printed "0 episodes / 0 attempts", replaced a 2-episode `hero_kitchen_sim` with an empty one that `validate` rejects (`missing meta/stats.json`), and exited 0; `work --once` and `summary` also exited 0. All three now exit 2 with `--families is empty: give at least one family name, or all` before anything is written. `eval` already refused it from the manifest check ("evaluation needs distinct, nonempty family names") and now gets the same message as `sim`. `test_empty_families_is_a_usage_error` fails with cli.py from main and passes now.

**serve: one server per port on Windows, and a one-line bind error.** `ThreadingHTTPServer` sets `SO_REUSEADDR`, which on Windows lets a second socket bind a port that is already listening. A second `arrival serve --port 8766` started next to a running one and printed "Arrival serving on http://127.0.0.1:8766/" as if it owned the port, with requests going to either process. On Windows `ArrivalServer` now binds with `SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE` instead (other platforms keep `SO_REUSEADDR`, which does not allow two listeners there). The second server now fails, and `cmd_serve` turns that, or a host that does not resolve, into `serve: cannot listen on 127.0.0.1:8766: [WinError 10048] ...` and exit 2 instead of a traceback. Rebinding the port right after a server that answered requests stops still works. `test_a_port_already_served_cannot_be_bound_again` and `test_serve_reports_an_address_it_cannot_listen_on` fail with main and pass now.

**Tavily cache ships empty.** `arrival/models/tavily_cache.json` came with five answers for the standard appliance queries (dishwasher, stove, fridge, microwave, sink), each labelled `"source": "tavily_api"` and citing a URL that does not exist (four domains do not resolve, `plumbingstandards.org` is a parked page). `TavilyResearchAgent.search` checks the cache before the API, so with a `TAVILY_API_KEY` set `arrival research` and `sim --tavily` never called Tavily for those five; they printed `source=cache` and the made-up links. The shipped cache is now `{}`. With a key the five queries go to Tavily and get cached as before; with no key they use the built-in `FALLBACK_KNOWLEDGE` table, labelled `fallback`, with no sources. `tests/test_tavily.py::test_shipped_cache_does_not_shadow_the_api` loads the shipped cache with a stubbed search and checks all five reach it (0 of 5 did on main).

**Scan overlap check ignores float noise.** `scripts/intake_scan.py` on the one real kitchen scan, `tests/fixtures/roomplan/kitchen_real_01.json`, printed NO-GO for two overlapping fixtures: the dishwasher and the hinge cabinet on each side of it. They are mounted flush, and RoomPlan's transforms put the shared edges 0.1 mm or less into each other. The `_overlap` helper in `tests/test_scans.py`, which intake and `tests/test_compile.py` also use, allowed 1e-6 and projected onto edge vectors that were not unit length, so the tolerance was not even in metres. The edges are now normalized and the tolerance is 1 mm (`OVERLAP_TOL`), well under RoomPlan's own error and the 1 cm wall-closure tolerance beside it. The kitchen scan is now GO; amal.json is still NO-GO (its chairs cross the table by tens of centimetres). Tests: a unit test (0.04 mm flush passes, 5 mm into the neighbour still overlaps) and an intake test on the kitchen scan; both fail on main.

**A truncated meta file is reported, not a traceback.** A run killed while it writes `meta/` leaves a JSON file cut off partway. `arrival validate`, `arrival validate --episodes` and `scripts/check_dataset_loader.py` all crashed on that with a JSONDecodeError traceback, so the one tool meant to say what is wrong with a dataset could not say it. `validate` now parses every meta file first and returns `meta/<name> is not valid JSON (...)` (exit 1), the per-episode pass raises its usual QualityError, and the loader check reports a `meta_unreadable` finding. A test cuts the last 5 characters off each of the 7 meta files in turn; all 7 cases fail on main and pass here.

**Structural validation checks column types before values.** Wrong Arrow types were diagnosed but still entered list-length, timestamp, and task-index operations, producing TypeError instead of a validation report. Only correctly typed columns now enter those value checks, while schema errors and checks on other columns remain available. Regression cases cover scalar state/action columns and list-valued timestamps/task indices.

**EXPORT: report unreadable episode parquet without aborting validation.** A truncated parquet raised ArrowInvalid outside the structural validator's error path. It now reports the affected relative filename, advances by the declared episode length and continues checking subsequent episodes. A two-episode regression first validates a clean dataset, truncates its first parquet and verifies both a read error and the absence of a false global-index discontinuity for the intact second episode. The baseline raised ArrowInvalid. No website changes.

**open_fridge: the push follows the fixture's yaw.** The hooked pull took its heading from the built model, but the push after it (`rest` in the expert's `open_fridge` plan) took the panel heading as `pi + m*q` or `m*q`, which is right only for a fridge at yaw 0. u_kitchen_42's fridge stands on a side wall at yaw +90 deg, so every push waypoint was planned for a door turned a quarter turn away: the IK missed by 1.515 m, the arm hit fixtures in 4 of 4 episodes and `eval --room u_kitchen_42 --families open_fridge --episodes 4 --seed 5 --people 0` scored 0 of 4. The heading now adds the fixture's yaw. Measured after the fix: the same command gives 4 of 4 with IK error 0.000 and no arm contacts; with `--people 2` at seeds 3 and 7, 4 of 4 each, no human contact; the hero kitchen (yaw 0) is unchanged at 4 of 4 with 2 people. The rotated kitchen's fridge (yaw -90 deg) is still omitted by the pre-flight, because its free edge is in the room's corner, so no run there. Tests: `tests/test_fridge_yaw.py` (3, all fail before the fix).

**Integration of #253-#301, the Nemotron cache, and a live-API smoke script.** Merging #253 to #301 onto `main` gave 1640 passed and 4 failed: #259 made `cmd_fanout` read `a.dart`, `a.base` and `a.exocentric`, which the namespaces in #254's and #290's tests do not carry, so `cmd_fanout` now reads them with the same `getattr` defaults as `cmd_sim`; and #258's `test_eval_compare.py` and #298's `test_fridge_yaw.py` had no CI shard weight (0.2 s and 30.2 s measured). #298 was the same fridge-yaw fix as #279; #279's code is kept, with #298's three tests. #265 dropped the `--base mobile --policy vla` refusal that #256 and #287 had edited around, so the refusal test is gone and the bound checks from #269, #277 and #287 each appear once. `arrival/models/nemotron_cache.json` shipped six hand-written plans keyed on the hero kitchen's template wordings; `VLMPlanner.plan` checks the cache first, so `sim --planner vlm` with a `TOKEN_FACTORY_API_KEY` answered those tasks from the file and never called Token Factory, the same defect #299 fixed for Tavily. It now ships as `{}`; `tests/test_vlm_planner.py::test_shipped_cache_does_not_shadow_the_api` fails against the old file and passes with the new one. `scripts/smoke_live_apis.py` makes one planner call and one wording call to Token Factory and one Tavily research call through the same code the sim uses, each with a throwaway cache, and writes the model ids, latency, reported source and a reply excerpt (never a key) to `docs/results/live-apis-<date>.json`; it exits 2 with no key, which is what this machine has, so no real call has been made yet.

**Live Token Factory and Tavily, the planner benchmark, caches out of the package.** With real keys (Oct 26), `scripts/smoke_live_apis.py` got plans from `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b`, wordings from `nvidia/Nemotron-3_5-Lightning` and research from Tavily, all with `source` set to the live API. The first test run with the key showed the cache problem again: `sim --instructions nemotron` wrote real answers into `arrival/models/`, so the next run, or a second take of the video, answered from that file without calling Token Factory. The default caches for the planner, the wordings and Tavily are now `out/cache/*.json` (gitignored, beside every other run output), and the package ships none. The cache tests run from an empty directory and check that every query reaches the stubbed API. `scripts/planner_benchmark.py` scores the planner against `rule_plan` on the hero kitchen's inventory, with one live call per plan.
