On the first run 7 of 69 plans fell back to the parser. Nemotron had named the fixture as the object (`"object": "fridge_1"` for "open the fridge"), and `_parse_plan` refused the plan. A fixture id in `object` with no other target now becomes the target, and the planner calls at temperature 0. Rerun: 69 of 69 live; on the 15 indirect requests (written by us, labelled so in the report) Nemotron 13 and the parser 6; on the 54 Lightning-written wordings 52 against 47 (the parser's figure moves between runs because Lightning's wordings do, 40 on the first run); median 2.6 s a plan. Nemotron's misses: "cupboard" became a drawer twice, a bowl "to the far counter" went into the drawer, and "open the fridge door" became a cabinet door. The prompt was not tuned on the indirect set. The Tavily queries were picked by running two candidates per appliance against the live API, keeping the one that returned figures (the old fridge query got "the sources do not provide specific information"; the new one returns the 66.7 N federal opening-force limit from ecfr.gov), at the advanced depth. The safety guidelines that go with each answer still come from `FALLBACK_KNOWLEDGE` and are now labelled `"guidelines_source": "built_in_table"`.

**The render-gate tests on CI shard 3.** After #302, `main`'s CI failed in `rendering (Linux, shard 3/3)` on both Python versions: `tests/test_render_gate.py`'s two tests reported 0 passed where they expect 1 and 0 skipped where they expect 1. Both tests copy the real `tests/conftest.py` into a scratch folder and run pytest on it in a subprocess, and that subprocess inherited `ARRIVAL_TEST_SHARD=3/3`. The inner suite is one file, so the shard split puts it in shard 1 and deselects every inner test. The new weights in #302 moved `test_render_gate.py` from shard 1 to shard 3, which is why it only showed up now. The test file now clears `ARRIVAL_TEST_SHARD` before the inner runs. Before: `ARRIVAL_TEST_SHARD=2/2 python -m pytest tests/test_joint_limits.py tests/test_render_gate.py` gave 2 failed, 5 deselected. After: 2 passed, 5 deselected, and 7 passed with no shard set.

**Recording what wrote each instruction.** `sim --instructions nemotron` asks Nemotron Lightning for the wordings, but with no key, a failed call or an unusable reply `generate_instructions` quietly falls back to the template wordings, and nothing in the dataset said which happened. A run that was meant to show Token Factory could ship template text with no sign of it. Each episode in `episodes.jsonl` now carries `arrival_instruction_source` (`token_factory` or `template_fallback`), the same way `arrival_planned_by` does for `--planner vlm`; template runs do not get the field. With no key, `python -m arrival sim --instructions nemotron --families open_drawer,counter_to_sink --episodes 2 --people 0` now writes `template_fallback` on both episodes and `validate` still passes. `tests/test_sim.py::test_each_episode_records_what_wrote_its_instruction` covers a failed call, a stubbed good reply and a template run; it fails on `main` with a `KeyError`.

**`sim --tavily` needs the VLM planner.** Only `VLMPlanner` reads the Tavily research (`collect` builds the research context inside its planner branch), and the enriched twin is not saved. So `sim --tavily` with the default `--planner rule` ran its lookups (three for the hero kitchen, real calls when `TAVILY_API_KEY` is set) and then wrote the same dataset as without the flag: `episodes.jsonl` and the episode parquet were byte-identical for `--families open_fridge --episodes 1 --people 0`. `docs/REFERENCE.md` showed exactly that command, and the README and `docs/DEVPOST-SUBMISSION.md` named `sim --tavily` as the way the findings reach the planner. `sim` now exits 2 with "--tavily needs --planner vlm" before any lookup, and the three docs show `sim --planner vlm --tavily`. `tests/test_cli.py::test_sim_tavily_without_the_vlm_planner_is_a_usage_error` fails on `main` (the research runs) and passes now.

**The smoke script says why the wordings call failed.** `scripts/smoke_live_apis.py` keeps the planner's log, so a failed planner call shows its reason, but `generate_instructions` swallowed every error and returned only `source: template_fallback`. With a key and `NEMOTRON_SUPER_MODEL` but no `NEMOTRON_LIGHTNING_MODEL`, the script printed `FAIL token_factory_wordings: source=template_fallback` and the report had no reason. A fallback now carries an `error` field (the exception, or "the reply had no JSON list of strings"); it is never cached, and real replies do not get it. The script copies it into the report and the FAIL line: `(TokenFactoryNotConfigured: No model id for role 'lightning'; set NEMOTRON_LIGHTNING_MODEL.)`, and against a closed local port `(URLError: ... actively refused it)`, with the key absent from the report. `tests/test_smoke_live_apis.py::test_a_failed_wordings_call_says_why` fails on `main` and passes now.

**fanout checks family names before it launches shards.** `arrival fanout` passed `--families` to every shard without checking the names. `sim` rejects an unknown family, but only inside each shard, so `fanout --families bogus --shards 2` ran each of the two shards three times (six failing sim processes) and ended with "shard 0/2 failed 3 times", never saying the name was wrong; with `--backend nebius` each attempt is a Serverless AI Job. `cmd_fanout` now checks the names against `SIM_FAMILIES` before it builds a backend, the same check `sim`, `eval` and `work` make, and exits 2 with the list of valid families. `tests/test_cli.py::test_fanout_unknown_family_is_usage_error_before_any_shard` fails on main and passes here; `--families all` still goes through.

**eval-compare refuses a dataset's episodes.jsonl with a message.** A LeRobot dataset and an `eval` run both keep an `episodes.jsonl`, and `eval-compare` accepts a folder or the file. Given a dataset's `meta/` folder it read the rows (none has a `manifest_id`, so the one-manifest check passed) and crashed with `KeyError: 'trial_index'`. `load_rows` now checks each row for the six fields it uses and exits 2 with `... is not an eval run's episodes.jsonl (row 0 has no manifest_id, ...)`. `tests/test_eval_compare.py::test_dataset_episodes_jsonl_is_refused_not_a_traceback` fails on main and passes here; every row `arrival/eval/harness.py` writes has all six fields.

**Eval reports name the policy server.** `eval --policy vla` never asked the server who it was, and `checkpoint_id` is only the caller's label, so a report from the offline mock server read the same as one from the GR00T shim. Before the first episode the client now reads `GET /health` once and the report keeps the answer as `policy_server` (`{"ok": true, "server": "mock", "mode": "hold"}` from the mock, `"server": "gr00t_shim"` from the shim). A server with no `/health` is still evaluated and the report records the error instead. The two new tests in `tests/test_policy_server.py` fail on the old code and pass now; the policy-server and eval test files pass (107 tests). Checked by hand with `--base fixed` and `--base mobile` against the mock: both ran one `open_drawer` episode with no errors.

**EVAL: refuse a run that mixes checkpoints.** Paired comparison labeled each side with its first row's checkpoint even if later rows came from a different checkpoint, pooling results under the wrong label. Each loaded run must now have one consistent checkpoint ID, analogous to the existing manifest-consistency rule. Two regressions mix checkpoints on either side and reproduce the previous acceptance; valid comparisons between two distinct single-checkpoint runs are unchanged. No website changes.

**GPU run notes: the fine-tune commands paste as written.** The stats and `launch_finetune.py` commands in `docs/ref/GPU-RUN.md` ended their lines in `\\`. Markdown does not unescape code blocks, so a pasted copy hands `stats.py` a literal `\` argument and no `--modality-config-path`, then bash runs `--modality-config-path ...` and the other continuation lines as commands of their own ("command not found"). With a single `\` each block is one command again: with `python` stubbed to print its arguments, the stats line gets 7 and the fine-tune line 21, ending at `--dataloader-num-workers 4`. `scripts/check_docs.py` only read the guides in its list, so it never saw `docs/ref/`; it now also flags a fenced line ending in two backslashes in `README.md` and every markdown file under `docs/`. Those three lines were the only ones in the repo. Test: `tests/test_docs_claims.py::test_double_backslash_line_end_in_a_fenced_block` (fails on `main`, where the check does not exist).

**`make install` installs ruff, so `make lint` works after it.** CONTRIBUTING.md has contributors run `pip install -e ".[dev,sim,lint]"`, "or: make install", and then lists `make lint`. The Makefile's `install` installed only `.[dev,sim]`, so after `make install` the lint step stopped at `No module named ruff`, since ruff is pinned in the `lint` extra only. `install` now installs `.[dev,sim,lint]`; `pip install --dry-run` on that line adds `ruff-0.17.1`. Test: `tests/test_infra_files.py::test_make_install_installs_what_make_lint_and_contributing_need` checks that `make install` and the CONTRIBUTING line name the same extras, `lint` among them (fails on `main`).

`validate --episodes --drop` left a family in the cleaned copy's `arrival_families` after dropping every episode of it, so the room page listed a family the dataset no longer held (five good open_drawer episodes plus one zero-motion pick_place episode came out as `["open_drawer", "pick_place"]`). `drop_flagged` now rebuilds the list from the kept episodes, the way `SimDatasetWriter.finish` does. tasks.jsonl still keeps the dropped task's row; validate and the loader check both pass with it, so that is left as is. `tests/test_quality.py::test_drop_of_a_whole_family_removes_it_from_info` fails on main and passes here.

`--families` help on `sim`, `work` and `summary` listed four or five of the fifteen names `SIM_FAMILIES` accepts, and `eval` listed none, so nothing in `--help` mentioned close_drawer, wipe_counter, open_fridge or the dishwasher families; REFERENCE.md carried a sentence apologising for it. All four now print the full list from one `FAMILY_NAMES` string in `arrival/cli.py`, spelled out because importing `arrival.sim.tasks` loads MuJoCo. Names are separated by ", " so argparse wraps between names instead of inside them; the parsers already strip the spaces. `tests/test_cli.py::test_families_help_lists_every_sim_family` fails on main and checks the string matches `SIM_FAMILIES` exactly, so a new family cannot drift out of the help again.

`eval --holdout` held out wording template 4 only for `counter_to_sink` and `counter_to_counter`. The other thirteen families had three wordings, and `make_tasks` keeps all three for a family that short, so their held-out trials used training wordings; docs/07-eval.md named only `open_drawer` and `open_door` as exceptions. Every family in `SIM_WORDINGS` now has a fourth wording. Seven reuse the fourth line `arrival/tasks/instructions.py` already had for them; close_drawer, close_door, put_in_drawer, wipe_counter, open_fridge and move_on_table got new ones. Training draws are unchanged: they still pick from wordings 0 to 2 with the same RNG calls, and `make_tasks` over every family on hero_kitchen (fixed and mobile base, seeds 0 to 2, 270 tasks) gives byte-identical output before and after. `tests/test_eval.py::test_every_family_has_a_held_out_wording` fails on main; `test_training_tasks_exclude_the_holdout` now expects wording 3 for `open_drawer` held-out trials instead of 0 to 2.

`python -m arrival.sim.teleop --room <typo>` died with a FileNotFoundError traceback from `load_room`, where `sim` prints "no scan for room". It now exits 2 with `no scan for room 'nope_room' under scans` before any window opens. `tests/test_teleop.py::test_unknown_room_is_a_usage_error_not_a_traceback` fails on main and passes here.

`scripts/smoke_hosted.py` failed against any `--public` server, which is how DEPLOY.md tells you to host the demo and then run the script. It sent every request without cookies, so the job it had just uploaded belonged to a different visitor: `GET /jobs/<id>` was 404 and "job status readable" and "worker finished the job" failed. The script now keeps the cookie the upload sets, as a browser does. Against a local `serve --public` plus `work`, `--wait 400 --require-healthz` now passes every check, including the dataset.zip download (124,634 bytes). `tests/test_smoke_hosted.py::test_passes_against_a_public_server` fails on main and passes here; the existing tests only used a non-public server.

`scripts/scan_accuracy.py` crashed with a FileNotFoundError traceback on every scan landed by `scripts/intake_scan.py`. It always loaded `scans/<room>/scan.json`, and intake writes the export as `roomplan.json` (plus the `measurements.json` written for this script), so the SCANNING.md flow broke at the 5 cm check. It now loads `scan.json` when there is one and otherwise `roomplan.json`, the same order `arrival summary` uses. On the hero kitchen's RoomPlan export landed through intake it now reports `ERROR qa_kitchen: no scanned counter to measure` and exits 1 (that export has only a synthesized counter) instead of a traceback. `tests/test_lidar_accuracy.py::test_script_reads_an_intake_scan_with_only_roomplan_json` fails on main and passes here.

**Per-family seconds in the sim report.** `arrival cost` compares generated demos with the P3 teleop baseline through the baseline task's family row, but the `sim` report had no per-family `seconds`. So with counter_to_sink simulated in the hero kitchen, the comparison always printed `-` for generated seconds per demo and `-x` for the ratio. Each family in the report now has `seconds`: the wall time of its attempted tasks, summed. It uses the same per-task time the progress journal records, so a resumed run still counts the tasks it finished before the kill. `merge-shards` sums it per family and writes `null` if a shard report was written before the field existed. Measured here with `sim --families counter_to_sink --episodes 2 --people 0 --res 64` and then `arrival cost`: generated 5.745 s per demo against teleop's 61.518 s, a 10.71x ratio, where main printed `-`. `tests/test_shards.py` checks the per-family times, the merged sums and the cost comparison, and the new and changed tests fail on main with `KeyError: 'seconds'`. Not changed: the seed-replay report names its task under `family`, so `cost` still lists it as a room total.

**COST: report malformed family records without a traceback.** Cost collection assumed each value under a report's `families` object was itself an object. Null, scalar or list values in simulation or evaluation reports raised AttributeError and bypassed the CLI's existing error handling. Both loops now validate each value and raise CostError naming its source file and family. Six malformed-record cases and a CLI exit-code/output regression reproduce the original crash and verify a useful error; valid reports retain their existing totals. No website changes.

**Scan files are read as UTF-8 on every OS.** `scan/bundle.py` (scan.json), `scan/roomplan.py` (a RoomPlan export) and `twin/accuracy.py` (measurements.json) opened their file with no encoding, so on Windows Python used cp1252. A hero kitchen scan.json with the mug relabelled "Schüssel" loaded as "SchÃ¼ssel", and one labelled "Ásztal tál" stopped `sim` with a `UnicodeDecodeError` traceback, because "Á" is the UTF-8 bytes C3 81 and cp1252 has no character for 0x81. Object labels go into the instruction text (`sim/tasks.py`), so a garbled label would end up in the dataset. The web upload path already read the same file as UTF-8 (`jobs/worker.py:load_upload`), so the same scan worked uploaded and failed from the command line. All three now pass `encoding="utf-8"`, which is what JSON requires. `tests/test_scans.py::test_scan_files_are_read_as_utf8_whatever_the_locale` forces cp1252 for any text read that names no encoding, so it fails on main on every OS; reverting any one of the three lines alone fails it too. Measured on Windows: both labels load correctly and `sim --room` on the "Ásztal" scan runs 1/1.

**CAP-2: classify invalid UTF-8 manifests as bad JSON.** The RGB-D loader caught JSON syntax errors but leaked UnicodeDecodeError when a manifest could not be decoded as UTF-8. It now reports the existing bad_json ManifestError for either failure. Two raw-byte loader regressions and a real frames-upload regression reproduce the old exception/HTTP 500; uploads now return HTTP 422 identifying field frames and queue no job. No website changes.

`python -m arrival.generate.retarget` checked none of its arguments. A typo in `--room` died with a FileNotFoundError traceback, `--attempts 0` or `-2` ran the seed episode, wrote an empty dataset and exited 0, `--seed -1` crashed in numpy, `--res 0` crashed in MuJoCo (`gladLoadGL error`) and `--res 40` ran the seed episode before the writer refused the frame size. Each now exits 2 with a usage line before anything runs, using the same rules and wording as `sim`. `tests/test_retarget.py::test_bad_arguments_are_usage_errors_before_any_run` (6 cases) fails on main and passes here.

`arrival.render.variation` checks every argument before it copies the source, except one: with `--backend cosmos` it built the Cosmos client, which rejects a `COSMOS_URL` that is not http or https, after `copytree`. So `COSMOS_URL=ftp://example python -m arrival.render.variation SRC NEW --backend cosmos` copied the whole dataset, then raised, and left a copy with no variation label; the rerun with the URL fixed failed with "the copy directory must be new". The client is now built before the copy. `tests/test_variation.py::test_bad_cosmos_url_rejected_before_copy` fails on main and passes here.

`python -m arrival.render.variation` printed a traceback for every bad input: a mistyped dataset path (FileNotFoundError on meta/info.json), `--fraction 2`, a destination that already exists, and a source that does not validate. It now exits 2 with the same message on one usage line. A missing dataset is checked up front; the rest are the ValueError and FileExistsError `vary_dataset` already raises before it copies anything. `tests/test_variation.py::test_cli_bad_input_is_a_usage_error` (3 cases) fails on main and passes here.

`python scripts/render_rooms.py scans/hero_kitchn` (a typo) rendered nothing, printed nothing and exited 0, so it looked like a room with nothing to draw; a mistyped `.json` path died with a FileNotFoundError traceback. A path with no `scan.json` or `roomplan.json` (or a `.json` that does not exist) is now a usage error, exit 2, before the output folder is made. `tests/test_scans.py::test_render_rooms_refuses_a_path_with_no_scan` fails on main and passes here.

`python scripts/export_demo_data.py --room hero_kitchn` (a typo) cleared `--out`, which defaults to the committed `site/data`, and only then loaded the room and died with a FileNotFoundError. On a copy of `site/data` that took 37 files down to `todo.json` and two empty folders. The room is now loaded before anything is cleared, and a missing one is a usage error (exit 2) that leaves `--out` and `--scratch` as they were. `tests/test_export_demo_data.py::test_unknown_room_leaves_the_output_folder_alone` fails on main and passes here.

The demo site still said the GR00T fine-tune had not run and that no real kitchen had been scanned, while the README and `site/data/todo.json` record both: G1 is done (39 of 48 against 26 of 48, self-reported, small samples) and P1 ran `kitchen_real_01` end to end. The page's own revision schedule already gave the 39 of 48 result, so the close section and the "still to do" list contradicted it on the same page. The meta description, the scan chapter, the schedule lead and the close section now say what is true, and the "still to do" list names the open items G2, G3, P1 and P2 instead of DAT-2, POL-3, DAT-3 and CAP-3, which are no longer in the to-do list. `scripts/check_docs.py` now checks that every task the site's list names as `(ID)` is an open item in `todo.json`; on main it reports the four old IDs. `tests/test_docs_claims.py::test_site_todo_names_only_open_items` fails on main and passes here. The cost line ("No teleop baseline exists") is left for a separate change, since it comes from `stats.json`.

`make check-site` (`scripts/check_site.py`) with no server running took minutes on Windows and then printed a FAIL line for every path: it fetched the page last, so each of the 62 referenced paths waited on its own refused connection first (4.1 s each to `localhost` on Windows, measured). It now fetches the page first, and if nothing answers at `--base` it prints one line naming `make site` and `--offline` and exits 1 (4.4 s here, against over 60 s before it was stopped on main). Against a running `python -m http.server --directory site` it still reports `page ok` and checks all 62 paths, exit 0. `tests/test_check_site.py::test_no_server_is_one_line_and_one_request` fails on main (one request per path) and passes here.

`scripts/intake_scan.py` refused an iPhone photo in its default HEIC format as "not a readable image (cannot identify image file ...)", although the phone opens it, so a teammate landing a CAP-3 scan had nothing to act on. Intake now reads the first 12 bytes and refuses a HEIC/HEIF file (by its `ftyp` brand, so a renamed `.jpg` is caught too) with how to get a JPEG instead. Still exit 2 and nothing written. `tests/test_intake.py::test_heic_photo_refused_with_how_to_get_jpeg` fails on main and passes here.

`scripts/intake_scan.py` keeps a NO-GO folder so its notes can be finished, and then said "re-run validate_scan_dir", which is a Python function with no command, and which skips the geometry checks intake ran. Intake also refuses an existing folder, so a teammate who filled in the missing sink width had no way to reach GO short of deleting the folder and starting over. `intake_scan.py <room_id> --check` (module `check(room_id)`) now re-runs `validate_scan_dir` and the geometry checks on the existing folder, writes nothing, and exits 0 on GO, 1 on NO-GO, 2 when the folder does not exist; the NO-GO line prints that command, with `--scans` when it is not the default. `tests/test_intake.py::test_check_reruns_every_check_on_an_existing_folder` and `::test_cli_no_go_points_at_check` fail on main and pass here.

`arrival validate` caught truncated meta JSON but not JSON of the wrong shape. `meta/info.json`, `modality.json`, `embodiment.json`, `episodes.jsonl` or `tasks.jsonl` holding a list (or an episodes row without `length`) died with an AttributeError, TypeError or KeyError traceback, and `meta/stats.json` holding `[]` printed OK. Each meta file must now be a JSON object, each JSONL line an object, and episodes and tasks rows must carry the keys validate reads (`episode_index`, `length`; `task_index`, `task`); anything else is one INVALID line naming the file and line, exit 1. The `--episodes` pass checks the same for `info.json` and `episodes.jsonl`. `scripts/check_dataset_loader.py` crashed the same way on all seven meta files and now reports each as `meta_unreadable` through the same check (`validate.meta_shape_error`). `tests/test_quality.py::test_wrong_shape_meta_file_is_reported_not_a_traceback` (9 cases) fails on main and passes here.

**EXPORT: validate chunk size before dataset arithmetic.** The reader trusted `info.chunks_size`, so zero caused division by zero, strings/null caused type errors, and boolean or negative values entered invalid chunk calculations. It now requires a positive integer and returns a descriptive validation error before using the size. Six regressions start from a valid two-frame dataset, mutate only that metadata field and verify a chunk-size error instead of a crash or acceptance. The existing default when the optional field is absent remains unchanged. No website changes.

`python scripts/scan_accuracy.py scans/<room>` checked a mistyped room path the same way as a real room with no `measurements.json`: it printed `no_such_room: no measurements.json, skipped`, `0 room(s) measured, 0 failing` and exited 0, so the TWN-1a accuracy check passed on a folder that does not exist. A named path that is not a folder is now a usage error (exit 2) before anything is compared. A real room with no `measurements.json` is still skipped with exit 0, as the existing test expects, and the no-argument sweep of `scans/` is unchanged. `tests/test_lidar_accuracy.py::test_script_rejects_missing_room` fails on main and passes now.

`scripts/smoke_hosted.py` says it exits 2 on bad arguments, but only checked the URL. A mistyped `--scan` gave a FileNotFoundError traceback (exit 1), and `--timeout 0` ran against the server and reported `FAIL index page: GET / -> 0`, which reads as the server being down. `--scan` that is not a file, `--timeout` that is not above 0 and a `--wait` below 0 (NaN included for both) are now usage errors (exit 2) before any request. `tests/test_smoke_hosted.py::test_bad_arguments_exit_2_before_any_request` (5 cases) fails on main and passes now.

`scripts/teleop_baseline.py` (P3, `arrival/sim/teleop.py`) checked only that the costs were not negative and `--successes` was at least 1. `--operator-usd-per-hour nan` (or `inf`) passed that check, so an operator could finish a full timed session and get a `teleop_baseline.json` with `cost_basis_usd: NaN`, which `arrival.eval.cost.load_baseline` then refuses. A negative `--people` or `--seed`, or a `--res` that is not a multiple of 16, also opened the window. Each is now a usage error (exit 2) before the window opens, using the same rules as `sim`. `tests/test_teleop.py::test_bad_arguments_stop_before_the_session` (5 cases, the window stubbed to fail if it is opened) fails on main and passes now.

**`arrival realism` reports a bad twin or frame file instead of a traceback.** `--twin` only checked that the file existed, then handed whatever `json.loads` returned to the renderer. A cut-off file gave a JSONDecodeError traceback, a JSON list or string gave AttributeError, and `{}` gave KeyError 'walls', all from deep inside `run_realism`. Now an unreadable file, a non-object, or an object without the `walls`, `fixtures` and `objects` lists is a `realism:` line with exit 1, and nothing is written. The same went for a frame file that exists but cannot be read: a corrupt RGB or confidence PNG gave UnidentifiedImageError, and a corrupt `.npy` or float64 metric depth gave ValueError or ManifestError, none of which `cmd_realism` catches. Those are now a `realism: frame <id>: ...` line with exit 1. Tests: `tests/test_realism_cli.py::test_bad_twin_json_is_a_readable_error` (5 cases) and `test_unreadable_frame_file_is_a_readable_error` (4 cases), all fail on main.

**docs/SCANNING.md matches the intake script and the capture app.** The `notes.md` template in the scan protocol used fields (`Room type / layout`, `Device / iOS`, `Lighting / clutter`, tape values only in a table) that `validate_scan_dir` does not read, so a folder written from it got eight "missing or blank" problems and NO-GO. The doc also said the CAP-1 capture app was still planned, never named `scripts/intake_scan.py`, and had nothing on RGB-D frames, though `arrival realism` sends people there to record them; `ios/README.md` said to commit a scan by copying `roomplan.json` into `scans/`, which skips the EXIF strip, notes and measurements. The template now uses intake's field names, the doc shows the intake command (run as written on the kitchen_real_01 fixture: GO) and says where frames go, and `tests/test_scanning_doc.py` checks the template against `validate_scan_dir`.

**SCAN: collect unreadable-note problems without aborting validation.** An invalid UTF-8 or inaccessible notes.md raised an exception before the scan validator could return its problem list. Notes reads now convert decoding/filesystem failures into a notes-specific problem and continue checking measurements, photos and geometry. Two regressions cover malformed bytes and a simulated permission error, also verifying that a separate missing-measurement problem is collected. Both previously raised. No website changes.

**`site/data/stats.json` lists the files with their real sizes.** The `files` block is meant to give every file in `site/data` and its size in bytes, but the committed one was off in three ways. The keys were Windows paths (`episodes\open_door.json`), because the export used `str(path)`, so a re-export on Linux or macOS would rewrite every key. The JSON and README files were written with `write_text`, which gives CRLF on Windows, so the recorded sizes for `README.md`, `rooms.json` and `scan_points.json` were the CRLF sizes, not the bytes git stores and the site serves (7384 against 7278 for the README). And stats.json listed its own size from the first write, before `files` was filled in (16328 against 17177). The export now writes LF on every OS, keys the paths with `/`, and rewrites stats.json until its own entry stops changing. The committed `files` block was regenerated with that code from a fresh LF copy of `site/data`; nothing else in stats.json changed. Tests: `tests/test_export_demo_data.py::test_stats_lists_every_file_with_its_real_size` and `test_committed_stats_match_the_committed_files`, both fail on main.

**C4 live: object detection on Token Factory.** Token Factory lists `nvidia/Nemotron-3.5-Super-VL`, a vision model, so `NEMOTRON_OMNI_MODEL` can be set and `arrival/twin/detect.py` called for real. `scripts/smoke_live_apis.py` gains a `token_factory_detect` check (run when `NEMOTRON_OMNI_MODEL` is set) that reads the six rendered fixture frames with an empty cache, and `--only NAME` to run one check. On Oct 26, `nvidia/Nemotron-3.5-Super-VL` on Token Factory read the six fixture frames for real (`python scripts/smoke_live_apis.py --only token_factory_detect`, `docs/results/live-detect-2026-10-26.json`): it boxed the mug in all three mug frames and reported nothing in the three empty ones, and the mug was placed 1.2 cm from its true centre (two of its three boxes were rejected as `flat_surface`, so one view placed it). These are 128 x 96 renders of the twin, not a real scan; real RGB-D frames wait on the Swift recorder (P1). A first run gave 0.98 cm with one box rejected; the replies move by a pixel or two between runs at temperature 0. `tests/test_smoke_live_apis.py` covers the check with a stubbed reply. The hand-written `replies.json` stays for the tests.

The demo site said "No teleop baseline exists" in its to-do list, and site/data/stats.json had `comparison.measured: false` with the reason "no teleop baseline", even though the P3 keyboard teleop baseline is in docs/results/p3-teleop-2026-10-21/. `arrival cost` falls back to that file, but scripts/export_demo_data.py called `cost.build(scratch)` with no baseline, and the site line was hard-coded. The fallback now lives in `cost.default_baseline(out)`, which the CLI and the export both use. The site line is written from `stats.cost.comparison`: the reason when nothing was compared, otherwise both seconds-per-success numbers with their n. A re-export on this branch gives 8.32 s of local generation per counter_to_sink success (n=1) against 61.5 s of operator time (n=20). The committed site data is not regenerated here, so the site keeps its old "not compared" line until the next export. Test: `tests/test_cost.py::test_default_baseline_is_the_shipped_p3_run_unless_the_out_dir_has_one`, fails on main.
**CAP-2: preserve frame archives when ZIP creation fails.** `ZipWriter` previously removed an existing archive and wrote directly to its final path. A read failure after the first entry left a partial `frames.zip`, which later operations could mistake for a finished archive. ZIP creation now writes to a unique sibling temporary file, closes the complete archive, then replaces or moves it into place. Deferred cleanup removes staging files on failure without touching the previous destination. Three offline Swift-driver regressions cover failure with/without an existing archive and successful replacement checked with Python's ZIP reader; both failure cases reproduce the old bug. The test fixture removes read permission from one synthetic file and restores it in `finally`; it skips only if the host does not enforce that permission. Website files are unchanged. Checked on the standalone archive branch: all eight iOS bundle/archive tests, the simulator build, lint, documentation links and diff whitespace checks pass. Read-only review found no actionable issues. Disk-full and process-termination scenarios were not exercised.
**CAP-1: stop polling cancelled capture jobs.** The worker and retention contract treat `cancelled` as terminal, but the iOS saved-scan model only recognized `done` and `failed`. It now also recognizes `cancelled`, so the existing poll loop exits after cancellation instead of requesting status indefinitely. Saved-scan metadata moved unchanged into Foundation-only `Scan.swift` to exercise the actual model without RoomPlan. Seven offline compiled-Swift cases round-trip metadata through JSON and check terminal, active, missing and unknown status values. The cancelled case failed before the predicate fix. No changes to server behavior, metadata schema or website.
**CAP-2: reject NPZ archives masquerading as depth arrays.** NumPy accepts ZIP-based NPZ content regardless of a `.npy` filename. The frames upload validator then accessed `.shape` on an NpzFile outside its error handler, producing HTTP 500. It now checks that the loaded object is an ndarray, closes an unexpected archive and returns the existing `bad_depth` validation error. A real multipart upload regression replaces one metric depth file with NPZ bytes, expects HTTP 422 with field `frames`, and verifies no job was queued; it returned HTTP 500 before the fix. No website changes.
**COST: report malformed family records without a traceback.** Cost collection assumed each value under a report's `families` object was itself an object. Null, scalar or list values in simulation or evaluation reports raised AttributeError and bypassed the CLI's existing error handling. Both loops now validate each value and raise CostError naming its source file and family. Six malformed-record cases and a CLI exit-code/output regression reproduce the original crash and verify a useful error; valid reports retain their existing totals. No website changes.
**EXPORT: validate chunk size before dataset arithmetic.** The reader trusted `info.chunks_size`, so zero caused division by zero, strings/null caused type errors, and boolean or negative values entered invalid chunk calculations. It now requires a positive integer and returns a descriptive validation error before using the size. Six regressions start from a valid two-frame dataset, mutate only that metadata field and verify a chunk-size error instead of a crash or acceptance. The existing default when the optional field is absent remains unchanged. No website changes.
**Real kitchen: cabinet doors in a blind corner and on a narrow cabinet.** On `kitchen_real_01`, the one real iPhone scan, the expert failed every cabinet door task (`open_door` and `close_door` 0 of 3 each at seed 5) and one drawer in three. `counter_xml` always put a 0.5 m drawer stack in the left half of a cabinet and the door in the right half, hinged 2 cm in from the right end, and two real cabinets break that. CC64C90B is 0.43 m wide: the stack was wider than the cabinet and ran into the dishwasher, and `door_w` gave the door -0.074 m. F9337652 runs into the corner, where 11793D80 on the other wall stands against the last 0.64 m of its front; the door was drawn in that blind part, hinged beside the neighbour, and it stopped at 0.8 deg. `scene.front_span` now trims the part of a front another fixture stands against (within 5 cm of the face, reaching to within 2 cm of an end: the scan leaves the neighbour 1e-4 m short of the shared end). Drawers and door use the open front only. With only the open front shortened, the door went to the right of the stack and the hand met the neighbouring run at 59 deg, short of the 60 deg success bar: hinged beside the corner, the door turns its handle side toward it. So with a blind corner at the right end the stack takes that end and the door the open left end, still hinged on its right edge (`drawers_at_right`), and the expert's plans are unchanged. A front that cannot hold the stack and a door of at least 0.3 m (`MIN_DOOR_W`) gets one door across it and no drawers (`has_drawers`); `part_names` and the drawer, door and put_in_drawer pools follow, and the pre-flight's door gaps use the same hinge (`door_hinge_x`). The stack is also kept on the open front everywhere (`max(x, x0 + DRAWER_W / 2)`), which changes cabinets under 1 m that are not in a corner, none of them in a committed room (found in review by @Aadi-Tiwari): on a lone cabinet, 1.00 m and wider is unchanged; from 0.84 to 0.99 m the stack moves right by the 0.3 to 4.0 cm it used to hang past the cabinet's left end, and the door is that much narrower (0.30 to 0.45 m); under 0.84 m the cabinet is one door, where main gave 0.79 to 0.83 m a stack 4.3 to 5.2 cm past the end and a 0.30 to 0.33 m door. Measured, every family with 12 episodes: seed 0 with no people 6 of 12 on main, 12 of 12 after; seed 3 with 2 people 7 of 12 before, 10 of 12 after (the two misses, `open_door` and `open_oven`, are walker contacts on both sides). The door and drawer families alone at seed 5 are 16 of 16; the corner door opens to 75 deg. The MJCF and the task lists of every family, on both bases, are unchanged for hero_kitchen, u_kitchen_42, rotated_kitchen, office and study (sha256 of `build_mjcf` and of `make_tasks` output). Tests: 5 new in `tests/test_real_kitchen.py`, all failing on main.
**Fridge hinge from the walls (rotated kitchen's fridge was never opened).** A RoomPlan scan does not say which side a fridge door is hinged on, and `compile_twin` dropped a `hinge` a scan did give, so every fridge was left-hinged. rotated_kitchen's fridge stands with its right side in the room's corner: hinged left, the stand spot beside the free edge was (1.45, -0.02), outside the room, and the pre-flight rejected `open_fridge` with either base. `compile_twin` now keeps a scan's own `hinge` and otherwise calls `fridge_hinge`: the door is hinged on the side with less floor in the 0.9 m band in front of the fridge (`_front_side_room`, walls by their face and other fixtures by their footprint; a neighbour flush with the fridge's face is behind the band and does not count). It writes `hinge: "right"` and `hinge_source: "inferred_from_walls"` only for the right side, so a left-hinged twin is unchanged. Room in front, left / right: hero_kitchen 0.00 / 3.24 m, u_kitchen_42 0.09 / 2.28 m, kitchen_real_01 0.00 / 0.96 m (all left, as before), rotated_kitchen 2.03 / 0.07 m (right). Measured on a Linux Codespace (MuJoCo 3.17, OSMesa), rotated_kitchen with every family at 12 episodes: seed 0 with no people, main ran 12 tasks with `open_fridge` skipped (10 successes) and this change runs it (11 of 12, `open_fridge` 1 of 1); seed 3 with 2 people, 12 of 12 on both, `open_fridge` skipped on main and 1 of 1 here. The sha256 of `build_mjcf` and of `make_tasks` output for every family on both bases is unchanged for hero_kitchen, u_kitchen_42, kitchen_real_01, office and study; rotated_kitchen changes only its `open_fridge` tasks. The four tests that used the rotated fridge as their unreachable example now use a scan that says `"hinge": "left"`, so the pre-flight's rejection stays covered and a hinge given in an uploaded scan is shown to reach the twin. Tests: 6 new in `tests/test_fridge_hinge.py`.
**Planner benchmark v2: more inventories, blind requests, the rejected-reply rate, intervals.** The first report scored the planner on the hero kitchen's eight ids with requests we wrote or Lightning wrote, and a reply `_parse_plan` refused was counted under Nemotron with the keyword parser's answer. `scripts/planner_benchmark.py` now also plans on `kitchen_real_01` and `office` from `tests/fixtures/roomplan`, built the way `arrival demo --from-roomplan` builds a twin (default objects placed at seed 0 on a first compile, then compiled again), with the ids read as `collect` reads them. That gives the same ids as the tests' order (compile, then place), and a test checks it. The real kitchen's fixture ids are RoomPlan UUIDs and it has no sink. Three sets are new: `blind` (requests from `docs/results/planner-blind-requests.json`, written by people who had not seen the prompt but were given all 15 families in plain words, so it is scored over all rows and again over the rows asking for one of the 9 listed families), `out_of_scope` (20 requests that name no family's verb, fixture or object, checked against a keyword list in the test; the report gives what each planner picked) and `unlisted_families` (20 requests for the six sim families the planner prompt leaves out, expected to fail by construction: the prompt lists 9 of the 15 in `SIM_FAMILIES`, and `rule_plan` cannot return them). Each unlisted family runs in the room whose twin has the fixture its sim task needs (oven and dishwasher in `kitchen_real_01`, the faucet at the hero kitchen's sink, the table in `office`), and names only objects the sim moves there: `make_tasks` loads and unloads only the cup and the apple (the mug is out of reach and plates and bowls are not `PICKABLE`) and moves only the book along the table (the laptop is not `TABLE_MOVABLE`). A test builds each request's task with `make_tasks`, so a miss is about the missing family, not the room: `unlisted_families` is held to sim-usable objects on purpose. The blind set is not: it is a language test, so a row asking for something the sim cannot run in its room (say, the laptop along the table) is kept and scored like any other. Each scored row carries `sim_executable`, computed with the same `make_tasks` check (null without MuJoCo), and the blind summary gives the accuracy over all rows beside `sim_not_executable`, the count of rows the sim could not run. The script wraps `token_factory.complete` to keep each raw reply and reruns `_parse_plan` on it for the rejection reason; production code is unchanged. Of each call the report keeps the model id, the temperature and the base URL, never the key or a header. Per set it reports family, object and plan accuracy, the rejected-reply rate, all with Wilson 95% intervals, confusion counts and the median latency. The v2 rates count a rejected reply as wrong; the v1 keys keep their v1 meaning so the first report still reads. The script exits 1 only when a request got no reply, not when a reply was rejected, which is now measured.

**Planner benchmark v2, live (Oct 27).** The run of record is `docs/results/planner-benchmark-2026-10-27.json`: all five sets, 124 planner calls to `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b` at temperature 0 and 9 wording calls to `nvidia/Nemotron-3_5-Lightning`, every one answered, no call errors, no retries, median 2.6 s a plan (slowest 8.3 s). An earlier run the same day, `docs/results/planner-benchmark-2026-10-27-run1.json`, used the same model ids and the same code minus the retry wrapper (5f2a55a; 37d37d5 adds the wrapper, which no call in the run of record hit) on four sets, without blind. It is kept so the spread shows: temperature 0 does not make the endpoint deterministic, and the two runs are not averaged. Its first attempt stopped when Lightning answered HTTP 529 (overloaded), so the script now retries a 429 or 5xx after 2, 4 and 8 s (four calls at most), logs each retry with its case and records the count per row; nothing else is retried. Rates below are the planner's, Wilson 95% intervals in brackets, the keyword parser beside it. Generated (54 Lightning wordings, hero kitchen): 52/54 [0.87, 0.99] against 45/54 [0.71, 0.91]; run 1 had 48/54 against 50/54, on different wordings, since Lightning writes new ones each run. Indirect (15, ours): 15/15 [0.80, 1.00] against 6/15 [0.20, 0.64]; run 1 had 13/15 [0.62, 0.96]. Blind (w1's 15 requests, the only blind rows at the time; w1 is outside the team and had not seen the prompt): 7/15 [0.25, 0.70] against 2/15 [0.04, 0.38]; on the 9 rows asking for a family the prompt lists, 7/9 [0.45, 0.94] against 2/9 [0.06, 0.55]; on the 10 rows in the scanned kitchen and the office, 4/10 [0.17, 0.69] against 0/10 [0.00, 0.28]. Five of the blind rows ask for something the sim cannot run in that room and are kept (`sim_not_executable`: 5). Blind was not in run 1. The planner's blind misses are the six unlisted families (dishwasher to a drawer or the sink, the oven to the fridge, the faucet to the sink, the laptop request to putting the book in a drawer), "This bowl's hogging the only counter space" sent to the sink, and the office's "looking for a stapler" opened as a drawer when the room has a cabinet. Out of scope (20, ours): the planner refused 8/20 [0.22, 0.61] (unknown family) and picked a task for the other 12, the fridge 5 times; run 1 refused 4/20 [0.08, 0.42]. Unlisted families (20, ours): 0/20 [0.00, 0.16] for both, as expected; run 1 also 0/20. Rejected replies across the run: 9 of 124 [0.04, 0.13], 8 of them the out-of-scope refusals; in generated 1/54 [0.00, 0.10] (`counter_a` given as the object to scrub), against 2/54 in run 1. On Oct 26, v1 had 13/15 indirect and 52/54 generated; the prompt and the planner have not changed since, so today's 15/15 is not an improvement, and run 1's 13/15 the same day shows the spread.

**Blind set, two writers (Oct 27).** A second person outside the team who had not seen the prompt (w2) wrote 15 more requests, mapped to rooms, families and objects as w1's were, wording verbatim; one of them, "I'm done with this plate." in the scanned kitchen, is word for word one of w1's. `--sets blind` on all 30 went to `docs/results/planner-benchmark-2026-10-27-blind.json`; the run of record is unchanged. 30 calls to the same Super model at temperature 0, all answered, no call errors, no retries, no rejected replies, median 3.1 s (slowest 19.4 s). Planner against the keyword parser, Wilson 95% intervals: over all 30, 12/30 [0.25, 0.58] against 3/30 [0.03, 0.26]; on the 18 asking for a family the prompt lists, 12/18 [0.44, 0.84] against 3/18 [0.06, 0.39]. By writer: w1 7/15 [0.25, 0.70] against 2/15 [0.04, 0.38], listed 7/9 [0.45, 0.94] against 2/9 [0.06, 0.55], the same rows right and wrong as in the run of record; w2 5/15 [0.15, 0.58] against 1/15 [0.01, 0.30], listed 5/9 [0.27, 0.81] against 1/9 [0.02, 0.43]. The 12 rows for families the prompt does not list are all wrong for both planners. w2's four misses on listed families: the mug "right where I'm trying to chop" went into a drawer instead of to the other counter, the sponge to be put away "when people come over" went to the sink instead of a drawer, "We're out of printer paper" opened a drawer in the office, which has only a cabinet, and "Someone's going to walk into that cabinet door" was planned as open, not close. Eleven of the 30 rows ask for something the sim cannot run in that room and are kept (`sim_not_executable`: 11). With 15 requests a writer the intervals overlap; the split shows the spread between writers, not a difference between them.
**G7/DAT-4: native object-centric SE(3) retargeting.** `arrival/generate/se3_retarget.py` records an executed `counter_to_sink` source (actual end-effector poses, joint states and commands), anchors approach/grasp/lift in the object frame and transport/place/retreat in the sink frame, and connects re-anchored poses with minimum-jerk translation/quaternion bridges. Every target is solved with position-and-orientation-checked IK and stepped in MuJoCo; unreachable plans, lost grasps, failed basin placements and human-contact results are excluded from export. Successful LeRobot episodes carry `object_centric_se3_retarget`, real simulated joints, the source seed/hash and retargeted anchor matrices; the self-contained source includes scan/code provenance. The S20 translation replay and external MimicGen probe are unchanged. The new module's 15 tests passed at 32 px, including shifted/rotated object starts, a shifted physical sink, rejection paths and a check that replay never replans with the expert. Both `--seed 0` source-recording and `--seed 1 --source` reuse CLI runs (`--attempts 2 --res 32`) produced 2/2 successes, no drops and valid datasets. The CLI, source format and timing scope are documented in the reference. This native implementation does not claim upstream MimicGen integration or completion of issue #62's seed-set/100-episode benchmark. Final gates: `PYTEST_ADDOPTS='-n 4' make PY=.venv/bin/python test` passed with 1742 passed and 13 skipped (337.10 s), and `make PY=.venv/bin/python lint`, `make PY=.venv/bin/python docs-check`, `make PY=.venv/bin/python demo OUT=out/g7-demo` and `make PY=.venv/bin/python package-smoke` passed.

**`job_retry_backoff` validated as a whole number like other integer settings.** `ARRIVAL_JOB_RETRY_BACKOFF=2.5` silently truncated to 2, unlike `ARRIVAL_JOB_RETRIES=1.5` which raised a clear error. The backoff field is documented as seconds and declared as `int` in the dataclass, so non-integers should be refused. `Limits.from_env` now includes `job_retry_backoff` in the integer check alongside `max_episodes`, `max_people`, `max_queue` and `job_retries`. `tests/test_retry_backoff_env.py::test_retry_backoff_must_be_whole` fails on main (no error) and passes here.

**`_pieces` handles fixtures without `source_category`.** A twin.json loaded from disk or constructed manually may lack `source_category` on its fixtures. `_pieces` crashed with KeyError when checking if a counter was synthesized. The check now uses `.get()` so a missing key is treated as not synthesized. `tests/test_accuracy_source_category.py` fails on main (KeyError) and passes here.

**`subset` and `merge-shards` reject datasets without video features.** Both commands crashed with IndexError when a dataset had no video features (e.g., from `--videos none`). They now raise a clear error explaining that at least one camera is required. `tests/test_subset_no_video.py::test_subset_rejects_dataset_without_video_features` and `test_merge_shards_rejects_shards_without_video_features` fail on main (IndexError) and pass here.

**`tree_rss` tolerates a truncated `/proc/[pid]/stat`.** On Linux the worker reads every `/proc/*/stat` to sum resident memory for a job and its children. A process that exits between `listdir` and the read is already skipped via `OSError`, but a truncated or oddly short `stat` line raised `IndexError` on `f[1]`/`f[21]` and escaped the `(OSError, ValueError, subprocess.SubprocessError)` handler, crashing the memory check. `IndexError` is now caught with those, so `tree_rss` returns `None` as documented when the process table cannot be read. `tests/test_tree_rss_indexerror.py` fails on main and passes here.

**Retention `delete_job` returns `None` instead of crashing when job folder disappears.** `delete_job` read `status.json`, computed `job_paths` (which filters paths by existence), and unpacked the result with `*owned, folder = paths`. If the job folder was deleted between reading the status and computing paths (a race with another process, manual deletion, or disk failure), the paths list could be empty and the unpack failed with `ValueError: not enough values to unpack`. The function now checks `if not paths` and returns `None` before attempting the unpack, handling the race gracefully like the other early-return cases. `tests/test_retention_empty_paths.py` mocks `job_paths` to return an empty list and verifies the function returns `None` instead of crashing.

**The policy client refuses an observation without a wrist frame.** Every modality config in `configs/gr00t` lists `ego_view` and `wrist`, `SimEnv` renders both for `sim --policy vla` and `eval --policy vla`, and `decode_request` (mock server and GR00T shim) answers a request without either camera with a 400 `missing video.<camera>`. `encode_request` only required `ego_view`, though, and sent an observation without a wrist frame as an ego-only request, so the client found out from the server's 400. `encode_request` now requires both cameras and raises `ProtocolError: obs has no wrist frame; the policy server needs ego_view and wrist` before anything is sent; `decode_request` stays strict for both. No current caller sends an observation without a wrist frame. Tests: `tests/test_protocol_cameras_required.py`, whose encoder (wrist case) and `VLAClient` tests fail on main and whose decoder and shim tests pin the strict server side (the shim answers an ego-only request with 400 and never calls the policy), and `tests/test_policy_server.py::test_request_without_wrist_camera_is_refused`, which replaces the test that expected the ego-only request.

**A plan for another family no longer retargets the task.** With `--planner vlm`, `collect` already kept the task's family when the planner returned a different one, and logged "keeping the task's", but it still copied the plan's `object` and `target` onto the task. Those fields mean the other family's roles: a `wipe_counter` plan names the counter to wipe as its target, so a `counter_to_sink` task given that plan carried the object to `counter_b` instead of the sink and missed. Run with a stubbed planner on the hero kitchen, one episode: MISS on main, OK with a matching plan. A plan for another family now leaves the task's fields alone; a plan for the same family still refines them as before. Test: `tests/test_vlm_planner.py::test_sim_collect_ignores_plan_for_another_family`, fails on main.

**L27 follow-up: a walker held by the arm walks away after 4 s.** Walkers stand still while the robot base or an arm link is within 0.25 m (`safety.YIELD[0]`), and hold a counter pause while that is true, with no time limit. On main 03690dd an arm left reaching out beside walker seed 4 in the hero kitchen keeps them still for all 30 s of a 30 s run, and a hand beside the counter stop that steps off one step in twenty holds the pause for the whole 60 s. Now, when `Walkers.step` gets a clearance callback (collect passes one), a progress clock counts the time a walker goes without getting 0.10 m (`PROGRESS`), held pause time included. A pause is held only while that clock is under 4 s (`WAIT_MAX`). At 4 s the walker walks to the point within 0.4 m (120 headings, inside their floor box, clear of fixtures) with the most room from every robot collision geom (`SafetyMonitor.clearance_many`), then routes back to their path. No step of their own ends below 0.25 m from any robot geom; with a base that drives this applies to walk-away steps only, since the base already waits for people in front of it. The fridge trip is left alone, and if no point gains room the walker stays put and tries again next step. Four new tests in `tests/test_people.py`. On main's walker code with only the two constants added, they measure 30 s, 30 s, 55.45 s and 60 s of standing still against the 4 s cap.

**A missing room says which rooms exist.** `arrival demo --room rotated_kitchen` stopped with "room 'rotated_kitchen' not found under scans" and nothing else, and a typo like `hero_kitchn` gave the same line. The two test kitchens only run with `--scans tests/fixtures/scans`, which no doc outside this log says. `demo`, `sim`, `eval`, `research`, `summary` and the retarget and teleop scripts now add the rooms they found under `--scans` and, for a room that is in `tests/fixtures/scans`, the flag it needs: "room 'rotated_kitchen' not found under scans (rooms under scans: hero_kitchen; rotated_kitchen is a test room, add --scans tests/fixtures/scans)". With that flag the same demo runs (1 episode, 1 attempt). Exit codes are unchanged. Three tests in `tests/test_cli.py` and `tests/test_summary.py` fail on main.

**A malformed `floors` entry in an uploaded scan.json fails once, with a message.** `scan.bundle.validate` checked every other list of a scan (walls, fixtures, objects, doors, windows, openings) and raised `ScanBundleError` for a bad entry, but the floors check called `fl.get("id")` and `len(...)` on whatever it found. A floor given as the bare corner list (`"floors": [[0, 0], [4, 0], [4, 3]]`), a string, `"corners": null` or corners as flat numbers escaped as `AttributeError` or `TypeError`, which says nothing about the scan, and the upload worker, which retries anything that is not a `ValueError`, ran the same upload again `job_retries` times before failing it with `AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get'`. The check now refuses a floor that is not an object, and corners that are not a list of `[x, y]` pairs, with a `ScanBundleError`. `tests/test_scan_floor_entries.py` fails on main and passes here.

**`fanout` refuses a room with no scan before any shard runs.** A misspelt `--room` (or one missing from `--scans`) was only found by each shard's own `arrival sim`, so the local backend started every shard, retried each until `max_attempts` and stopped with "shard 0/2 failed 3 times; finished shards are kept, rerun to resume". A rerun could not help: `run.json` had already stored the misspelt room, so running again with the right `--room` under the same `--run-id` was refused as "started with different settings; use a new run id". With the local backend the command now checks `scans/<room>/scan.json` (resolved the way `sim` resolves `--scans`) after the other settings and exits 2 with `fanout: no scan for room ...`, writing nothing, the same as #307 does for an unknown family. Nebius shards read the scans baked into the job image, which this machine cannot see, so that backend is not checked. Two `tests/test_cli.py` fanout tests that never ran a shard used the placeholder room `nope_room` and now name `hero_kitchen`. `tests/test_fanout_unknown_room.py` fails on main and passes here.

**Offline `--instructions nemotron` has real wordings for every family.** With no Token Factory key, a failed call or an unusable reply, `generate_instructions` falls back to template wordings, but `FALLBACK_TEMPLATES` had none for six of the fifteen sim families: close_drawer, close_door, put_in_drawer, wipe_counter, open_fridge and move_on_table. Their episodes got the placeholder `Do task <name>` as the instruction (`Do task counter b` and `Do task fridge` on the hero kitchen), and with a single wording there was no held-out one, so `sim --instructions nemotron --split eval` stopped with `ValueError: empty range for randrange()` before any episode ran. The six families now fall back to the sim's own four wordings (`SIM_WORDINGS`); the fourth, the template path's held-out wording, is their eval split. `tests/test_instruction_fallback_families.py` fails on main and passes here.

**Provenance names no code sha for code outside its own checkout.** `git_state` ran `git rev-parse HEAD` in the folder above the `arrival` package, and git looks upward for a repository. Installed as a wheel into a venv that sits inside some other project's checkout (`pip install` into `myproject/.venv`), the package found that project's repository, so `twin.json` and every dataset's `arrival_provenance` gave the other project's HEAD as `twin_code.sha` and `run_code.sha`, with `source: "git"`. The module's rule is that a field that cannot be known is null, never a guess. The sha and dirty flag now come only from a checkout whose top level (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`) is that folder itself; anything else gives null, as a wheel with no repository around it already did. An editable install or a clone still reports its HEAD. `tests/test_provenance_outside_checkout.py` fails on main and passes here.

**fanout from outside the repo.** Local shards run with their cwd at the repo root, but got `--scans` and `--out` exactly as typed. From any other directory, `arrival fanout --scans myscans --out out` failed every attempt of every shard with "no scan for room" (the shard looked for `myscans` under the repo), and with an absolute `--scans` the shards wrote their datasets into the repo's `out/` while fanout waited under the caller's, so it stopped with "wrote no expected shard folder" (exit 2). `LocalBackend` now hands each shard absolute paths; a `--scans` that does not exist in the caller's cwd is still passed as typed, so the shard falls back to `<repo>/scans` as before. The Nebius path and run.json are unchanged. From a temp directory with a copy of hero_kitchen, the same command now runs 2 of 2 shards into a valid 2-episode dataset. Test: `tests/test_fanout_relative_paths.py`, fails on main.

**`doctor` warns when the Token Factory key has no model ids.** The README's live-API setup sets `TOKEN_FACTORY_API_KEY` and three `NEMOTRON_*_MODEL` ids, and `.env.example` leaves the ids blank. With only the key filled in, `doctor` said everything was in place, and `sim --planner vlm` then printed "Token Factory not configured" and planned with the rule-based parser. `doctor` now warns for each blank id the code calls (super for the planner, lightning for the instruction writer, omni for detection) whenever the key is set, in the environment or in `.env`. Ultra is never called, so it is not checked. A warning keeps exit 0. Test: `tests/test_doctor.py::test_token_factory_key_without_model_ids_warns`, fails on main.
**CONFIG: preserve literal quotes inside dotenv values.** The lightweight reader used `strip` with both quote characters, removing every quote at either end rather than one matching outer pair. Values containing a literal opposite quote could change before use. It now removes exactly one matching quote pair after whitespace trimming. Four synthetic-value regressions cover opposite quote characters at one or both ends and reproduce the previous loss; existing environment precedence is unchanged. No website changes.

**Start offsets stay on the counter.** `make_tasks` moves an object's start 0.08 m along its counter (`POSE_OFFSETS`) and never checked that the moved spot was still on the counter. On `u_kitchen_42` the mug stands 0.42 m from the centre of `storage_sink`, which is 1.0 m long, so the -0.08 offset put its centre on the counter's end, next to the dishwasher. `unload_dishwasher` sets the mug down at that spot, and it fell onto the dishwasher top: `eval --room u_kitchen_42 --scans tests/fixtures/scans --families unload_dishwasher --episodes 8 --seed 1 --people 0` got 6 of 8, and the two misses were the two episodes with that offset (mug at z 0.885 instead of 0.95). The same offset put rotated_kitchen's bowl 2.5 cm and u_kitchen_42's cup 2 cm past an edge. An offset now stops 2 cm (`EDGE_GAP`) short of the edge for the object's footprint; a start the twin already put nearer the edge is left where it is. Same command: 8 of 8. hero_kitchen has no start near an edge, and its `make_tasks` output is byte-identical to main (seeds 0 and 3, train and held-out, fixed and mobile base). Tests in `tests/test_any_wall.py`: every start in both fixture kitchens keeps the footprint on its counter, and the unload episode nearest the dishwasher puts the mug back on the counter; both fail on main. `test_start_offsets_run_along_the_counter_whatever_wall_it_is_on` now allows an offset that is shortened at an edge.

**GR00T's own loader reads today's datasets.** The status table said the dataset had not been loaded by GR00T itself since the latest fixes (the mobile base's 11-float rows, the third camera, the validator changes). On a 4-core Linux Codespace with no GPU, Isaac-GR00T `dac54b3` (torch 2.9.0, torchcodec 0.8.0, Ubuntu's FFmpeg 6.1.1) read three `hero_kitchen` datasets written by main `03690dd` at 224 px, 3 episodes each: fixed arm (`counter_to_sink,open_drawer`), mobile base (`counter_to_counter,counter_to_sink --base mobile`) and three cameras (`--exocentric`). New `scripts/check_gr00t_loader.py`, run with GR00T's interpreter, builds GR00T's `LeRobotEpisodeLoader` with the matching config in `configs/gr00t/` and reads every episode: the row count matches the episode length, every video frame decodes to H x W x 3 uint8, every state and action key the config names is present and finite, and every row has an instruction. No problems in any of the three. GR00T's `stats.py` exited 0 on each and wrote `stats.json` and `relative_stats.json`. Without an FFmpeg 4 to 7 runtime every video read fails in torchcodec, which is a machine problem, not a dataset one. No fine-tune or inference ran. Evidence: `docs/results/gr00t-loader-2026-10-27/README.md`.

**The bowl wall is thicker than the gap the closed pads leave.** The procedural hand's pads stop 12 mm apart when they close, and the bowl's octagon wall was 6 mm, so a straight-on pinch never squeezed it. The rim grasp held only when the rim point fell near a corner of the octagon, where the panel crosses the pads at an angle. On `rotated_kitchen` the bowl on counter_w sits nearly straight on: `sim --room rotated_kitchen --scans tests/fixtures/scans --families counter_to_sink --episodes 6 --seed 0` went 2/6, and every miss was the bowl, never lifted (the fingers closed fully with no contact). On the hero kitchen the angle happened to work. `BOWL_WALL` is now 16 mm, wider than the gap the way the plate's 18 mm rim already was. The same command gives 6/6, and the hero kitchen's `sim --families all --episodes 14 --seed 5 --people 2 --res 64` is 14/14 before and after. The hero MJCF hash pinned in `tests/test_fridge_hinge.py` moves to c611053011fbe2d1; only the bowl geoms differ. Tests: `tests/test_rotated_kitchen.py::test_closed_pads_squeeze_a_bowl_wall_and_a_plate_rim` and `test_expert_carries_the_bowl_on_the_west_run` (both start spots), all three fail on main.
**`subset --help` gave a drawer bar that drops clean pulls.** It said the clean expert ends at 0.300, but every clean open_drawer pull ends at 0.2997 m (hero_kitchen, u_kitchen_42 and rotated_kitchen, seed 0), so `--min-drawer-opening 0.3` kept none of them ("the rule keeps none of the 2 episodes"). The help now gives 0.2997 and says 0.28 keeps every clean demo, the bar the eval runs used; `subset.py` and `docs/07-eval.md` give the same number. `test_the_help_threshold_keeps_every_clean_drawer_pull` reads both numbers from the help, runs three clean pulls and checks them; it fails on main.
**A scan.json wall needs an id of its own.** `arrival/scan/bundle.py` checked that every fixture and object has a non-empty id no other fixture or object uses, but read wall ids only for its messages. The sim's MuJoCo model names each wall's box and each object's geom after its id, so an authored scan with two walls of one id, or a wall named like an object, passed validation and then failed the model build of every task (`XML Error: Error: repeated name 'wall_north' in geom`); uploaded, the job got as far as `sim` and failed as `unsupported layout: no task can run in this room`. A wall with no id passed too, and the job failed at `objects` with `KeyError: 'id'` when the twin's layout was written, and was retried twice as a machine fault. A wall id must now be a non-empty string that no other wall and no object has, as fixture and object ids already must be, so such a scan is a `ScanBundleError` when it is loaded (`normalize` for an upload, not retried). A wall may still share an id with a fixture, since a fixture is a MuJoCo body, not a geom, and that scan builds. RoomPlan imports already give walls unique ids and are unchanged. Test: `tests/test_scan_wall_ids.py`, 10 of its 11 cases fail on main.
**`sim --split eval` without `--instructions nemotron` is refused.** `--split` only chooses which generated wordings `--instructions nemotron` draws (the train set or the held-out eval set). With the default template wordings `collect` passed the split on as a label only, so `sim --split eval` ran the train split and wrote `"split": "eval"` into the dataset's provenance and the progress journal: on the hero kitchen, 4 episodes of open_drawer and counter_to_sink with `--split train` and with `--split eval` gave byte-identical `episodes.jsonl`, `tasks.jsonl` and parquet files, the eval one labelled eval. A held-out set built that way is the training data. The template wordings keep their holdout for `eval --holdout` and never go into a sim dataset, so `sim` now exits 2 with "--split eval needs --instructions nemotron" before anything runs, like `--tavily` without `--planner vlm` (#305), and the `--split` help says what it applies to. `--split train`, and either split with `--instructions nemotron`, run as before. Test: `tests/test_sim_split_needs_nemotron.py::test_eval_split_with_template_wordings_is_a_usage_error`, fails on main.
**`fanout` stops its local shard processes when it stops early.** `run_fanout` raises as soon as one shard fails its last attempt, and `cmd_fanout` cancelled the jobs still open only for `--backend nebius`. With the default local backend the other shards' `arrival sim` children kept running after the command had printed "shard 0/2 failed 3 times; finished shards are kept, rerun to resume" and exited. Doing what that says started a second process for the same shard in the same scratch folder while the first was still writing there: in a two-shard run where shard 0 could not resume (a corrupt progress journal) and shard 1 was simulating, the rerun had two shard 1 processes side by side and shard 1's journal listed each of its tasks twice, which the next `--resume` of that shard refuses. `LocalBackend.cancel_open` now terminates the shard processes still running (killing any that do not exit within 10 s), and `cmd_fanout` calls `cancel_open` for every backend, so the status record shows those attempts as failed at `run`, "cancelled when the fan-out stopped", as it already did on Nebius. `tests/test_fanout_stops_local_shards.py` fails on main and passes here.
**Top-level CLI exposes `arrival retarget` with SE(3) physics and seed replay.** PR #356 added native physical SE(3) retargeting (`arrival.generate.se3_retarget`) and S20 added seed-replay retargeting (`arrival.generate.retarget`), but both were orphaned from the main CLI (`arrival`). `python -m arrival retarget` now provides a first-class command dispatching to either generator via `--method {se3,seed_replay}` (defaulting to native physical `se3`). It supports `--room`, `--scans`, `--out`, `--attempts`, `--res`, `--seed`, `--family`, `--source` and `--baseline`, validates numerical bounds and method-specific flags, handles scans resolution with `resolve_scans(scans)`, outputs report summaries, and exits with standard code 2 on usage errors. 5 new unit tests in `tests/test_cli.py` verify dispatch, numerical bounds, usage errors, and method validation.
**README status rows caught up with the code.** Two rows of the status table said less than the repo does. "VLA client tested offline" was stale: `eval --policy vla` has scored GR00T checkpoints through a live GR00T server (docs/ref/POL-3.md), which the eval row above it already says. "MimicGen, splats, Cosmos, Nebius scale-out ❌ Not built yet" was stale too: seed-demo replay (DAT-4a) and object-centric SE(3) retargeting (G7) multiply demos in physics, `arrival/render/splat.py` composites by depth, the Cosmos restyle client exists (SIM-7a) and the Nebius fan-out is built. The row now names each piece and how far it was run: none at scale, upstream MimicGen only probed, no Gaussian splat trained, Cosmos only against a local mock, Nebius only against in-process fakes. No code changed.
**Architecture doc: where each Nebius step stands now.** `docs/03-architecture.md` still described the MVP night ("Tonight none of them are called"; Restyle, Fine-tune and People "Not built"). Its Nebius mapping keeps that column, renamed "MVP night", and gains "Now (Oct 27)": the omni, super and lightning models are called live through Token Factory (scan-frame detection, the planner, wordings), the ultra role is configured but never called, the Cosmos client and the Nebius fan-out exist but ran only against a mock and in-process fakes, and the GR00T fine-tunes ran on a 96 GB GPU on Cosmos, not Nebius. The "later boxes" paragraph says the same. Docs only.
**A Token Factory reply with no text says so.** `token_factory.complete` returned `json.load(resp)["choices"][0]["message"]["content"]` unchecked. A reply with empty `choices` raised `IndexError: list index out of range`, an `error` object instead of choices raised `KeyError: 'choices'`, and `content: null` (what an OpenAI-compatible endpoint sends when the token limit runs out mid-reasoning, `finish_reason: "length"`) came back as `None`, so the wordings fallback recorded `TypeError: expected string or bytes-like object, got 'NoneType'` and the planner an `AttributeError` on `None.index`. None of those says the model answered with nothing. `complete` now raises `ValueError: Token Factory reply from <model> has no message text (finish_reason=...)` with the start of the reply; it is still not an `OSError`, so the planner and the detector do not mark the endpoint unreachable. `tests/test_token_factory_reply.py` stubs `urlopen` with the three replies and checks the wordings `error` field; four of its five cases fail on main.
**TWN-1a measures a real RoomPlan kitchen's base cabinets.** The accuracy harness took counter pieces only from fixtures typed `counter`, but Apple's RoomPlan has no counter category: a real export's base cabinets come in as `storage`. `tests/fixtures/roomplan/kitchen_real_01.json`, the one real kitchen capture, failed with `no scanned counter to measure`, and so would any room landed by `intake_scan.py` with its required tape measurements; `u_kitchen_42` (sink set into storage) failed the same way. A floor-standing storage cabinet at counter height (`is_counter_host`, the rule that already lets one hold a sink) is now a counter piece, unless it stands more than 30 cm from every wall, where the block is an island and is not measured; a tall pantry or a wall cabinet is still left out. kitchen_real_01 now reads a 2.29 m run (two cabinets with the dishwasher between them) at 0.91 m; hero_kitchen and rotated_kitchen read as before. Tests in `tests/test_lidar_accuracy.py`: the real export's run and height, and a storage island and a tall pantry left out; both fail on main.
**check_layout supports --scans and direct RoomPlan exports, and missing rooms hint existing scans.** `scripts/check_layout.py` only supported `scans/` and crashed on test rooms like `rotated_kitchen` without an existing twin directory. It now accepts `--scans` (with `resolve_scans` fallback to repo scans) and a new `--roomplan` option to compile and check raw RoomPlan JSON files directly. When a room is not found, both `check_layout.py` and `arrival/generate/se3_retarget.py` report the rooms that exist under `--scans` and suggest `--scans tests/fixtures/scans` for test fixtures, matching PR #366. Mutually exclusive input validation is enforced for twin directories, `--room`, and `--roomplan`. 4 new tests in `tests/test_layout_check.py` and a new CLI test in `tests/test_se3_retarget.py` verify `--scans`, `--roomplan`, mutual exclusivity, and room hint output.
**An empty variable no longer hides the `.env` value.** `config.get` treats an empty variable as unset and calls `load_dotenv` to fill it, but the reader skipped every name already in `os.environ`, empty or not. With `export TAVILY_API_KEY=` in a shell profile, or a CI secret that is not defined (`env: TAVILY_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.X }}` gives an empty string), the key in `.env` was never read: `get` returned `None` and the run fell back offline, while `arrival doctor` reported the key "set in .env". The reader now fills a variable that is unset or empty; one with a value still wins. `tests/test_config.py::test_an_empty_variable_does_not_hide_the_dotenv_value` fails on main and passes here.
**Mock policy server refuses a chunk shorter than one step.** `python -m arrival.policy_server.mock_server --chunk 0` started and then answered every `/act` with HTTP 500 ("chunk must be [T,8] ..."), and `--chunk -1` was worse: `np.tile` raised inside the handler, so the server printed a traceback and closed the connection, and `VLAClient` saw `RemoteDisconnected` with no reason. `MockPolicyServer` now raises `ValueError` for a chunk below 1 before it binds a port (as it already did for an unknown mode), and the CLI exits 2 with "--chunk must be at least 1 step". `tests/test_policy_server.py::test_mock_refuses_a_chunk_shorter_than_one_step` covers 0 and -1 for both.
**SE(3) retarget refuses a source from another room before writing anything.** `python -m arrival.generate.se3_retarget --room u_kitchen_42 --scans tests/fixtures/scans --source <hero_kitchen meta/se3_source.json>` stopped with `error: 'counter_a'` (a `KeyError` from `SimEnv`), after `generate` had already created `datasets/u_kitchen_42_se3_retarget/meta/se3_source.json` with no `info.json` beside it. u_kitchen_42 has a `bowl_1` but no `counter_a` or `sink_1`. A resized source object took the same late path: `RetargetPolicy.reset` raised its `ValueError` after the dataset directory existed. New `check_twin` runs right after `validate_source` and names what is missing ("the source needs counter_a, sink_1, which u_kitchen_42 does not have") or says the object is a different catalog item or size, before any output. `tests/test_se3_retarget.py::test_source_from_another_room_is_refused_before_any_output` covers both cases; it fails on main.
**A door or window on a curved wall names the chord it sits on.** `_walls` splits a curved wall into chords named `<id>`, `<id>_2`, `<id>_3`, ..., but a door, window or opening whose `parentIdentifier` was that wall still got `wall_id` = `<id>`, the first chord. `portal_wall` in the sim takes that chord and `portal_xml` clips the portal to its length, so a portal further round the arc was put on the wrong line or not drawn: a window 72° round a 4 m quarter-circle wall got `wall_id` `north`, whose chord passes 3.8 m from it, and `portal_xml` returned no geoms. The normalizer now keeps each wall's chords together and gives the portal the chord nearest its centre (`north_5`, 2 cm away). A straight wall is one chord, so its portals keep the same `wall_id` as before. Test: `test_portal_on_a_curved_wall_names_the_chord_it_sits_on` in `tests/test_roomplan_coverage.py`, which fails on main.
**validate: `--json` alone writes its report.** `arrival validate DIR --json q.json` without `--episodes` skipped the per-episode pass, so it printed `OK`, exited 0 and wrote no `q.json`, even on a dataset with a flagged episode; `--frames` and `--drop` already implied `--episodes`, `--json` did not. It now implies it too, and its help and `docs/REFERENCE.md` say so. On a dataset of six good episodes and one that never moves, the same command now writes the report and exits 1 for the `zero_motion` episode, the same as with `--episodes`. Test: `test_cli_json_alone_runs_the_episode_checks_and_writes_the_report` in `tests/test_quality.py`, which fails on main.
