**The planner prompt lists the room's families (Oct 27).** `VLMPlanner.plan` offered the model 9 families in a hand-written string, the same in every room, while `_parse_plan` accepted all 15 in `SIM_FAMILIES`. `arrival.sim.tasks.family_fixtures(twin)` now maps each family to the fixtures it acts on (a counter run, tables included, for the counter families; a cabinet with drawers or a door; a table; a stove; a dishwasher; a sink in a counter run for the faucet; a fridge), and `make_tasks` reads its fixture lists from that map: on all three benchmark twins, fixed and mobile base, it makes the same tasks and the same pre-flight report as before. `room_families(twin)` is the families with any such fixture, in `SIM_FAMILIES` order, whatever the base; reach stays in the pre-flight. The planner offers `room_families(twin)` (all 15 when the caller gives no twin), `_parse_plan` rejects a family outside that list like any other rejection (`family_not_offered`), and `collect` passes its twin. The hero kitchen keeps `counter_to_counter` and `open_oven` (`stove_1` is a stove) and drops `move_on_table` and the three dishwasher families; `kitchen_real_01` drops `counter_to_sink`, the faucet and `move_on_table`; the office offers only `counter_to_counter`, `wipe_counter` and `move_on_table` (its table is a worktop), because it has no sink and its STORAGE-01 is not built as a cabinet, so it has no door or drawer. Three live runs on the same requests, `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b` at temperature 0, every call answered, no call errors, no retries, Wilson 95% intervals: the 9-family prompt (the Oct 27 run of record for indirect and unlisted_families, `planner-benchmark-2026-10-27-blind.json` for blind), then the 15 families everywhere (`planner-benchmark-2026-10-27-all-families.json`, kept as the intermediate result), then the room's families (`planner-benchmark-2026-10-27-room-families.json`). Indirect (15, all of the old nine): 15/15 [0.80, 1.00], 10/15 [0.42, 0.85], 14/15 [0.70, 0.99]. Under the global list two of the five new misses were `move_on_table` in the hero kitchen, which has no table: a family impossible in that room ("Make some room by shifting the bowl down the counter", "The mug is dirty, get it ready to be washed"). Under the room's list both are right again, and so are the other two that had moved; the one miss is "I want to grab a plate from the cupboard under the counter", a drawer instead of a door, which was also missed in the earlier Oct 27 run under the 9-family prompt. Unlisted families (20): 0/20 [0.00, 0.16], 19/20 [0.76, 0.99], 18/20 [0.70, 0.97]; the room list's two misses are "Fold the dishwasher door down" (the reply named `close_dishwasher`, which is no family, and was rejected) and "Put the apple in the dishwasher rack" (a drawer). Blind (30): 12/30 [0.25, 0.58], 20/30 [0.49, 0.81], 20/30 [0.49, 0.81]; its 18 rows for the old nine stayed 12/18 [0.44, 0.84] in all three runs, not always on the same rows; its 12 others went 0/12, 8/12, 8/12. The office's three door requests ("I'm looking for a stapler", "We're out of printer paper", "Someone's going to walk into that cabinet door") are not offerable under the room list, since no door is modelled on STORAGE-01; they stay in the blind denominator, are wrong by construction there, and the report counts them (`not_offerable`: 3, `rows[].offerable`). On the 27 offerable blind rows: 12/27, 19/27, 20/27. All 33 rows asking for one of the old nine (indirect plus blind): 27/33 [0.66, 0.91], 22/33 [0.50, 0.80], 26/33 [0.62, 0.89]. These are single runs of 15 to 33 rows with overlapping intervals; indirect was 13/15 under the 9-family prompt in the earlier Oct 27 run, so they show the spread and say neither that the global list was a regression nor that the room list fixes it. Temperature 0 does not make the endpoint deterministic: the blind set holds "I'm done with this plate." twice, w1's and w2's, in the same room with the same label, and in one run the two copies got `load_dishwasher` and `counter_to_sink`; across the three runs the pair went (sink, sink), (dishwasher, sink), (dishwasher, dishwasher). The benchmark passes each inventory's twin and records `rows[].offered_families`; the report's `planner_system_prompt` is only the first call's. The planner's cache key now includes the families offered, so a plan cached under another offer (the 9-family prompt, or another room's list) never answers, and caches written before this change are ignored rather than cleared.
**Known limits, measured: the 28-episode run at 224 px, and the Menagerie arm with people (Oct 27, main `897aa05`, Linux Codespace, MuJoCo 3.17, OSMesa).** The README listed two runs as never done. `sim --room hero_kitchen --families all --episodes 28 --people 1 --seed 5 --res 224`: 28 of 28, 0 dropped, 0 human contacts, and `validate --episodes` flags 0 of 28 (unreadable, nonfinite, zero_motion, stuck_arm, length_outlier, joint_at_limit), in 971 s; the 64 px result after #152 holds at 224 px, so that line leaves the known limits. `sim --room hero_kitchen --arm menagerie --families all --episodes 14 --people 2 --res 64`: seed 0, 12 of 14 (misses: `open_oven`, one `counter_to_sink`); seed 3, 13 of 14 (miss: `open_oven`); 0 human contacts and 0 flagged episodes in both. With the real Franka, `open_oven` missed in both runs; it is reported here, not fixed.
**Teleop logs the largest IK error of an attempt, not the last.** Each row of `<room>_teleop_attempts.jsonl` has `max_ik_error_m`, but it held the error of the last IK solve only. Driving the target 1.6 m up out of reach and back down, then giving up, logged 0.5274 m while the worst solve in the attempt was 2.2823 m, so an attempt that spent time out of reach could look fine. `TeleopController` now keeps the largest error of the attempt beside the latest one, and the row writes that. `test_max_ik_error_is_the_largest_of_the_attempt_not_the_last` in `tests/test_teleop.py` fails on main.
**A Nebius API reply that is not JSON is a `ShardError`.** `NebiusBackend._api` turned HTTP errors into `ShardError` but passed a 200 body straight to `json.loads`. An HTML page with status 200 (a proxy or gateway in the way) raised `JSONDecodeError`, which nothing catches: inside `cancel_open`, which `arrival fanout` runs on the way out so remote jobs do not outlive it, the first garbled cancel reply stopped the loop, so the remaining open jobs were never cancelled and were not listed as "cancel them by hand". A JSON reply that is not an object (`[]`) raised `AttributeError` in `poll`. Both are now a `ShardError` naming the call and quoting the start of the body (redacted). `cancel_open` then reports that one job and cancels the rest. Test: `tests/test_nebius.py::test_a_reply_that_is_not_a_json_object_is_a_shard_error`, fails on main. Like the rest of the Nebius backend, this was tested only against the in-process fakes.
**`doctor` fails the disk check when a file sits on the `--out` path.** `check_disk` walked up from a missing output folder to the nearest path that exists and checked free space and write access there, without asking whether that path is a folder. With `arrival doctor --out README.md/out` it found `README.md`, reported "756.1 GiB free where README.md/out will be written" as ok and exited 0, though no folder can ever be created under a file. It now fails and names the file in the way. Test: `tests/test_doctor.py::test_check_disk_fails_when_a_parent_on_the_path_is_a_file`, fails on main.
**doctor reports a `.env` key the way `config.get` reads it.** `arrival doctor` read the `.env` files itself and called any key with a value there "set in .env (read by the module that needs it)". `config.get`, which every module reads its settings through, does not use that value in three cases: with `ARRIVAL_DOTENV=0` it reads no `.env` file; a variable the environment sets, even to an empty value, is never filled from `.env`, and `get` treats the empty value as unset; and the first `.env` line that names a key decides, so an empty `KEY=` in the cwd's `.env` hides the value in the repo root's. In each case doctor said `[ok]` while the module got no key: with `TAVILY_API_KEY` in `.env`, `TAVILY_API_KEY= python -m arrival doctor` printed `[ok  ] TAVILY_API_KEY: set in .env (read by the module that needs it)` and `config.get("TAVILY_API_KEY")` returned `None` (the same with `ARRIVAL_DOTENV=0`). Doctor now applies the same rules: a key `.env` would not supply is `info` "not set", naming `ARRIVAL_DOTENV=0` when that is the reason, and an empty variable that hides a `.env` value is a `warn`. The Token Factory model-id warnings follow the key the same way. `tests/test_doctor_dotenv.py` asks doctor and then `config.get` in each case; three of its four tests fail on main.
**cost leaves the scripted placeholder out of the teleop comparison.** `arrival demo` writes `out/reports/<room>.json` with `physically_valid: false` (scripted placeholder motion, not to be fine-tuned on), next to the `<room>_sim.json` that `arrival sim` writes for the same room. `arrival cost` read both as report rows, and with a room-level teleop baseline (`"task": "all_tasks"`) it added every room total of the baseline's room to the generated side. After `arrival demo --episodes 4` and `arrival sim --episodes 2 --people 1 --res 64` into one `--out`, the comparison counted 6 generated demos at 8.525 s each and a 7.22x teleop-over-generated ratio, although only the 2 physics-checked demos count, at 25.45 s each and 2.42x. The task-level comparison against the shipped P3 baseline was not affected, because the demo report has no per-family rows. Rows from a report that says `physically_valid: false` are now marked `placeholder`, listed as `report (placeholder)` in the table and left out of the comparison. `tests/test_cost_placeholder.py` writes the demo report with `arrival.eval.report.build_report` next to a sim report; both of its tests fail on main.
**PPL-2: mobile walkers yield to arm links.** Mobile and fixed bases now use the same minimum clearance over the pedestal, all seven arm links, hand and fingers. Mobile counter-transfer crossing lanes sit 15 cm farther behind the drive route so the walker and the carrying arm do not wait indefinitely for each other; fixed-base paths are unchanged. The headless extended-arm regression covers both base modes, including a translated and turned mobile base, positive clearance where the previous mobile subset still allowed walking, and stop/resume behavior. `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_people.py tests/test_mobile_base.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` passed all 26 cases with rendering (49.06 s). `ARRIVAL_DOTENV=0 .venv/bin/python -m arrival sim --base mobile --families counter_to_counter,open_door --episodes 2 --people 2 --res 32 --seed 3 --out out/ppl2-mobile` produced 2/2 successful physics-checked episodes, zero human contacts, and a valid dataset (minimum clearances 0.2521 m and 0.3074 m). `PYTEST_ADDOPTS='--no-render -rs' make PY=.venv/bin/python test` passed with 1488 passed and 254 skipped; the offline demo and packaging smoke also passed. These are scripted simulation checks, not real-robot measurements.

**Doctor and config agree on empty variables again.** #397 made `config.load_dotenv` fill a variable that is set but empty, and #407 made `arrival doctor` report a `.env` key as set only when `config.get` would use it, written against the old rule. Merged together, doctor warned "an empty value in the environment hides the one in .env" for a key `config.get` now returns. Doctor now follows #397: an empty variable or an empty `.env` line is filled by the next file, and `ARRIVAL_DOTENV=0` only turns off the default files, as in `load_dotenv(paths)`. `tests/test_doctor_dotenv.py` states the new rule.

**Cosmos Transfer2.5 ran for real.** Hosted Cosmos endpoints were not available to our NVIDIA account, so we rented one RTX PRO 6000 (96 GB) on RunPod and ran `nvidia/Cosmos-Transfer2.5-2B` with edge control on three 93-frame sim episodes: open_fridge and open_drawer in the scanned `kitchen_real_01`, and counter_to_sink in `hero_kitchen`, with walkers. About 8 minutes of generation per clip; NVIDIA's guardrail passed all three. Every output frame passed the restyle stage's own edge check (`check_frame`, correlation 0.74 to 0.83 against 0.6). The side-by-side video, the specs and the per-clip numbers are in `docs/results/cosmos-transfer-2026-10-27/`. The dataset run through `arrival/render/variation.py` is still open, and the GR00T fine-tunes did not use restyled frames.

**`sim`, `eval` and `fanout` read a room that has only a RoomPlan export.** A phone capture lands as `scans/<room>/roomplan.json` (CAP-1, `intake_scan.py`), and the "rooms under" hint lists such a room, but `sim`, `eval` and `research` loaded only `scan.json`: `sim --room kitchen_real_01 --scans <dir with kitchen_real_01/roomplan.json>` printed "no scan for room 'kitchen_real_01' … (rooms under …: …, kitchen_real_01, …)" and exited 2, and `fanout --backend local` refused the room the same way. The only way in was to run `demo --from-roomplan` first so a twin sat in `out/twins`. `_load_twin` now builds a room with no `scan.json` from its `roomplan.json` exactly as `demo --from-roomplan` does (`cli.roomplan_twin`: normalize, place the catalog objects with seed 0, note the seed), still preferring a cached twin that is newer than the export; the fan-out check accepts either file. Measured: `sim --room kitchen_real_01 --families all --episodes 3 --res 32 --people 0` straight from the export, 3 of 3. `tests/test_cli.py::test_a_room_with_only_a_roomplan_export_is_built_like_demo_from_roomplan` fails on main (`FileNotFoundError … scan.json`) and passes here.
**G6 / TWN-4: twin layouts built in real RoboCasa.** On the 4-core Codespace (no GPU), RoboCasa 1.0.1 (`2ce1aec`) and robosuite master (`5ce6643`) from their own repos, with only the texture and fixture asset packs (1.9 GB unpacked), built the hero kitchen and the RoomPlan `kitchen_real_01` twin (both written by `python -m arrival demo` on main `897aa05`) through RoboCasa's `KitchenArena` and compiled them to MuJoCo with robosuite's `ManipulationTask`, no robot. `layout.yaml` as written loads without an error but builds a different room: RoboCasa's `Wall` and `Floor` take half extents in their own rotated frame plus a `wall_side`, so every wall landed 2.4 m off its line and the floor came out 8 x 6 m for a 4 x 3 m room; a RoboCasa fixture faces -y at `z_rot` 0 while the twin's faces +y at yaw 0, so all 11 fixtures faced their wall; the sink is an interior fixture that only its counter places, so it floated 14.5 cm low in a solid counter; and `pos` is a body origin, which put the stove 15 cm into the floor. New `arrival/twin/robocasa_layout.py` turns the contract `layout.yaml` (unchanged, still checked by `scripts/check_layout.py`) into RoboCasa's form without importing RoboCasa: walls with `wall_side`, half extents and the interior face as `pos`; a half-extent floor; one group per facing with `group_z_rot` = yaw + pi; each sink as its counter's `interior_obj` at the twin's spot; body origins moved by RoboCasa's `origin_offset`; oblique walls and unknown types as explicit boxes. New `scripts/check_robocasa_layout.py` builds both forms, reads the offsets on a first build, and compares RoboCasa's own exterior box of each fixture and the collision box of each wall with the twin. With styles 1 and 5, both kitchens build with all 11 fixtures and 8 walls: every fixture centre within 2.5 cm horizontally (the counters, whose RoboCasa box leaves out the 5 cm overhang; everything else within 1 cm), walls exact, fronts matching, and no overlap the twin does not already have (kitchen_real_01's cabinet backs sit 1.3 to 2.4 cm in the wall, as in the scan). The sink's height is RoboCasa's, flush with the counter top and 5.5 cm below the hero twin's sink box; the script reports it and does not count it. `tests/test_robocasa_layout.py` checks the conversion offline. Not done: the RoboCasa form is not written into the twin folder, the offsets need one RoboCasa build per style, and no robot, task or render ran in the built rooms. Evidence: `docs/results/robocasa-layout-2026-10-27/README.md`.
**The real Franka opens the oven.** With `--arm menagerie`, `open_oven` never moved the oven door: 0 of 6 over seeds 0, 3 and 5 with 0 and 2 people (the door's joint stayed at 0.0 rad), where the procedural arm opens it to 1.50 rad every time. The expert picks the flap tilt whose whole chain has the smallest IK residual, and for the real arm that was the steepest one (1.35 rad). IK reaches the bar there, but IK does not see collisions: a step trace shows the real hand's body (`stove_1|hand` contact) stopping the eef 3.4 cm short of the bar, so the pads close on nothing. Forced to each tilt over the same 6 episodes, 0, 0.35 and 0.7 rad open it 6 of 6 and 1.35 rad 0 of 6. `menagerie.HAND` now lists the oven's tilts without the steepest (`flap_tilts`), and the expert takes the arm's list when it has one; the procedural arm and the dishwasher keep every tilt, so their plans are unchanged. `sim --room hero_kitchen --arm menagerie --families all --episodes 14 --people 2 --res 64`: seed 0, 12 to 14 of 14; seed 3, 13 to 14 of 14; 0 flagged, 0 human contacts. (Seed 0's `counter_to_sink` miss in the earlier run did not recur on today's main either; this change does not touch that path.) `tests/test_menagerie.py::test_menagerie_arm_opens_the_oven[0, 3]` and `test_only_the_real_hand_narrows_the_flap_tilts` fail on main and pass here (17 passed).
**The study's desk gets move_on_table tasks.** `sim --families all` on the study (`tests/fixtures/roomplan/study.json`) wrote an empty dataset at fixed and mobile base, seeds 1 and 7: every family was skipped, with no pre-flight reason. The twin compiled and `place_default_objects` put a book and a cup on the desk, so two things dropped them. First, the desk's open long edge is the only one (the other is 0.10 m from the east wall), and its chair stands on the midway stand spot (0.00 m clear); `table_move` returned None there, so the variation never reached the pre-flight, whose reach planner (`find_shift`) stands 0.25 m further along the edge, 0.18 m clear of the chair. `table_move` now keeps the first on-table, unoccupied target even when its base check fails, and the pre-flight decides. Second, with that spot the book was still never lifted: the desk runs along world Y (yaw -90 degrees), the expert pinches across the run, and the book was placed square to world X, so the fingers met its 18 cm length (wider than the 10 cm opening) and stopped 2 cm high on the cover, missing by 0.43 m. `place_default_objects` now lays an object along a run closer to world Y (round objects are unchanged). The study now gives move_on_table 24/24 at fixed and mobile base, seeds 1 and 7, with 0 human contacts (was 0 episodes). The kitchens' MJCF and tasks are byte-identical. The office's MJCF is unchanged; its tasks gain the cup, stood 0.10 m beside its chair, and it still gives 24/24 (fixed s1, 9 cup episodes; mobile s7, 10). `test_desk_behind_a_chair_is_worked_from_beside_the_chair` in `tests/test_non_kitchen.py` (fixed and mobile) replaces the test that asserted the desk got no task.
**The fridge visitor keeps out of the door the robot works too.** On rotated_kitchen, `sim --families all --episodes 24 --people 2 --seed 1` lost both `close_door` tasks (`sim_close_door_0004`, `sim_close_door_0014`) to human contact, with `--base fixed` and `--base mobile` alike. The fridge stands next to `counter_w`'s door. In both episodes the person sent to the fridge stood at their stand point, holding the fridge open, 0.55 m from that door's hinge (0.62 m from the base, so just past `CLEAR`). The robot's door keep-out disc has a 1.12 m radius. Reaching round the open door, the hand went from 0.35 m clearance to -0.19 m and -0.20 m at step 11, and stayed inside the person until about step 62. The person was standing still (paused at the fridge and then held by the yield), so walker yielding could not help. L28 kept every walker's stops and legs out of `SimEnv._door_keepout`, except the fridge visitor's ("they stand at the fridge, not the counter"). Here the fridge is at the counter. `plan_fridge_visit` now takes the same `keep` discs and plans no visit when the walk from s1 to s2 comes inside one. The base check (`CLEAR`) and the rest of the plan are unchanged. Only visits whose stand points are inside a keep disc are dropped. Across hero_kitchen, rotated_kitchen, u_kitchen_42 and kitchen_real_01, fixed and mobile, seeds 1 and 7, 24 tasks each, that happens only in rotated_kitchen at a `counter_w` task: 7 tasks at seed 1 and 3 at seed 7, for each base. `build_mjcf` and `make_tasks` do not touch this code. Seed 1 after the change: fixed 22/24 → 24/24 and mobile 21/24 → 23/24 (`open_fridge_0007` misses before and after), human contacts 2 → 0 in both. The two `close_door` episodes now have a minimum distance of 0.19 m. Rotated seed 7 fixed stays at 24/24 with no contacts. Hero kitchen seed 1 fixed is byte-for-byte the same per task. Test: `test_no_fridge_visit_inside_the_door_the_robot_closes` in `tests/test_any_wall.py`, which fails on main (the visit is planned, and the episode makes contact at -0.19 m).
**The parked mobile base no longer turns under the wipe strokes.** On u_kitchen_42 with `--base mobile`, `wipe_counter` missed all four attempts at seeds 1 and 7 (outcome `miss`), and the fixed base wiped the same spot in the same room. The base parks where the fixed one does (0.28 m out from storage_a, facing it) and the IK error was 3e-8 m, but the base's yaw servo (kp 3000) was exactly as stiff as joint 1 (kp 3000), the joint that swings the arm for each stroke. Each stroke turned the base up to 0.053 rad the other way (28 mm at the sponge, plus 17 mm of slide on x), and the sponge travelled 0.27 m pressed to the top against the 0.4 m the check needs (0.62 m fixed). The yaw servo is now ten times stiffer, kp 30000 with kv 630 for the same damping ratio: the base turns 5.4 mrad and the sponge wipes 0.49 to 0.58 m. x and y keep their gains: ten times those as well wiped 0.60 to 0.72 m, but changed how the base tracks a counter_to_counter drive, and that put hero_kitchen's seed-1 episode 0023 into a stand-off with a walker it does not meet on main. The fixed-base scene and every room's task list are unchanged; only the mobile scene's `b_yaw` line differs. Mobile sweeps with two people: u_kitchen_42 22/24 to 24/24 at seeds 1 and 7; hero_kitchen 23/24 and rotated_kitchen 21/24 at seed 1, the same families as before. `tests/test_mobile_base.py::test_parked_mobile_base_does_not_turn_under_the_wipe_strokes` fails on main (0.272 m) and passes here.
**A parked mobile base no longer waits for people, and a driving one waits only for those on its route.** The scripted expert held the arm whenever the base pose read off the plan's base target and a person stood within `STOP_CLEAR` (0.7 m) on that side. The base servos sag 0.2 to 0.9 mm while the arm pulls, so on `--base mobile` every step of a task that never drives looked like a drive: in kitchen_real_01 (the iPhone scan) a walker standing near the stove froze the arm for 295 of 400 steps with the oven door at 0.16 rad, and `open_fridge` and `open_dishwasher` ran out of steps the same way (with no people, or on the fixed base, all three passed). A drive is now the plan moving the base target between steps. That exposed the half-plane "ahead" test: a walker in its crossing lane 0.69 m beside the route, at 84 deg to the drive, held hero `counter_to_counter_0023` for 155 steps (on main the 49-step wait in `test_people_keep_off_the_drive_route` was also for a walker 0.686 to 0.692 m off the line). Driving, the robot reaches 0.35 m either side of the base centre and a torso is at most 0.142 m, so the base now waits only for a person ahead and within `DRIVE_LANE` (0.6 m) of the drive line. Mobile sweeps (`--families all --episodes 24 --people 2 --res 32`): kitchen_real_01 seed 1 20/24 to 23/24, seed 7 16/22 (interrupted) to 20/24, hero_kitchen seed 1 23/24 to 24/24, rotated_kitchen seed 1 21/24 to 22/24 (its `open_fridge` miss); the fixed base is untouched. Still open in kitchen_real_01: at seed 1 `counter_to_counter_0022` stalls on a walker standing still on the route (0.40 m off the line, 0.30 m ahead; it yields to the arm while the base waits for it, 238 steps); at seed 7 `counter_to_counter_0011` is held 248 steps, `0000` and `0022` end with the cup on the floor (`0022` with a human contact, the same on main), and `open_oven_0018` (both bases) starts with a walker 0.20 m from the base centre: with no free strip in front of the stove the walkers fall back to `free_path`, whose footprints leave out the robot. `tests/test_mobile_base.py` drives a parked base with a ring of people 0.5 m around it (held 151 steps, door shut on main; held 0, door open now) and checks that a person on the route holds the drive and one 0.65 m beside it does not.
**check_layout: an input it cannot read is a usage error, not a failed layout.** `scripts/check_layout.py` exits 1 when the layout fails the check and, since #294, 2 for a missing room or twin folder. An input that exists but cannot be used still exited 1, with a traceback: `--room` on a `scan.json` the scan checks refuse (`units must be 'meters', got 'feet'`), `--roomplan` on an export the normalizer refuses, and a twin folder whose `twin.json` is not JSON or whose `layout.yaml` is not YAML. A caller could not tell those from a layout with a defect. They now exit 2 with one line that names the file, for example `check_layout.py: error: scans/bad_room/scan.json: units must be 'meters', got 'feet'`. `tests/test_layout_check_bad_input.py` covers the four inputs; all fail on main.
**A comment after a `.env` value is no longer part of the value.** `config.load_dotenv` kept everything after `=`, so `GR00T_POLICY_URL=http://gpu-box:8000  # the A100 box` gave the URL `http://gpu-box:8000  # the A100 box`, and `TAVILY_API_KEY="tvly-..." # from the console` kept both quotes and the comment, because the quote pair was only stripped when the line ended in a quote. python-dotenv and `source .env` read both lines as the value plus a comment, and `arrival doctor` still reported the key as set. An unquoted value now ends where whitespace and `#` begin, a quoted value ends at its closing quote when only a comment follows, and a `#` with no space before it (`http://cosmos:9000/#frag`) or inside quotes is kept. `tests/test_dotenv_inline_comment.py` covers both cases; both tests fail on the old parser.
**Eval table: the totals sit under their own columns.** The `total` row that `arrival eval` prints left 24 blanks after the Wilson interval where the family rows use 17 (two spaces, then the 7-wide `sim_s` and 8-wide `wall_s` columns). Every total after it moved 7 characters right: the human-contact total stood under `min_d_m` and the arm-contact total under `arm_mm`, where "3" reads as a distance or 3 mm of penetration, and nothing stood under `contact` or `arm_hit`. The padding is now 17. `eval --room hero_kitchen --families open_drawer --episodes 1 --people 0 --res 64` prints the same numbers, each under its header. Test: `tests/test_eval_table_totals.py`, fails on main.
**render_rooms names a room's roomplan.json after its folder.** `scripts/intake_scan.py` lands a phone scan as `scans/<room>/roomplan.json`, and `scripts/render_rooms.py` named a `.json` argument by its stem, so `python scripts/render_rooms.py scans/kitchen_a/roomplan.json scans/office_b/roomplan.json` printed `out/renders/roomplan_top.png` twice, exited 0 and left one PNG: the office plan, drawn over the kitchen's. A file named `roomplan.json` is now named and given its room id as its folder would be (`kitchen_a_roomplan_top.png`, room `kitchen_a`); any other export keeps its stem (`office.json` is still `office_top.png`). `tests/test_render_rooms_names.py` renders two rooms' exports, compares the file form with the folder form and keeps the stem rule; its first two tests fail on main.
**scan_accuracy: a room folder with no scan is an error line, not a traceback.** `scripts/scan_accuracy.py` measures every room folder that holds a `measurements.json`. A folder with the tape values but neither `scan.json` nor `roomplan.json` (the export not copied in yet, or saved under another name) raised `FileNotFoundError` out of `bundle.load_room`, which the script did not catch, so it stopped at that room with a traceback and every room after it went unchecked. That room now prints `ERROR no scan.json or roomplan.json in <folder>` and counts as failing (exit 1, as for any room that cannot be measured), and the other rooms are measured as before. Tests: `tests/test_scan_accuracy_no_scan.py`, fail on main.
**A room is named after its scans folder in sim, eval and retarget too.** `bundle.load_room` kept the `room_id` written inside `scans/<room>/scan.json`, while `demo`, `summary` and the upload worker name the room after its folder. A room started as a copy of `scans/hero_kitchen` still says `"room_id": "hero_kitchen"`, so `arrival sim --room my_kitchen --families wipe_counter --episodes 1 --people 0 --res 64` reported `dataset: out/datasets/hero_kitchen_sim`, exited 0 and replaced the hero kitchen's 2-episode counter_to_sink/open_drawer dataset (and its report and progress journal) with my_kitchen's 1 wipe_counter episode, while `out/datasets/my_kitchen_sim` never appeared and the web page for my_kitchen said no simulation dataset. eval, research, retarget and teleop took the same name, and `demo` wrote it into `out/twins/my_kitchen/twin.json`, which sim reads next. `load_room` now gives the bundle the folder's name and keeps a different file `room_id` as `source_room_id`, as `load_upload` does; a scan in its own folder loads unchanged. `tests/test_scan_room_id_from_folder.py` loads a copied room, builds its twin the way sim and demo do, and checks an unchanged room; the first two fail on main.
**Summary: the tape column says FAIL when the twin does not match the tape.** `arrival summary` printed the largest error over the dimensions the twin has a value for, and nothing else. A room whose scan has no sink (RoomPlan missed it) got "0.0 cm max" in summary.md while summary.json said `"ok": false` and `"sink_width_m": null`, and a counter 6 cm longer than the tape got "6.0 cm max" with no sign that the tolerance is 5 cm. A failed comparison now reads "0.0 cm max, FAIL: sink_width_m not in the twin" or "6.0 cm max, FAIL: over the 5.0 cm tolerance" (both reasons when both hold); a passing one reads as before. Tests: `tests/test_summary_tape_verdict.py`, fail on main.
**The room page warns about the placeholder only when there is one.** The box at the top of `/rooms/<room>` ("arrival_physically_valid: false ... Do not fine-tune on it while this flag is false") reads the flag of the scripted placeholder in `out/datasets/<room>`, which `arrival demo` writes. A room whose only dataset was `out/datasets/<room>_sim` from `arrival sim` still got the box, so after `arrival sim --episodes 2 --people 0 --res 64` the hero kitchen page said its motion was a scripted placeholder that must not be fine-tuned on, directly above its own headline "2 episodes, 100% success rate, 0 human contacts, physically valid: true"; `arrival export-site` copied the same page. The box is now left out when the sim dataset exists and the placeholder does not; it stays while the placeholder exists and on a room with no dataset yet. `tests/test_web_room_placeholder_box.py` covers the three cases; the sim-only one fails on main.
**Floor walks keep clear of the robot base.** When there is no free strip in front of the fixture the robot works at, people walk free loops over the whole floor, with no counter stop. Every task in kitchen_real_01 (our iPhone scan) and in office falls back to this. Those loops were routed round the fixture footprints only. At seed 7, kitchen_real_01's open_oven started person_0 0.199 m from the base centre: -0.078 m to the base at step 0 on both bases, before the arm moved. Across those two rooms at seeds 1 and 7 on both bases, 49 of 384 walkers started within CLEAR (0.6 m) of the base, and the closest path came 0.045 m from its centre. The loop now also keeps out of the base, stretched along a mobile base's drive. The walker's centre stays CLEAR from it, the rule the counter-stop loop's free points and the fridge stand points already follow. That made two gaps show up in the small real kitchen, and both are closed. First, `free_path`'s last resort put a person on the floor centre without checking it (0.52 m from the base); it now picks the nearest free point. Second, the fridge visitor's loop was drawn on the far side of the base; it is now drawn where it can be reached from the fridge, and a leg with no route at CLEAR + r takes one at CLEAR before going straight (straight legs had come 0.40 m from the base). After the fix, 1 of the 384 walkers starts within CLEAR: a standing point at 0.564 m, where no floor is free at the walker's full radius. Walker plans for hero_kitchen, rotated_kitchen, u_kitchen_42 and study are bit-identical (fixed and mobile, seeds 1 and 7), and make_tasks output is unchanged for all six rooms. Sweeps (`sim --families all --episodes 24 --people 2`): kitchen_real_01 fixed s7 went from 23/24 with 1 human contact to 24/24, and mobile s7 from 17/24 with 2 contacts to 21/24 with none (the 3 left are misses with no contact). Mobile s1 went from 20/24 to 24/24. Fixed s1 and office fixed s7 stay at 24/24. hero_kitchen and rotated_kitchen seed 1 are identical task for task on both bases. In kitchen_real_01 more walkers now stand still (5 to 8 fixed and 5 to 10 mobile at seed 7), because little of its 2.8 x 1.7 m floor is left once the base and a door's keep-out are taken out. `tests/test_people.py::test_floor_walk_without_a_counter_stop_keeps_clear_of_the_base` (fixed and mobile, 30 seeds, with fridge visits) and `::test_free_path_never_stands_a_person_in_a_footprint` fail on main.
**Upload: a scan named without Latin letters is still a .json file.** `safe_filename` replaces every run of characters outside `A-Za-z0-9._() -` with `_` and then strips leading `_` and `.`, so `Кухня.json`, `厨房.json` or `مطبخ.json` came out as `json`, with no extension left, and `POST /jobs` answered 415 "the scan must be a .json file" for a valid RoomPlan export. A frames bundle named `кадры.zip` was refused with 415 "frames must be a .zip of a CAP-2a bundle" the same way. A name with no letter or digit left before its extension now keeps that extension on the default's stem (`upload.json`, `upload.zip`); every other name comes out as before. Tests: `tests/test_web_non_latin_filename.py` (the names, and a scan and a frames zip uploaded through the real server), fail on main.
**`sim --resume` refuses a journal written in another twin.** The journal's fingerprint kept the run's settings but nothing about the room, so after a kill, an edit to `scan.json` and `--resume`, the episodes the journal kept came from the old room and the rest from the new one, and `arrival_provenance` in `info.json` named only the edited scan. On main, with hero_kitchen killed during the second of two `counter_to_sink` tasks (`--people 0 --res 32`) and `mug_1` then moved 25 cm in the scan, the resumed dataset's episode 0 differs from episode 0 of an uninterrupted run on the edited scan (first three joint angles at frame 60: `[-0.217, 0.068, -0.105]` against `[-0.209, 0.055, -0.117]`) and its episode 1 matches. The fingerprint now also keeps `twin`, a sha256 of the twin without its `provenance` block (code sha, dirty flag) and its Tavily notes (whose `source` is the live API on the first run and the cache on the next), so a recompile of the same scan resumes and an edited one stops with "different twin" before any task runs. A journal written before this change has no twin hash and resumes as before. Test: `tests/test_resume_other_twin.py`, fails on main.
**TWN-3: draw the open RoomPlan door leaf.** Open wall doors retain their frame and dark recess and now draw a 4 cm wood leaf and its knob swung 90 degrees toward the room's bounding-box centre. `hinge: "right"` selects the wall's upper-u jamb; `"left"` or an omitted hinge selects the lower-u jamb. This is a visual convention, not a measured hinge. Both added geoms are static on the world body with `contype=0` and `conaffinity=0`; no wall collision, articulation, scan normalization or curved-wall portal logic changes. `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_openings_sim.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` passed 25 cases, covering both hinges/default on all four walls, exact scene preservation after stripping portal visuals, bit-for-bit robot/object trajectories, and identical actions, walker positions and safety in a two-episode pick-to-sink comparison. Renders are at most 64 px. `PYTEST_ADDOPTS='-n 4' make PY=.venv/bin/python test` passed with 1886 passed and 13 skipped (345.24 s); lint, docs, the offline demo and packaging smoke passed.
**README Join in: statuses brought up to date.** The team plan's task list and its Pull requests subsection were written around Oct 19 and still called landed work open: SIM-5, MAN-3 and MAN-6 said their PRs (#57, #33 and #36, #25) needed a rebase, POL-3, DAT-1 and DAT-2 waited on a GPU that has since run (G1, G2), TWN-1's test was "not in the repo yet", and CAP-5's licences were unchecked. Each task now ends with Done, Partly done or Open, its to-do id and its PRs, taken from `site/data/todo.json`, this log and the PR states on GitHub. The Pull requests subsection's Oct 19 status line, merge list and rebase table are replaced by one paragraph on what superseded #77, #57, #33, #36, #27 and #25, pointing at the to-do list and `docs/CONTRIBUTORS.md` for credit. `python scripts/check_docs.py` and `python scripts/render_todo_readme.py --check` pass.
**G7 / issue #62: 104 physics-checked counter_to_sink episodes from human teleop seeds.** SE(3) retargeting only had one scripted-expert source, and issue #62's benchmark (at least 100 physics-checked episodes from a seed set, validated, with attempts, drops, throughput and variation) had not run. `python -m arrival retarget --teleop DIR` (`arrival/generate/teleop_seeds.py`) now seeds it with the successful demos of a recorded teleop run. Each demo is replayed by its recorded joint targets and kept only if the oracle passes again. `generate(sources=[...])` cycles through the seeds, and `record_source` takes the policy that drives the demo. The P3 demos turned out to be slides, not lifts: of the 14 seeds, 12 never raise the object more than 0.9 cm, so teleop sources hand over from the object frame to the sink frame once the object has moved 2 cm instead of after a 15 cm lift. Of P3's 20 demos, 14 became seeds; 5 no longer reach the sink on replay (contact differs from the operator's machine) and 1 is a length outlier. 140 attempts gave 104 physics-checked episodes (36 dropped by the oracle, 0 unreachable, 0 human contacts). `validate --episodes --frames` passes them with 0 flagged, and a 200-attempt rerun matched the first 111 outcomes. Throughput was 15.2 s per episode on one core, against 61.5 operator seconds per teleop demo and 3.6 s for the scripted expert. The report now carries `room_id` and `families`, so `arrival cost` makes that comparison (ratio 4.04); dollars stay unmeasured. Upstream MimicGen is still not integrated. Evidence: `docs/results/g7-teleop-seeds-2026-10-27/`. `tests/test_teleop_seeds.py` covers the replay, the skip, a log from another run and the float32 gripper, and `tests/test_cli.py` covers the `--teleop` usage errors.
**TWN-3: per-wall polygon bounds.** `arrival/scan/roomplan.py` now reads finite wall-local `polygonCorners` as 2D/3D arrays or x/y/z dictionaries, using local X for span and Y for height. Two or more points can supply missing/non-positive dimensions or refine wider bounds, with asymmetric centres applied through the wall transform before floor-height inference. Matching centred polygons preserve dimensions exactly, smaller polygons do not shrink valid dimensions, and malformed/non-finite points raise a named `ScanBundleError` without mutating input. The existing chord and portal algorithms are unchanged. All four existing RoomPlan inputs retain byte-identical normalized bundles, twin geometry and layouts. The 42 new cases in `tests/test_roomplan_coverage.py` passed in 0.07 s; coverage, RoomPlan and scan regressions passed 106 cases, and `PYTEST_ADDOPTS='-n 4' make PY=.venv/bin/python test` passed with 2029 passed and 13 skipped (359.41 s). Lint, docs, the offline demo and the packaging smoke target (using its documented `--offline` mode) passed.

**To-do after the Oct 28 merges.** #418 and #439 merged together with #436, which left two lines behind: G6 still said "review" with no PR (it is done, #418), and the DAT-4 bullet still called issue #62's 100-episode benchmark open (it ran in #439: 104 physics-checked episodes from 140 attempts). The to-do header now says Oct 28, 379 merged and 1 open PR, and counts 66 shipped, 0 in review.
**TAV-1: validate Tavily replies and cached findings.** `arrival/models/tavily.py` now rejects non-JSON/non-object HTTP-200 replies and missing/non-list results with a clean `ValueError`, skips unusable result rows and normalizes missing/null/non-string title, URL, content and answer fields before research consumers see them. `arrival/tasks/tavily_research.py` also validates in-process clients and treats malformed stored entries as cache misses, retaining only canonical API findings; corrupt/truncated/non-object cache files recover through the existing miss path. Entirely unusable malformed replies fall back without being persisted, while sanitized usable findings can be cached and non-finite JSON cannot be written. Valid request/response data and the `built_in_table` guidance labels are unchanged. The Tavily modules' 41 offline tests passed using in-process HTTP-200 byte streams, including preservation of existing good cache data and recovery/reuse after corrupt files. `PYTEST_ADDOPTS='-n 4' make PY=.venv/bin/python test` passed with 2016 passed and 13 skipped (369.24 s); lint, docs, the offline demo and the packaging smoke target (using its documented `--offline` mode) passed. No real Tavily request was made.
**The web page lists a room that has only a phone capture's roomplan.json.** #417 let `sim`, `eval` and the fan-out build a room from `scans/<room>/roomplan.json` alone, which is how a CAP-3 phone capture is committed, but the page still counted a folder as a room only when it held `scan.json`. Repro: copy `tests/fixtures/roomplan/kitchen_real_01.json` to `scans/phone_kitchen/roomplan.json`, run `python -m arrival sim --room phone_kitchen --episodes 3` (3/3 episodes, `out/datasets/phone_kitchen_sim` written), then `serve`: the index said "No rooms yet", `/rooms/phone_kitchen` answered 404 and `export-site` left the room out. `arrival/web/app.py` now has `room_scan`, which reads `scan.json` or else normalizes `roomplan.json` with `roomplan.to_bundle` (12 ms for the kitchen); the index, the room page (geometry_source `roomplan`, the export's 6 fixtures, "Source: roomplan.json (twin not compiled)") and `static_export.room_data` use it, and an export that fails to normalize still gets its card instead of a 500. The upload path was already right: the five uploads checked end to end (kitchen_real_01, office, study, and hero_kitchen's `scan.json` and `roomplan.json` through `POST /jobs` and `arrival work --episodes 3`) each ended `done` 3/3 with a zip that passes `validate --episodes`. `tests/test_web_roomplan_only_room.py` covers the listing, the room page, a broken export and the static export row.
**Sim preview: the overview video keeps its 480x360 size.** The README's first sim command (`python -m arrival sim --episodes 8 --people 2 --save-preview`, in a fresh Windows venv) opened with `IMAGEIO FFMPEG_WRITER WARNING: input image is not divisible by macro_block_size=16, resizing from (480, 360) to (480, 368)`: the overview camera renders 480x360, and `write_video` always passed imageio a macro block of 16, so `out/previews/hero_kitchen_sim_overview.mp4` came out 480x368, stretched 2% vertically. `write_video` now takes `macro_block_size` (default 16, so every dataset video and `check_video_size` are unchanged) and `_write_preview` passes 2, the least yuv420p needs, for this human-only mp4. The command now prints no warning and the mp4 decodes at 360x480. `tests/test_multiview.py::test_overview_preview_keeps_its_480x360_size` fails on main (368 rows) and passes here; the six test files that use `write_video` or previews pass (240 passed, 10 skipped).

**README quick start: the preview step works on Windows.** The README says the quick start runs as is on Windows, but its third command, `open out/previews/`, is macOS only: in PowerShell (and cmd) it fails with `open : The term 'open' is not recognized as the name of a cmdlet`, so a Windows judge following the steps literally hits an error right after a successful sim and validate. The comment on that line now gives `start out\previews` for Windows and `xdg-open` for Linux. `tests/test_docs_claims.py::test_readme_quick_start_opens_previews_on_windows_too` fails on main and passes here; `scripts/check_docs.py`, `scripts/check_links.py` and the rest of `tests/test_docs_claims.py` pass.
**teleop, retarget and check_layout read a phone capture too.** #417 taught `sim`, `eval` and `fanout` to build a room from `scans/<room>/roomplan.json` alone, which is how a phone capture is committed (CAP-3), but `arrival.sim.teleop`, `python -m arrival.generate.retarget`, `python -m arrival.generate.se3_retarget` and `scripts/check_layout.py --room` still read `scan.json` only and stopped with "no scan for room" (exit 2) on a room `sim` had just run on. They now share `arrival.twin.compile.room_twin`, which compiles `scan.json` or else builds the export as `sim` and `demo --from-roomplan` do (`roomplan_twin`, moved next to it; `arrival.cli.roomplan_twin` keeps its name). On `kitchen_real_01` as a roomplan-only room, `check_layout --room` passes with 6 fixtures, and teleop and both generators get the same twin `sim` uses. `tests/test_roomplan_room_entry_points.py` covers all four and that a `scan.json` still wins.
**doctor tests off-screen rendering context before sim and eval runs.** MuJoCo simulation, demo recording, realism scoring, and policy evaluation require an active off-screen OpenGL rendering context (EGL, OSMesa, or WGL). When running in headless environments, CI containers, or remote VMs without a display, `import mujoco` succeeds but `mujoco.Renderer` fails when initializing the OpenGL context, leading to fatal crashes mid-run. `arrival doctor` now probes off-screen context creation via `check_rendering()` using a minimal in-memory scene, reports the active `MUJOCO_GL` backend, and provides actionable, platform-aware configuration hints (`set MUJOCO_GL=egl or osmesa` on Linux, `glfw or wgl` on Windows, `glfw or cgl` on macOS) without repeating already-failing backends if context initialization fails. Both `check_mujoco` and `check_rendering` preserve the full underlying exception message so invalid `MUJOCO_GL` values or native library issues are surfaced immediately. Missing MuJoCo reports info (skipped), broken imports or failed offscreen contexts report a warning keeping exit 0. Covered by unit tests in `tests/test_doctor.py` for functional rendering, missing MuJoCo, broken native libraries, platform-specific hints, and backend failure diagnostics.
**merge-shards refuses shards simulated from different scans.** `merge_shards` compared each shard's settings (seed, families, res, base and so on) and its features with shard 0's, but not the scan its twin was compiled from, so a shard rerun after `scan.json` was edited joined the others, and the merged `info.json` kept shard 0's `arrival_provenance`, which names only the first scan. On main, `sim --shard 0/2` of hero_kitchen (`--families counter_to_sink,open_drawer --episodes 2 --people 0 --res 32`), then `mug_1` moved 25 cm in the scan, then `sim --shard 1/2` and `merge-shards`: `OK: merged 2 shards`, exit 0, with shard 1's provenance input `bundle_sha256` 98ca55586a4e and the merged dataset's 7e4e6b021a8a. Each shard's `arrival_provenance.input.bundle_sha256` is a hash of the scan's content, so the merge now refuses shards whose hashes differ ("shard 1 was simulated from another scan than shard 0", exit 2) and writes nothing. The file hash and the code sha are not compared: the same scan saved again, or compiled on another checkout, still merges, and shards with no provenance merge as before. Test: `tests/test_merge_shards_one_scan.py`, fails on main.
**`arrival cost` counts the time of every attempt of a retried job.** The worker writes one ledger line per attempt (`job_attempts` tells them apart), but `arrival.eval.cost.job_usage` kept one entry per job id, so the last line and then `status.json` replaced the earlier attempts. A job whose first attempt failed after 1500 s and whose second finished in 1600 s showed 1600.0 s in its job row (72 demos/hour, 50 s per demo) and `wall_seconds 1600.0` in the worker metering line, also after `arrival gc` had removed the job folder. `wall_seconds` is now summed over a job's attempts, a finished `status.json` still replaces the ledger line of its own attempt, and episodes, frames and bytes come from the last attempt, so the same job shows 3100.0 s (37.2 demos/hour, 96.875 s per demo). An invalid `wall_seconds` in an earlier attempt is refused like one in the last. `tests/test_cost_retried_job.py` covers both; four of its five cases fail on the old reader.
**`fanout` collects its shards when `--out` is a symbolic link.** `LocalBackend` kept its store path as given (`Path.absolute()`) but resolves each object's path, so `list_objects` asked for a resolved path relative to the unresolved store and raised `ValueError`. `run_fanout` lists objects only in the collect step, after every shard has run. On main, `fanout --backend local --shards 2 --episodes 2 --families open_drawer --people 0 --res 32 --out out` with `out` a link to another folder ran both shards ("shard 1/2 done", "shard 0/2 done") and then stopped with a traceback ("... is not in the subpath of .../out/fanout_store"), exit 1; the rerun skipped both shards and stopped the same way. An `out` that links to a data disk does this, and so does `--out /tmp/...` on macOS, where `/tmp` is a link to `/private/tmp`. `list_objects` now takes the keys relative to the resolved store, the folder `_path` already checks them against: the same rerun merges both shards (exit 0), and a fresh run writes a dataset that `validate` passes. Test: `tests/test_fanout_linked_out.py`, fails on main.
**Upload: a NUL in a frames manifest path is a `bad_path`, not an HTTP 500.** `rgbd.load_manifest` checked that each frame's `rgb`, `depth` and `confidence` path is a relative path without `..`, then called `resolve()` on it, which raises a plain `ValueError` ("embedded null character in path") when the path holds a NUL character. That is not a `ManifestError`, so `web/frames.validate` did not turn it into a `FramesError`: on main, `POST /jobs` with the hero_kitchen scan and a synthetic frames zip whose manifest names `"rgb/f000\u0000.png"` answered HTTP 500 `{"error": "Internal server error"}` and logged "unhandled ValueError in POST /jobs", where REFERENCE.md promises 422 with the loader's code, or 400 for a bad archive. A NUL in a manifest path is now refused like an absolute or `..` path: `bad_path`, HTTP 400, nothing queued (nothing was queued before either). Tests: `test_nul_in_a_frame_path_is_a_bad_path` in `tests/test_rgbd.py` and a `_nul_in_path` case in `tests/test_web.py`, which fail on main.
**A carried cup no longer squeezes out of the finger pads.** On the real kitchen scan (`kitchen_real_01`), `eval --families counter_to_counter,load_dishwasher --episodes 4 --people 2 --seed 5 --base fixed` was 3/4 on load_dishwasher, and the same task failed with `--base mobile`: the cup ended on the floor. Nothing but the two fingers touched it before it fell. The procedural pads were 20 mm wide along the gripper's x axis, and the held cup rolled along them during the carry until its contact was 9 mm off centre, at the pad edge; there the fingers snapped shut from 26.6 to 16 mm and the cup left at 1.1 m/s. The squeeze was a steady 10.5 N per pad, so the grip was not weak. The pad collision box in `arrival/sim/robot.py` is now 30 mm wide and the finger visual widens by the same 10 mm. 30 mm and 40 mm both fixed it; the smaller one was kept. `HANDLE_ROOT`, the pan handle grasp point, was the pad half-width plus 7 mm, so it moves to 22 mm. Same command: 4/4 and 4/4. The clean expert's drawer pull now ends at 0.3063 m instead of 0.2997, so the `subset --min-drawer-opening` help gives the new number (0.28 still keeps every clean demo); the DART numbers in `docs/07-eval.md` were measured on the old hand and are left as they were. The hero MJCF hash in `tests/test_fridge_hinge.py` moves to 605c03358c8ffaa9; only the four finger geoms differ. `tests/test_real_kitchen.py::test_cup_stays_in_the_pads_on_the_way_to_the_dishwasher` runs the failing task and fails on main. Full suite: 2096 passed, 55 skipped, 1 failed (the drawer help number, then fixed; its files pass). Limits: the real Franka fingertip pad is about 17 mm wide, so the procedural hand is now further from the real one, and checkpoints trained on datasets from the old hand would be scored on a slightly different gripper. The menagerie arm is unchanged. The plate slipping out of its rim pinch on the same scan (mobile counter_to_counter) is a separate miss and is not fixed here.
**scan_accuracy: a measurement integer too large for float is an error line, not a traceback.** `load_measurements` rejects a value that is not a positive finite number with `MeasurementError`. `math.isfinite` on a JSON integer past the float range (`10**400` in `counter_length_m`) raises `OverflowError` instead. `scripts/scan_accuracy.py` catches `MeasurementError` so one bad room does not stop the others; this integer was not caught, so the script died on that room and never printed the next. `validate_scan_dir` catches the same error and reports `measurements.json is invalid`; it crashed the same way. The check now treats the overflow as not a positive finite number, so the message is the existing one (`must be a positive number of metres`). Two rooms, the first with that integer and the second the hero fixture: on main the script raises `OverflowError` at `arrival/twin/accuracy.py`; here it prints `huge_room: ERROR ... positive number of metres`, then the ok room, and `2 room(s) measured, 1 failing` (exit 1). `tests/test_lidar_accuracy.py::test_an_integer_too_large_for_float_is_an_error_line_and_the_next_room_is_measured` and `tests/test_intake.py::test_huge_integer_in_measurements_json_is_a_problem_not_a_crash` fail on main.
**An integer too large for a float in an upload is refused, not retried.** `bundle.numbers` converts each value with `float`, and RoomPlan's curve and object-dimension checks call `math.isfinite`. A JSON integer such as `10**400` raises `OverflowError`, which is not a `ValueError`, so `retryable` is true. With the default of 2 retries the worker runs that upload three times and then fails with `OverflowError: int too large to convert to float` (`job_attempts` 3, `retry_errors` holding that text twice, `error` the same on the last attempt). NaN in the same field is already `ScanBundleError` and is not retried. `numbers` now treats the overflow as not a finite float and returns None, and the RoomPlan checks use the same rule they already had for NaN and inf: a bad coordinate or dimension is `ScanBundleError`, a curve angle or radius that overflows is dropped like a non-finite one, and a non-finite curve centre is ignored. On main the worker's first attempt is requeued (`status` `queued`, `retry_errors` `['OverflowError: int too large to convert to float']`); here the job fails once with `ScanBundleError` and a second `run_once` finds nothing queued. `tests/test_upload_huge_integer.py` fails on main and passes here.
**A shard that exits 0 without meta files is a fetch failure, not a marker failure.** `StatusRecorder.fetch_result` copied a succeeded job's files and then treated the attempt as ready for its done marker. `require_shard_artifacts` runs after that copy and raises when `meta/info.json` or `meta/episodes.jsonl` is missing. `arrival fanout` calls `cancel_open` on the way out, which saw a succeeded job whose marker had been noted and recorded `failed_stage` `marker` with the reason "the fan-out stopped before this result was stored". The missing path was dropped. The artifact check now runs inside `fetch_result`, before the marker is noted, so the attempt is `failed` / `fetch` and the reason is the `ShardError` (an empty shard folder: `shard 0/1 is missing r1/shards/0of1/datasets/hero_kitchen_sim/meta/info.json; done marker is not trustworthy`). A non-zero exit is still `run`, and a missing shard folder was already `fetch`. The fan-out still stops on that error rather than retrying the shard, as it does for any other `ShardError` from the copy. `tests/test_fanout_missing_meta.py` (an empty shard folder, and `info.json` without `episodes.jsonl`) fails on main with `('failed', 'marker')` and passes here.

**Loose scans in one folder no longer share its measurements.json.** Since #463, `arrival summary` read `measurements.json` from the folder of any explicit scan file. Several exports saved side by side (`hero.json` and `other.json` in one folder, with the hero kitchen's tape values beside them) then all took that one file. `other.json`, the rotated kitchen, got a failing tape row with the counter 1.43 m short, from numbers that were never taken in that room. The folder's file is now read only when the scan is `scan.json` or `roomplan.json`, the same rule `resolve_room` uses to name the room after its folder. Both cases #463 added still pass. `tests/test_summary.py::test_loose_scans_in_one_folder_do_not_share_its_measurements` fails on main (tape FAIL) and passes here (tape `None`).
**A site re-export no longer deletes the film's reader lines.** `scripts/export_demo_data.py` clears `site/data` and kept only `todo.json`. `site/data/readers-lines.json` is hand-written too: it is the 37-line film script that `readers.html` shows and `api/readers.mjs` checks every sign-up against. A re-export deleted it, so the readers page said "The script did not load. Reload the page to try again." and the sign-up API failed with "readers-lines.json: no file". It is now in `KEEP`, and `tests/test_export_demo_data.py` checks it survives a re-run.
**The web page's front page shows the placeholder note only when a room has the placeholder.** `arrival serve` showed "Motion in this dataset is a scripted placeholder ... It is not physically valid" on every visit, including on a page whose only datasets were physics-checked sim and upload runs (five uploads of real scans, all `physically_valid: true`, sat right under it). #432 had already made the room page show its box only for a room with the `scripted_retarget` dataset; the front page now does the same, with the note unchanged. Two tests in `tests/test_web_room_placeholder_box.py` cover both cases.
**Demo site: data re-exported for Oct 28, and the first screen agrees with itself.** `site/data` was last exported around Oct 19, before the P3 teleop baseline existed, so the page said "Cost against teleoperation. Not compared: no teleop baseline". `python scripts/export_demo_data.py` on today's main finds the shipped baseline and now reports 7.41 s of local generation per counter_to_sink success (n=1) against 61.5 s of operator time (n=20), with no dollar saving claimed. The batch is still 6 of 6 with 0 human contacts; throughput went from 206 to 465 demos/h and the engine line reads MuJoCo 3.17.0. Text corrected in place in `site/index.html` (no layout or element changes): the hero said "no real kitchen has been scanned yet" while step 1 and the schedule on the same page say one has; the State and action plate showed 11 / 11 under "seven joint angles plus gripper opening" and the chapter said "eight-number" (the base's x, y and yaw are the other three); the title block date. `docs/DEPLOY.md` and `docs/REFERENCE.md` said no real kitchen was scanned; `kitchen_real_01` is checked in (P1). `readers-lines.json` was restored by hand after the export deleted it (fixed in the exporter separately).
**A plate held by its rim no longer rolls out of the pinch on a mobile carry.** On the real kitchen scan (`kitchen_real_01`) with `--base mobile`, every plate `counter_to_counter` trial missed: seeds 5, 7 and 11 of `eval --families all --episodes 4 --people 2` and a `--holdout` run, 0 of 5. The plate hangs from its rim pinch during the drive and slowly turns about the line between the two pads (about 0.01 rad/s) until the rim slides out. Nothing else touches it. The pads already use `condim="6"`, but their rolling friction was 0.001 m, which at the 10 N squeeze resists about 0.01 N·m, far less than the plate's weight 10 cm off the pinch. A higher squeeze force changed nothing, and a stiffer finger servo fixed the plate but stopped the fridge handle turning in the pads. The pad rolling friction in `arrival/sim/robot.py` is now 0.05. On the four known misses, 0.01 and 0.02 fix none, 0.03 fixes two, 0.05 and 0.08 fix all four; peak plate tilt drops from 125 to 135 degrees to about 33. It also stopped the finger rolling along the faucet lever (rotated_kitchen `turn_on_faucet` 0/4, lever at 0.21 rad), so the lever geom in `arrival/sim/scene.py` takes `priority="1"` with the contact values main used for that pair (2.0 0.05 0.001, condim 6); its four trials pass again. Replays of the three kitchen_real_01 mobile manifests: 44/44 each, where main gives 43/44, 42/44 and 43/44 and every miss is the plate. hero_kitchen mobile seed 13: 44/44 with the same arm contacts as main. The hero MJCF hash in `tests/test_fridge_hinge.py` moves to 8263ff9319dcc811; only the two finger collision geoms and the faucet lever differ. `tests/test_real_kitchen.py::test_plate_stays_in_the_rim_pinch_on_a_mobile_carry` runs seed 5 trial 3 and fails on main. Limits: 0.05 m is more than the pad's 30 mm half-height, so the hand resists tilting more than a flat pad of that size could, and 0.03 only fixed half. The menagerie arm's pads (`arrival/sim/menagerie.py`) get the same 0.05: on 0.001 the real hand dropped the plate back onto the source counter a few steps into the lift on that same seed 5 trial. With its assets fetched, the kitchen_real_01 mobile seed 5 replay with `--arm menagerie` goes from 43/44 to 44/44 (the miss was the plate), and `sim --arm menagerie --families all --episodes 14 --res 64` stays 14/14 at seed 5 with no people and at seeds 0 and 3 with 2 people. The plate test runs on both arms; the menagerie case skips when the assets are missing.
**C3: arrival cost calculates teleop USD multiple and supports top-level report.json.** `arrival/eval/cost.py` previously only computed `seconds_ratio_teleop_over_generated` (4.04x) when comparing against a teleop baseline; even with priced cloud/compute usage, `usd_ratio_teleop_over_generated` was left out of the comparison object and markdown summary table. `compare()` now calculates `usd_ratio_teleop_over_generated` using unrounded per-demo costs when both teleop and generated USD per demo are present, and `markdown()` outputs the multiple (`USD, teleop over generated: <ratio>x (estimate)`). In addition, `collect_rows()` now directly discovers top-level `out/report.json` in single-run directories alongside `out/reports/*.json`, and `build()` automatically defaults to `<out>/usage.json` when present. Covered by unit tests in `tests/test_cost.py` for unrounded USD ratio calculation preventing rounding distortion, markdown rendering, single-report discovery, and automatic out-directory usage resolution.
**Demo video and lighting renderers cleanly release GL context on Windows (issue #72).** PR #71 merged the wrist camera framing angle and grasp heading replanning to keep joint 7 off its stop, but left issue #72 open because `scripts/make_demo_video.py`'s episode loop was deferred while #50 was editing it. In `scripts/make_demo_video.py`, `episode_frames()` instantiated `SimEnv` and two standalone `mujoco.Renderer` instances (`big` and `small`) without closing them upon completion. On Windows, unclosed WGL/OpenGL contexts leak across sequential tasks, leaving no active GL context for subsequent episodes and causing every frame after episode 0 to render black. `episode_frames()` now wraps the rollout loop in `try ... finally: big.close(); small.close(); env.close()`. In `tests/test_lighting.py`, `_mean_brightness()` now frees its off-screen renderer in `try ... finally: r.close()`. Covered by `tests/test_sim.py::test_demo_video_episode_frames_lifecycle_on_windows`, ensuring consecutive tasks render valid, bright frames across all cameras on Windows without GL context loss.
**Dynamic Nemotron instruction and prompt style variation (Issue #341 / POL).** Policy robustification and dataset diversity for foundation VLA models (e.g. NVIDIA Isaac-GR00T) require exposure to varied linguistic formulations rather than uniform imperatives. `arrival/tasks/instructions.py` now supports prompt style conditioning across six styles (`INSTRUCTION_STYLES`: `mixed`, `concise`, `polite`, `descriptive`, `urgent`, `goal_oriented`). When querying Nemotron Lightning via Nebius Token Factory, prompt directives instruct the model to produce instructions matching the desired stylistic register. Cache keys namespace non-default styles (`::style=<style>`) while keeping the legacy format for default `mixed` generations to preserve backward compatibility. In offline mode, `_style_templates()` adapts template fallbacks per style. `arrival/sim/tasks.py` and `arrival/sim/collect.py` accept `instruction_style` (default `mixed`) and propagate it to task metadata, and `arrival sim` accepts `--instruction-style` (enforced to require `--instructions nemotron`). Covered by 11 unit tests in `tests/test_instruction_styles.py` verifying style validation, fallback splits, prompt injection, cache isolation, task propagation, and CLI usage validation.
**A number written as a string in an upload is a bad scan, not a retried TypeError.** `bundle.numbers` called `float()` on each value, and `float()` also reads `"2.4"`, so a string passed the scan.json field checks while the values kept their str type. A wall start of `["0", 0.0]` then raised `TypeError: '>' not supported between instances of 'float' and 'str'` in the wall-span check; that is not a `ValueError`, so the worker queued the upload again twice and failed it on attempt 3 with no field named. A wall height of `"2.4"` passed, the twin kept the str, and the sim's `build_mjcf` failed on it. `numbers` now takes only an int or float, the rule `is_finite_number` already applies, so every such field is a `ScanBundleError` that names it (`wall wall_north: start and end must be [x, y] numbers ...`) and the job fails once. An object or fixture number and a RoomPlan transform written as a string are refused the same way; `compile_twin` and the RoomPlan normalizer used to convert those. `tests/test_scan_number_strings.py` fails on main and passes here.
**Submission checklist brought up to Oct 28.** `docs/SUBMISSION-CHECKLIST.md` still told the team to call G2, G6, C4 and Cosmos (C5) "not done" on the form, said `main` stopped at #301, asked for a Tavily call and a "Not yet" edit that are already done, and listed issues to close without #62 and #69. Each line now says what is true on `main`: done and not-done items separated, C3's measured time, the two feedback brackets still open, and that the live site needs a redeploy.
**Import hygiene in the offline tooling: one import block, no unused names, and the two docstrings that ran past 120 columns.** `arrival/doctor.py` imported `arrival.jobs.nebius` on line 25, below `MIN_PYTHON` and `CORE`, which is the shape ruff reports as `E402` (module-level import not at the top of the file) and the only `E402` in the repository. The import moved into the block above the constants; the module-level names `JOB_SETTINGS` and `STORE_SETTINGS` are still bound at import time, and `arrival.jobs.nebius` imports only `arrival.config`, `arrival.jobs.fanout` and `arrival.jobs.shards`, so no cycle is broken by the move. Two unused imports are gone: `urllib.parse.urlparse` in `scripts/check_site.py`, and `pytest` in `tests/test_doctor.py` (that file uses the `patch`, `tmp_path` and `capsys` fixtures and never references `pytest` itself). `import sys` in the same test file is used, five times as `sys.platform`, but sat between the two `arrival` imports; it moved into the stdlib block. `scripts/check_site.py` carried five imports on one line and no `from __future__ import annotations`, unlike `check_links.py` and `check_docs.py`; it now matches them, and its one `bad.append(...); continue` line is two statements. `ruff check . --select E402,F401,I` goes from 83 findings to 79 (E402 1 → 0, F401 15 → 13, I001 67 → 66); the rest is in files this change does not touch. The CI gate is unchanged — `[tool.ruff.lint] select` is still `E9`, `F63`, `F7`, `F82`, so `make lint` is green before and after. Two docstrings past the `line-length = 120` set in `pyproject.toml` are rewrapped: the module docstring in `arrival/doctor.py` (136 characters) and `load_dotenv` in `arrival/config.py` (128). E501 is not enforced and 443 lines in the repository still exceed 120, so this is the two lines in the files this change already edits, not a sweep; turning on `I`, `F401` and `E501` is a separate change with its own diff. `scripts/check_site.py`'s `main()` keeps raising `SystemExit` rather than returning a code, because `tests/test_check_site.py` asserts the raise. `tests/test_doctor.py`, `tests/test_check_site.py` and `tests/test_ci_hygiene.py`: 43 passed.

**Refactor: one owner per shared helper, no private imports across modules.** Code written by many hands had copies of the same helper and modules reaching into each other's private names. `provenance` moves from `arrival/export/` to `arrival/provenance.py`, since scan, twin, generate, eval, jobs and export all use it and the scan loader no longer has to import the export package for a file hash. Three identical `_jsonl` readers become `arrival.export.lerobot.read_jsonl`; `read_status` moves from the worker to `arrival.jobs.queue`, next to `write_status`; `resolve_scans` moves from the CLI to `arrival.scan.bundle`, so library code no longer imports the CLI; `cli.roomplan_twin`, a pass-through, is gone. Helpers other modules used under a private name are public (`feature_stats`, `rect_distance`, `find_dataset`, `segment_distance`, `stand_problem`). No behaviour changes.

**Refactor: no import cycles in the simulator.** `sim.tasks`, `reach`, `collect`, `preflight` and `policy` imported each other in a loop, and so did `scene` and `people`, held together by imports inside functions. Pure fixture geometry (drawer stacks, cabinet and wall-cabinet doors, the fridge door, sinks in counters) moves out of `scene.py` into `arrival/sim/geometry.py`; `arrival/sim/preflight.py` becomes `arrival/sim/stand.py`, holding the stand rules, and its `check_task` joins `find_shift` in `arrival/sim/reach.py`; the control rate and hand constants move to `robot.py` and the close_door start state to `stand.py`. One `point_segment_distance` replaces three copies. The modules now import strictly downward (scan, twin, geometry, scene, stand, reach, tasks, policy, collect), with no cycle anywhere in `arrival/`.

**Refactor: merge modules that were one feature split across files.** `arrival/eval/realism.py` now holds the whole realism measurement: the metrics, the `run_realism` driver (was `eval/realism_cli.py`) and the worker's optional stage (`measure`, was `jobs/realism.py`). The per-run speed and cost report (`build_report`, `write_report`, was `eval/report.py`) joins the cross-run report in `arrival/eval/cost.py`, and the scripted placeholder's distance oracle (`judge`, was `generate/oracle.py`) joins `arrival/generate/scripted.py`; the unused `oracle.keep` is dropped. Files are merged by what they do, not by size: small modules that are their own concept (the MimicGen probe, the job child process) stay.

**Refactor: split the longest functions where their phases are separate jobs, and docstrings where they help.** `ScriptedExpert._waypoints` (327 lines) dispatches to one plan method per task family; `check_loader`, `validate`, `collect`, `make_tasks`, `Walkers.step` and the web room page have their self-contained phases pulled out. Single linear pipelines and guarded request handlers (`build_parser`, `_post_job`, `worker.process`, `merge_shards`) stay whole rather than becoming many small functions. Every public class, and every public function whose name does not already say what it returns, raises or decides, now has a docstring written from its code; one-line helpers and protocol method implementations do not.

**Lint and format: one style for the whole repository.** The ruff gate adds sorted import blocks, no unused imports or variables, imports at the top of the module and current Python syntax (I, F401, F841, E402, UP), and `ruff format` lays out every file; CI's lint job and `make lint` check both, and `make format` applies them. The formatting commit is listed in `.git-blame-ignore-revs` (`git config blame.ignoreRevsFile .git-blame-ignore-revs`), so `git blame` skips it.

**Refactor: one catalog module.** `arrival/catalog/fixtures.py` (scan category → RoboCasa fixture type, default sizes) and `arrival/catalog/objects.py` (object label → catalog id, physical specs, which objects each family can hold, room kinds) are both lookups from a scanned category to what the twin and the sim build, and become `arrival/catalog/categories.py`. Every name is unchanged; only the import path moves, so a branch that imports `arrival.catalog.fixtures` or `arrival.catalog.objects` needs `arrival.catalog.categories` instead.

**Refactor: split the long functions that did several jobs.** The twelve longest functions were each checked. Eight had separate phases and are split, with no change in behaviour, messages or order: `check_loader` (modality slices, per-episode table checks, base motion and the stats comparison move to `_check_modality`, `_check_table`, `_check_base_motion` and `_compare_stats`), `collect` (`_rollout` builds and runs one task, `_restore` counts a resumed journal), teleop `run` (`_attempt`, `_step`, which was written out twice, and `_write_baseline`), SE(3) `generate` (`_retarget_attempt`, `_report`), `evaluate` (`_run_trial`), `merge_shards` (`_check_shard_set`, `_episode_files`, `_merged_skips`), `load_manifest` (`_frame`, `_check_split_lists`) and `vary_dataset` (`_vary_frame`). Four stay whole because they are one job: `build_parser` is flat argument declarations, `worker.process` is one guarded pipeline whose failure handling needs its single `try`, `_post_job` keeps every cheap refusal before the body is read in one place, and `summary.run_room` reads top to bottom like the table it fills.
**A constant column stays constant in the loader check.** `check_loader` recomputed each std as the square root of E[x^2] minus the squared mean. On a column of identical values that difference is rounding noise, not motion. A fixed-base dataset of 10 episodes and 150 frames, base pose held at x = 2.1, y = 1.35, yaw = 1.5707964, stores std 0 for those three dims in `stats.json` (the writer's numpy std). The check recomputed state and action dim 8 as 2.9802322387695312e-08. `CONST_STD` is 1e-8, so both columns got `stats_std_zero` ("std is 0 on dims [8] that move in the parquet") and the `fixed_base_constant` warning named only dims [9, 10]. The std is now the two-pass std of the same rows already kept for the quantiles, which is 0 on that column. The warning names [8, 9, 10] and there is no `stats_std_zero`. `tests/test_loader_check_constant_std.py::test_constant_base_column_is_not_reported_as_moving` fails on main and passes here.
